##### NR09.3.2a: Retain local revisions observed in peer publications

What to do: Persist evidence that a peer batch contains a local pending revision and exclude it from save coalescing. Upgrade existing stores conservatively without changing revision provenance, outbox selection, or wire format.
Constraints: Commit exposure metadata with the incoming graph/cursor. Preserve schema 1/2 definitions and exact publication acknowledgment. Refuse rollback when the older schema cannot retain the protection.
Verify: Reproduce the unselected duplicate-revision deletion, then verify retention across reconciliation/reopen and later peer descendants, import rollback/retry, populated upgrades, failed upgrades, and guarded down/up migration. Run all storage and process-crash gates.
Proof notes: The focused `a_peer_observed_revision_cannot_be_coalesced_out_of_the_local_graph` test failed on the previous implementation. Revision IDs exclude writer identity, so a duplicate peer record retained local provenance and remained eligible for coalescing after its peer cursor advanced. Schema 3 adds a foreign-key-protected observation set. Import records pending duplicate IDs in the same transaction as graph/cursor/history, and save excludes them from coalescing. Local provenance, pending outbox, frozen selections, and the wire format remain intact. Upgrades conservatively protect existing outbox IDs; schemas 1/2 have pinned unchanged checksums. Offline rollback refuses observed unselected saves, while frozen selections can survive down/up without losing their existing protection. No new dependency or vendor fork is required.

The regression now verifies unchanged provenance/outbox, close/reopen and restored publication certainty, retained ancestors, and successful later peer descendants with both concurrent heads. A cursor-failure test proves observation metadata rolls back and retry protects the parent. Schema 2 upgrade tests preserve pending saves, refuse unsafe rollback, allow a frozen down/up round trip, and roll back a failed backfill/history commit before successful reopen. Existing populated schema 1, full-disk migration, damaged-body, and rollback refusal tests remain green. `cargo test -p quire_store --locked` passes 109 tests; `bun run test:reference` passes 55; `CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo bun run test:storage-crash` passes the 12 process-kill checks. Storage all-target Clippy with `-D warnings`, native build, formatting, prose lint, and scoped whitespace pass. The existing debug unwind warning remains.

Sequential review checked observation timing, rejected/duplicate batches, rollback atomicity, safe publication selection, conservative migration, unchanged schemas, and deletion restrictions. The simplification pass retains existing save/import/publication paths and adds only the persistent protection needed by the reproduced failure. Migration prevents future coalescing loss; it cannot recover revisions already deleted by an older build. The 10,000 schedule gate and parent feature work remain open.

Reopened for `rt` 0.9.57. `71b62cba83e3` changed the reference for this case: `replica.ts` import now removes every revision in any imported batch from the local outbox, not only this device's, because two computers can publish the identical merge and others may build on it. Such a revision is no longer this computer's to publish or replace. The native import (`crates/quire_store/src/notebook/import.rs`, the `observed_revisions` insert in `import_batch`) keeps it in the outbox and later republishes it. Peers treat the copy as a duplicate, so no content is lost, but the log grows and outbox counts differ from the reference that NR09.3.3 compares. Remove unselected outbox rows for imported revision IDs in the import transaction, keep frozen selections with their exact bytes, retain the observation protection for frozen rows, and add a regression with an identical peer merge.

Resolved on 2026-10-30: `import_batch` now deletes unselected outbox rows for every revision ID in an applied batch from any device, inside the transaction that commits the graph, cursor, and hash history. Rows frozen in a pending publication stay with their exact bytes and gain the existing observation row; they leave the outbox only when `complete_publication` acknowledges them. Regressions: `a_revision_a_peer_published_leaves_the_outbox_but_stays_in_the_graph`, `an_identical_merge_from_a_peer_removes_the_local_pending_copy` (a local merge, then the same merge ID from a peer with another device and clock; the local record keeps its provenance and a later save cannot replace it), and `a_frozen_selection_keeps_a_revision_a_peer_also_published` in `crates/quire_store/tests/import.rs`; all three failed with the deletion disabled. The unit test `failed_peer_import_keeps_the_outbox_and_retry_hands_the_revision_to_the_peer` checks that an injected cursor failure rolls the deletion back. Schema 3 tables and migrations are unchanged; the rollback guard still refuses unselected observed rows, which an upgrade can still create. Gates: scoped Clippy with `-D warnings`, `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store --locked --features quire/test-support`, `bun run test:reference`, and `bun run test:sync-schedules --count 15` (30 schedules plus seven focused cases, still on the frozen 0.9.49 scheduler; NR09.3.3 refreshes it).
Status: done

##### NR09.3.3: Verify at least 10,000 reproducible replica schedules across runtimes

What to do: Connect a persistent native storage/replica probe to the frozen TypeScript replica model and seeded delivery scheduler. Exercise real native save/select/publish/import/rescan operations and independent delivered-folder views without starting one process per action. Check saved contents and final heads for native/native and mixed replicas.
Constraints: Use the production native store and folder loop, retain reproducible seeds/traces, and keep frozen sources unchanged. Include delayed, duplicate, partial, and reordered delivery, missing parents/attachments, concurrent edits, stale windows, merges/tombstones, uncertain publication/reopen, and writer collisions. Assert frozen-selection safety during edits in flight even where the reference needs a separately recorded stronger native guarantee. Simulated/provider schedules do not establish real cloud or power-loss behavior.
Verify: At least 10,000 deterministic schedules finish with the expected saved content and graph heads; failing seeds replay independently. Retain the real-filesystem crash gates and add any schedule-discovered regression to focused tests. Record runtime and storage work counts; keep long-running checks observable while working on independent features.
Proof notes: Added the persistent `quire_store` example `replica_probe`, a synchronous FIFO transport, a native engine adapter, and the Node schedule runner behind `bun run test:sync-schedules`. The scheduler comes from rt `4985e4080d2302b2abb416f2db7fa390080786c8`; its source checksum and interface/import adaptations are recorded separately in `tests/reference/UPSTREAM.json`. Scheduling/delivery behavior is unchanged. The frozen format/graph/replica modules remain unchanged. Narrow read interfaces avoid fake storage mutations. Records cross the transport as canonical wire JSON strings. Every native peer owns a real WAL/FULL database and separate incoming/outgoing folder mirrors, so publishing does not implicitly import peers. Selection reconciles only own files, freezes production bytes, delivers through the simulated network/crash hook, then asks the real publisher to verify/adopt and acknowledge the exact file. Rebuild refuses pending local work; reopen retains it. Native diagnostics and changed pages reach the adapter, including pages discovered during own reconciliation.

`CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo bun run test:sync-schedules --count 100` passes 200 schedules at 60 actions each and seven focused cases. It took 139.52 seconds on this runtime, with 35,410 native requests including the focused cases. Scheduled work includes 1,559 save calls, 2,791 import calls, 2,174 selections, 509 completed publications, 80 uncertain-publication paths, and 118 rebuilds. Focused cases verify delayed attachments for native/native and both mixed directions, frozen selection plus a later save and reopen, tombstone/stale-window conflicts in both mixed directions, and a copied writer retaining its unpublished save. Saved semantic fields, head equality, ancestry, empty outboxes, and careful handovers are checked. Seed 37 passes independent mixed replay; seeds 9000/9001 pass alternating native/mixed replay and exercise a three-computer native schedule. An earlier 25-seed run passed 50 schedules and the same focused cases.

The example build and Clippy with `-D warnings`, strict TypeScript 5.9.3 checking, formatting, prose lint, and scoped whitespace pass. Type checking used temporary tooling outside the repository (`@types/node` 24.3.0 and `@types/bun` 1.3.0); no production dependency or vendor fork was added. Sequential review checked transport framing/UTF-8, owned fixture cleanup, real storage ownership, incoming versus own-folder isolation, crash timing, exact selection acknowledgment, canonical records, native diagnostics, seeded replay, and the unchanged scheduler. The simplification pass reuses production rescan/selection/publication APIs rather than adding a second native replica implementation.

The full acceptance command, CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo bun run test:sync-schedules --count 10000 --runtime alternating, finished with exit 0 on 2026-10-30. All seeds 1 through 10,000 pass at 60 actions per schedule: 5,000 native-only and 5,000 mixed-runtime schedules, plus seven focused cases. The real process handle was polled to terminal completion. It used Node v26.11.1, took 7,414.943 seconds, and handled 1,878,501 native requests. Scheduled native work includes 79,089 saves, 144,084 imports, 115,941 selections, 26,679 completed publications, 3,605 uncertain-publication paths, and 6,988 rebuilds. The harness/native executable was built from e08c7e68ea06 with the observed-revision retention repair; later metadata/JSON extraction did not change the exercised storage/codec algorithms. No failing seed or new regression was discovered in this run. The runner prints progress and retains failing databases/folder views with a request/reply trace and replay command. Its default runs both runtimes for all 10,000 seeds; alternating mode runs 10,000 total schedules, 5,000 per runtime. Native placeholders become invalid empty files until delivery, so diagnostic classification can differ from unavailable TypeScript reads. This harness covers synthetic UTF-8 edits and simulated delivery on Unix, not native editing of lone-surrogate wire text, real cloud/power-loss behavior, Windows, or release performance. Existing storage/process-kill checks remain separate evidence.

Reopened for `rt` 0.9.57: the scheduler pinned from `4985e4080d23` is superseded. `71b62cba83e3` changed `tests/sync/harness.ts` and `replica.ts`. A stale save that the replica reports as merged now continues from the merge, settling runs up to 12 rounds until every outbox is empty, and every schedule fails if any computer ends with competing versions; the earlier careful-mode-only conflict check is gone. `c3a036587871` adds two focused cases: a save during publication keeps the version being published, and a forgotten own revision is read back from this device's change files. Native replicas do not merge heads yet, so mixed schedules against the current reference cannot pass until NR09.4 lands. Refresh the frozen graph, replica, and harness copies and hashes, add the two focused cases, and rerun the 10,000-schedule gate. The 2026-10-30 run above is valid evidence for the 0.9.49 contract only.

Rerun for 0.9.57 on 2026-10-30: the scheduler, graph, and replica copies now come from `5ea5c22310ff` (see NR01), and native replicas merge as NR09.4 describes. The probe passes the harness clock on open, rebuild, and import, and returns save merges and overlapped pages. Focused cases grew from seven to twelve: the tombstone case now expects a stale save to merge with the edit outliving the deletion; independent TypeScript and native merges reach one revision ID in both mixed directions; a save made while a batch is being published keeps that version, for native/TypeScript and native/native pairs (`c3a036587871`); and a TypeScript computer reads back a revision it published but forgot, while a native computer builds on it. Native stores never drop a selected revision, so only the TypeScript side forgets. `CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo bun run test:sync-schedules --count 10000 --runtime alternating` exited 0: seeds 1 to 10,000 at 60 actions, 5,000 native-only and 5,000 mixed, plus the twelve focused cases, in 8,599 s on Node v26.11.1 with 2,056,611 native requests (78,990 saves, 144,090 imports, 115,939 selections, 36,821 completed publications, 3,627 uncertain publications, 6,988 rebuilds). An earlier run stopped at seed 582 to fix the rebuild hazard described in NR09.4; its partial result is not counted. The same limits apply as before: synthetic UTF-8 edits, simulated delivery on Unix, no cloud, power-loss, Windows, or release-performance evidence.
Status: done

#### NR09.4: Merge competing page heads deterministically

What to do: Port `src/core/merge.ts` and `mergeBase`/`mergeHeads` from `src/desktop/sync/graph.ts`. The merge is three-way against the newest common ancestor: lines changed on one side apply, lines both sides changed merge word by word, and only overlapping words keep both versions, the older head first, with a repeated block ID dropped from the second copy. Merge every page with several heads after each import, after a save that leaves several heads, and once at open for heads kept by older builds. Put the merge revision in the outbox, report overlapped pages for a warning naming the page, and show the conflict state only when a merge itself fails.
Constraints: Results must be byte-identical to the TypeScript and Go implementations: Myers diff with common prefix and suffix trimmed, the fixed tie rule, the 2,000-edit cap, `[ \t]+|[^ \t]+` word tokens, heads ordered by `created` then ID, an edit outliving a deletion, and the newer path winning between two moves. Merge revisions carry this device and the current clock, but their IDs equal any other computer's merge of the same heads, so same-ID peer duplicates become routine. Never coalesce a merge or root revision (NR08). Operate on lossless wire strings, including lone surrogates, and merge inside the transaction that created the heads.
Write set: `quire_core` merge module, merge hooks in `crates/quire_store/src/notebook/{revision,import}.rs`, merge fixtures and oracle.
Dependencies: NR09.2, NR09.3.2.
Verify: Every case in `rt/tests/fixtures/merges.json`; at least 4,000 seeded random cases against the frozen TypeScript merge, as `rt/tests/merge-go.test.ts` does for Go; identical merge IDs from two native replicas and from a mixed pair; overlap warnings; deletion revival; competing moves; open-time merging of legacy heads; rollback when a merge cannot be stored; and the reopened NR09.3.3 schedules.
Proof notes: `quire_core::merge::merge_text` ports `src/core/merge.ts` from `rt` `5ea5c22310ff` over UTF-16 code units, so lone surrogates merge as JavaScript does. `crates/quire_store/src/notebook/merge.rs` ports `mergeBase` (walking the indexed `parents` and `generation` rows instead of revision bodies) and `mergeHeads`. A save that leaves several heads merges in its own transaction and returns `SaveAck::merged` and `overlaps`. A rescan merges every conflicted page in one transaction, each page under a savepoint, and reports `RescanReport::merged`; a page whose merge fails keeps its heads and becomes a rescan problem. Merge revisions enter the outbox, carry this device, and use the save's `created` or the store clock (`NotebookStore::open_with_clock`).

Two deliberate deviations from `rt`. First, rescans merge after the whole graph pass, and only once this device's own log is reconciled, instead of inside each `import_batch` transaction as this task first asked; `rt` also merges after its import loop. A fresh review found that any merge waiting in the outbox makes the native importer refuse this device's own later batches ("unrecorded change files and local pending saves"), so merging while a rebuilt store still waits for its own batch 3 would strand that device. `a_rebuilding_device_merges_only_after_importing_all_of_its_own_batches` (`crates/quire_store/tests/rescan.rs`) failed with an ungated merge and passes now. Second, and for the same reason, opening a store does not merge; the first reconciled rescan merges heads kept by older builds or left by a crash between an import and its merge. The worker and app open a store before they can see the folder, so this costs one rescan of delay.

Evidence. `crates/quire_core/tests/merge.rs` checks all 20 cases in `tests/fixtures/merges.json` and 4,007 seeded cases in `tests/fixtures/merge-vectors.json`: the 4,000 inputs `rt/tests/merge-go.test.ts` sends to Go (seed 42; 1,025 overlap), lone-surrogate, CRLF, and astral edges, and a 2,500-edit case past the cap. `bun tools/merge-vectors.ts` regenerates the file byte for byte from the frozen sources. `head_merges_match_the_frozen_typescript_graph` imports 300 seeded head graphs (27 all-deleted, 124 with three or more heads) on per-device chains and compares the ID, page, parents, generation, path, deletion, text, and overlap count with the frozen `mergeHeads`. Mutating the hunk tie rule, the edit cap, the merge-base ID order, or the path rule fails these tests. Further tests cover identical merge IDs from two native replicas with their own provenance and the second outbox copy dropped (`two_folder_replicas_deliver_attachments_and_merge_to_one_identical_revision`), deletion revival and two deletions, the newer move and overlap reporting, rollback of a save whose merge cannot be stored, and a rescan whose page merge fails and succeeds on retry. Focused mixed cases check that independent TypeScript and native merges reach one revision ID in both directions and that a stale save merges with a tombstone. Earlier tests that made conflicts by saving now import the competing head, because saves merge. The app test `stored_recovery_draws_in_two_read_only_views_with_conflicts_and_tombstones` does the same.

Remaining: no UI shows the overlap warning that names the page; `SaveAck::overlaps` and `RescanReport::merged` are ready for NR11.4. Publication still requires every referenced attachment locally, so a merge that cites an undelivered attachment waits to publish. Each rescan finds conflicted pages with one grouped scan of `heads`; NR35 should measure it at 5x. Like `rt`, two computers can store the same merge ID with different `created` values, which can order a later merge differently and cost an extra merge round.

Gates on 2026-10-30: scoped Clippy with `-D warnings`; `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store --locked --features quire/test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` passes 310 tests (`runtime_shaders` only because this host cannot compile Metal shaders ahead of time); `bun run test:reference` passes 79; `bun run test:storage-crash` passes its process-kill cases, whose rescan step now checks the merged head; and the 10,000-schedule run recorded under NR09.3.3. `tools/test-storage-crash.ts` needed a larger `cargo metadata` buffer to run from this worktree.
Status: done

#### NR09.5: Port live publication timing, status heartbeats, and retrying imports

What to do: Match the 0.9.53 sync scheduler. Publish 150 ms after the last save, at most 1 s, while another Quire app on this computer is nearby; otherwise after 1 s, at most 5 s. Rewrite `devices/<device-id>/status.json` only when the outbox count changes, once a minute as a heartbeat, and at notebook open. A status from another device with the same `label` and a heartbeat under two minutes old counts as nearby. Rerun an import requested while one runs. React to watcher events within 50 ms and ignore dot-prefixed temporary files. Retry an unreadable change file after 1 s, and warn only when the same problem survives the retry.
Constraints: Keep `status.json` fields and `label` semantics compatible with the desktop and the Go terminal app, which sets `label` to the host name and `app` to `quire-tui <version>`. One computer may hold several writers on one notebook, each with its own device ID. Timing changes cannot weaken durability ordering or uncertain-publication handling.
Write set: notebook sync scheduler, status writer and reader, folder watcher adapter, scheduler tests.
Dependencies: NR09.3, NR11.
Verify: Injected-clock tests for both publish windows, heartbeat suppression and refresh, nearby detection with stale and other-label statuses, an import rerun after a late file, a partial-file retry without a first-attempt warning, and dot-file filtering. Measure native-to-desktop and native-to-terminal latency on one computer; the source reports 0.72 to 1.02 s desktop to terminal and 0.10 to 0.11 s back.
Proof notes: Source: `rt` `71b62cba83e3` (`engine.ts`, `replica.ts`, the `main.ts` watcher, `SyncStatus.tsx`), unchanged in 0.9.57 `5ea5c22310ff`. Four code slices: `fd211678381a` (store), `f7b821c7fd06` (app and close), `4906752b63ef` (Git), `5d117249877d` (latency harness).

Store (`quire_store::live_sync`). The rules are clock-free values that take the time as an argument: `PublishTimer` (both windows; the deadline set by the first save holds until publishing starts, as `publishDeadline ??=` does), `StatusMemory` (rewrite when the outbox count differs or 60 s passed), `nearby` (another device, same `label`, heartbeat under 120 s), `Imports` (one run, one rerun for any number of requests during it), `ProblemMemory` (warn only for a device, file, and reason the previous import also hit), and `delivered` (a path under `devices/` with no dot-prefixed part). `write_status` writes `.status-*` in the device folder, syncs it, renames it over `status.json`, and syncs the folder; `read_statuses` skips dot folders, unreadable or incomplete files, counts above 2^53 - 1, and a status naming another device, as `statusProblem` does. Field names are rt's camelCase. `host_label` is `gethostname`, as `os.hostname()` and Go's `os.Hostname()` return. `NotebookStore::watch_outbox` sends a numbered message whenever a save or merge adds to the outbox, and `publication_state` returns the outbox count, the last published sequence, and the number of the last save it counted, so the app never mistakes an older count for a newer save.

App (`crates/quire/src/live_sync.rs`, window wiring in `editor_host/stored/live.rs`). `Notebook::Store` gains an optional `folder` (serialized only when set, so existing `profiles.json` files read unchanged); `--synced-folder` sets it and `--migrate-from-rt` records rt's folder. `LiveSync` imports at open, then publishes (leftover saves go out and `status.json` is written at open), listens for saves, watches the folder with Zed's `RealFs` watcher, and every 30 s rescans and publishes (which refreshes the heartbeat), as rt's `rescanTimer` does. Publication loops `publish_to` until the outbox is empty, then writes the status if due; durability ordering is unchanged because each change file is still acknowledged only after `publish_to` synchronizes it, and a failure leaves publication uncertain in the store exactly as before. Imports follow rescan continuations, record `nearby` and the other computers from `status.json`, and emit the changed pages; the window calls the new `PagePersistence::store_moved`, which runs NR11.4's merge reading the version from the store, and refreshes the page list. Overlap merges add rt's warning naming the page. The status line and details follow `SyncStatus.tsx`.

Close coordination (NR33). The live sync is one of the window's close services: busy ("Saved changes are being published to the synced folder.") while a save is heard of, counted, or publishing; failed when a publication failed with saves waiting. `finish_close` starts publishing at once instead of waiting for the schedule; a failure from before the current close request is retried once, and a failure during it asks at once with rt's message plus "Closing anyway keeps them saved on this computer; Quire publishes them the next time this notebook opens."

Tests. Store (`live_sync::tests`, 11): both windows with steady-typing deadlines, a neighbour lost mid-burst, heartbeat suppression and refresh, nearby with own, stale, other-label, and slightly future statuses, retry-then-warn and a new reason starting quiet, the rerun rule, dot and outside paths, status round trip with rt's field names, a Go `quire-tui` status with an extra field, and seven damaged statuses skipped, an unavailable folder's message, the outbox signal and counts, and the host name. App with the executor's fake clock (`live_sync::tests`, 9): publication at exactly 1,000 ms (not 999) and a five-save burst published as one file at exactly 5,000 ms; 150 ms and the 1 s ceiling next to a terminal-app status; a stale neighbour and another computer's label leaving the calm window; status written at open with heartbeat at the start time, unchanged by a publication that leaves the count at 0 and by the 30 s rescan, rewritten at 60 s; an import held after its rescan while a late file lands and another import is asked for, which then runs again and imports the late file (a mutation that drops the rerun fails it); a half-written change file imported quietly after the 1 s retry (not at 999 ms); a file still damaged after the retry warning once; the watcher ignoring five dot or outside paths and importing 50 ms (not 49 ms) after a delivered file; and two writers on one host label with real watchers converging on one head and the merged text of concurrent edits to one page. Window (`live_tests`, 5): a page saved before opening published at open, another writer's change reaching the open page; quit with a slow publication (held) asking "still finishing" after 3 s and quitting once it lands; a failed publication (read-only log folder) asking at once, Keep editing keeping the save in the outbox, and the next quit retrying and quitting; an unavailable folder (moved away) with Close anyway quitting and the save kept; Personal Git sync publishing the save before the commit (two log files in the pushed tree) and bringing another computer's pushed change into the open page. Store Git (`tests/git_sync.rs`): temporary dot files under `devices/` are never committed.

Latency, measured. `quire --measure-sync-latency 10` (debug build, test-support, one Mac, two sessions with writers `desk-a` and `desk-b` and separate databases on one scratch folder, both seeing each other as nearby; time from the save's acknowledgement to the other session's import of it). Run 1: desk-a to desk-b min 0.332 s, median 0.414 s, max 0.523 s; desk-b to desk-a min 0.323 s, median 0.431 s, max 0.467 s. Run 2: 0.311 / 0.433 / 0.747 s and 0.314 / 0.340 / 0.503 s. Other agents' builds held the load average between 58 and 85 during both runs. A probe in run 1's build showed the parts: the 150 ms window, about 100 to 250 ms to publish (each change file and its folder are synchronized before acknowledgment), about 45 ms from the file landing to the import starting (watcher latency plus the 50 ms debounce), and 17 to 25 ms to import. A release build and an idle machine are unmeasured. rt's own numbers, which these are not: desktop to terminal 0.72 to 1.02 s and terminal to desktop 0.10 to 0.11 s (rt `plans/live-sync.md`, measured with the real apps, including the editor's 450 ms save pause, which this harness does not include). Native to rt desktop and native to the Go terminal app were not measured: neither ran here.

Deviations from rt. A close or quit publishes before it finishes and asks when publication is slow or fails, where rt's `will-quit` publishes once and logs a failure. Pages close with their typing recorded for recovery, not saved, so only saved changes publish at quit, as before. The status line has no hover; its meanings are shown under it. The synced folder must be named (`--synced-folder` or a profile); rt opens a Quire notebook by its folder.

Remaining: native-to-desktop and native-to-terminal latency with the real rt apps; a release-build measurement on an idle machine; Windows and Linux watchers (FSEvents only); real cloud providers; a window to open a Quire notebook by its synced folder alone (today `--notebook-db` and the identities are needed, or a profile); and rt's `separateSamePaths`, which is outside this task.
Status: done

What to do: Implement Quire block boundaries, source positions, IDs, references, title/frontmatter handling, and incremental analysis. Evaluate parser libraries against the frozen oracle, then implement Quire's extensions.
Constraints: No incidental text normalization, new IDs, or imported-script execution. Translate UTF-16 fixture offsets explicitly.
Write set: `crates/quire_core/src/markdown/`, parser/reference fixtures.
Dependencies: NR01.

Proof notes: pending.
Status: in_progress

#### NR10.1: Port shared page-name, relative-path, and frontmatter-boundary helpers

What to do: Establish the planned `quire_core` crate with pure UTF-8 metadata helpers derived from the frozen Markdown module. Reuse existing Unicode normalization for new-page names and preserve logical link-resolution behavior without changing notebook contents.
Constraints: Return byte offsets for native text and compare them explicitly with the legacy UTF-16 offsets. Match ECMAScript trimming, NFC, reserved names, strict URI decoding, fragments, and vault traversal. Logical link resolution is not filesystem authorization; file adapters must enforce notebook boundaries and ordinary-file policies. Preserve raw source and existing IDs. Do not claim lone-surrogate native editing or complete Markdown parity.
Verify: Frozen-oracle fixtures cover Unicode, limits, reserved names, malformed escapes, relative/root links, CRLF/BOM frontmatter, incomplete delimiters, and deterministic generated inputs. Verify the new package introduces no external dependency version, and keep the full Markdown/identity parent open.
Proof notes: Added `quire_core` with `page_name`, `safe_page_path`, `relative_vault_path`, and frontmatter byte-boundary helpers. New names reuse the existing pinned Unicode normalization package, ECMAScript whitespace, UTF-16 length limits, and reserved-name checks. URI decoding rejects malformed percent sequences and invalid UTF-8 before logical traversal resolution. The logical result still requires filesystem validation before use. The package has no editor, storage, or JavaScript runtime dependency. Its only runtime dependency is the existing Unicode normalization version; the lockfile adds only the local package and the optional editor edge.

`tools/metadata-vectors.ts` generates 640 deterministic fixtures directly from the frozen Markdown module. Rust tests compare outputs/error text and explicitly translate frontmatter byte offsets to the oracle's UTF-16 lengths. They cover BOM/CRLF, incomplete and empty envelopes, Unicode normalization/trimming/limits, reserved/control characters, fragments, strict escapes, and root-relative/traversal cases. A second test proves streaming scans stop at a verified delimiter or an invalid opener without consuming the remaining page. Both fixture generators reproduce their checked-in output exactly; the development generator passes strict TypeScript checking with the temporary tooling recorded in NR09.3.3.

Two new native fixtures reproduced the prior editor scanner treating the first body line as a closing delimiter, exposing later metadata as ordinary blocks. The shared scanner now consumes borrowed rope characters with constant scanner state and no page or line string allocation; it preserves the frozen envelope rule that requires a body newline before a closing delimiter. Editor block analysis uses that scanner, replacing its separate implementation. All 49 Markdown block/span vectors match, including both red regressions. Source text and IDs are not rewritten.

`CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo cargo test -p quire -p quire_core --offline --features quire/test-support` passes all 99 native app tests and the metadata fixture test; after adding the bounded-consumption regression, `cargo test -p quire_core --locked` passes both core tests. `bun run test:reference` passes 57 tests. Combined Quire/core all-target Clippy with test support and `-D warnings`, `cargo check -p quire --no-default-features --locked`, native build, formatting, strict generator checking, prose lint, and scoped whitespace pass. The existing debug unwind warning remains. No vendor file changed.

Sequential review checked frozen-source provenance, new versus existing path semantics, percent decoding/traversal, UTF-8/UTF-16 bounds, Unicode trimming/NFC/reserved names, streamed delimiter state and early termination, editor feature gating, native regressions, and dependency changes. The simplification pass keeps one streaming metadata implementation shared by text and rope callers. Full page-title/frontmatter tags, references, identity operations, incremental semantic analysis, notebook page creation/navigation, native lone-surrogate editing policy, and the rest of NR10 remain open. The 10,000 sync schedule run continues independently.
Status: done

They retain 20,000-level parse/serialize/clone/drop, WTF-8 validation, lone-surrogate ordering/round trips, duplicate keys, malformed grammar, Node-compatible UTF-8 replacement, and overflowing-number assertions. The prior six storage-owned JSON tests are now counted under core, with no assertions removed. `bun run test:reference` passes 57 tests, and `CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo bun run test:storage-crash` passes all 12 process-kill checks. #### NR10.3: Port page titles and frontmatter tags

What to do: Add lossless page-title extraction and ordered frontmatter tag extraction using the frozen Markdown module as the oracle. Reuse the shared envelope scanner and JSON/string representation.
Constraints: Preserve fallback filename behavior, JSON-to-JavaScript-string semantics, quoted/raw titles, multiline/Unicode whitespace, duplicate tag order, and the legacy lightweight tag forms. Parse data only. Keep original source untouched and distinguish lossless metadata from native display strings.
Verify: Differential fixed and seeded fixtures including surrogate escapes, non-finite JSON numbers, nested values, object conversion failures, CRLF/BOM, raw quoting, inline/list tags, duplicate fields, and malformed/incomplete metadata. Record any verified behavior gap before claiming the parent complete.
Proof notes: Added `page_title` and `frontmatter_tags` over the shared envelope scanner. Title extraction preserves the source's exact lowercase key, multiline anchors, greedy ECMAScript whitespace, JSON conversion, raw quote removal, and basename fallback. The core JSON module provides iterative data-to-JavaScript-string conversion, including null/empty array positions, nested arrays, ordinary objects, noncallable own `toString` fallback, signed zero, and overflowing numbers. No notebook functions or scripts execute. The returned `JsString` preserves lone surrogates; a read-only UTF-16 iterator and optional native UTF-8 view keep that boundary explicit. Ordered tag extraction preserves inline/list forms, duplicate fields/tags, lightweight quote/hash handling, and its stricter header line endings. Metadata is borrowed from the existing source; no source or imported identifier is rewritten.

`tools/frontmatter-vectors.ts` generates 725 fixed and seeded cases directly from the frozen Markdown module. Rust compares exact title code units, native representability, and tag order. Cases include BOM/CRLF and incomplete envelopes, multiline/Unicode whitespace, raw/mismatched/trailing quotes, invalid JSON, surrogate pairs/lone surrogates, finite/overflowing numbers, object conversion failures, 128-level array conversion, repeated fields, and tag/list boundaries. The first native implementation incorrectly accepted a tag header ending at a Unicode line separator. The frozen fixture and a direct Node regex check confirmed that the non-multiline tag expression requires the actual line end; the native parser now matches without changing the oracle. Bundling the same generator for Node produces byte-identical fixtures to Bun, and regenerating through Bun is deterministic.

`CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store --locked --features quire/test-support` passes all 211 tests: 99 app, nine core, and 103 storage. `bun run test:reference` passes 57 tests. Combined all-target Clippy with test support and `-D warnings`, native build, strict TypeScript generator checking, formatting, prose lint, and scoped whitespace pass. No dependency, lockfile, vendor, schema, or existing codec algorithm changed. The existing debug unwind warning remains.

Sequential review checked envelope slicing, UTF-8/UTF-16 boundaries, JavaScript anchors/whitespace/coercion, plain-data parsing, object fallback propagation, empty/null array joins, quote and list handling, duplicate order, raw-source preservation, lossless string representation, unchanged storage behavior, and the Node/Bun fixture comparison. The simplification pass reuses the existing JSON arena and byte-boundary scanner, with small metadata/coercion modules and no second parser or regex dependency. #### NR10.4: Port reference syntax over explicit source exclusions

What to do: Add shared native page/block/embed/PDF reference records and lexical extraction using current parser-provided code ranges. Preserve the frozen patterns, first-match behavior, labels, PDF precedence, whitespace, and byte ranges.
Constraints: Add frontmatter exclusions internally; preserve the source's start-point exclusion semantics. Normalize range order once for bounded lookup work. Use the existing regex package and explicit ECMAScript whitespace rather than silently accepting Rust's different whitespace class. This API requires code exclusions; it does not itself establish native Markdown parsing parity or navigation.

#### NR10.8: Match reference block boundaries on the page-index corpus

What to do: Make native block analysis match the reference `parseBlocks` on every page-index vector, then empty `tests/fixtures/markdown/page-index-block-divergences.json`.
Constraints: Fix block derivation in `markdown_blocks.rs` first; change the vendored grammar only where node boundaries themselves differ, and keep NR10.5.1 and NR10.7 reference and tag gates green. No text normalization.
Write set: `crates/quire/src/markdown_blocks.rs`, `vendor/quire-markdown` scanner where required, block fixtures.
Verify: `page_index` oracle test with an empty divergence list, the existing block, reference, and tag vector tests, and incremental-edit agreement.
Proof notes: The 49-input block oracle passed, but the 1,215-page analysis corpus (`tools/page-index-vectors.ts`) found 175 divergent pages, all from block boundaries: blockquotes and fences indented one to three spaces start at the line instead of the marker (43), list `subtreeTo` includes trailing whitespace (45), unclosed fences end one unit early (26), paragraphs and quotes absorb a following pipe table or lazy line (around 60), and a bare `#` is a paragraph rather than an empty heading. Links and tags matched on all 1,040 pages whose blocks matched.

Derivation fixed 132 of the 175 pages without touching the grammar. Lezer starts quotes, fences, and headings at their marker, so native starts now skip leading blanks. A list item starts after its container's prefix: the parent item's content column (marker width plus up to four spaces, else one) or a quote's `>` and one optional space. Each block ends at the end of its last line, keeping trailing spaces but not trailing blank lines; quotes, items, and lists end with their last child. An unclosed fence keeps every line up to the block that ends it, blank lines included, so it ends at the line break before the next block or at EOF. Lists nested under a heading inside an item now count as nested, because tree-sitter wraps them in a `section` that Lezer lacks.

The other 43 needed grammar changes, since the node boundaries themselves differed. The scanner already refused paragraph-interrupting tables to fix seeded-299 in NR10.5.1; that was too broad. Lezer's GFM `endLeaf` lets a table interrupt a paragraph or quote, including from a lazy line that closes the quote, when the line has an unescaped pipe, the raw next line matches `delimiterLine`, and `parseRow` counts the same cells from the line's base position. It never does so when the paragraph's first line has a pipe, which is the seeded-299 case. The line-ending simulation now feeds every advanced character into a small row counter and delimiter state machine, after the existing block-start check, so lines starting with block characters such as `#a | b` are still judged correctly. The first-line rule needs state that survives scanner calls, which only an emitted token persists. A hidden zero-width external token, accepted at the start of paragraphs and link reference definitions and within paragraph lines, records the result in two scanner state bits. It is emitted at the line start when nothing was consumed, else at the first inline token, which by construction is never a backslash. Pipe tables never interrupt tables. ATX headings may end at EOF, so a bare `#` or `# h` without a final newline is a heading instead of an ERROR node. The parser is regenerated with CLI 0.26.11 and ABI 14 with one new declared conflict (link reference definition versus paragraph); `node-types.json` is unchanged. UPSTREAM.json and the vendor README record the divergence.

The ratchet list and mechanism are deleted: the page-index test now requires every vector to match blocks, links, tags, and front tags, and a new test replays the whole corpus through minimal Unicode-safe edits in one persistent buffer and checks blocks after each reparse. The generator gains 39 targeted cases (1,254 vectors): indented quotes/fences/headings, list bases under items, quotes, and tabs, trailing spaces, unclosed fences in items, quotes, nested lists, and at EOF, headings at EOF and in items, and tables interrupting paragraphs, lazy quotes, and lazy items, inside quotes and items, with mismatched counts, escaped or first-line pipes, trailing-space delimiters, four-space headers, link references, block-start characters, CRLF, and pipes inside code. The nested-list fence case failed first and drove treating `list` as a container when ending blocks. The analyzer version is bumped so existing query indexes rebuild.

Gates on 2026-10-30: `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store -p tree-sitter-quire-markdown --locked --features quire/test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` passes 308 tests, including the 49 block, 569 reference, and 595 tag/completion vectors from fresh and incremental buffers and the original-grammar coexistence test. App Clippy with `--features test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` and core/store/grammar Clippy pass with `-D warnings`; formatting, `bun run test:reference` (57), and `bun run verify:vendor vendor/quire-markdown` pass. The upstream byte audit was not rerun because no pinned upstream checkout is available; the modified files are listed in UPSTREAM.json.

Follow-up on 2026-10-31 (list interrupts and EOF markers): batch A's outline vectors and an unresolved embed exposed three more gaps. First, Lezer's `isBulletList`/`isOrderedList` with `breaking` let an empty bullet interrupt a paragraph whenever any open context is a `BulletList`, and any ordered marker whenever one is an `OrderedList`; outside, only a non-empty item (ordered: starting at 1) interrupts, even on a lazy line. The scanner only knew `LIST_ITEM` plus indentation and gated on `matched == open_blocks.size`, so `- holder ^spot\n  - \n` was one paragraph (ID lost, `((spot))` unresolved) and `> foo\n-` started a list. Open blocks now carry an `ordered` flag per entry, serialized as packed bits after the blocks with a block-count byte inside the existing overflow guard, and `in_list` walks the whole stack like Lezer's `inList`. Second, a marker or thematic break ending at EOF (`- Parent\n- Sibling\n-`, `1.`, `a\n***`) was not recognized; marker lines now end at EOF, and a marker at EOF gets one zero-width blank line so its item closes without an ERROR node. The old block-analysis workaround that reparsed with a virtual `x\n` body after an EOF marker, which produced the wrong boundary for a bare `-`, is deleted. Third, "a code span holding `[[x]]` ends early" was not a grammar gap: the inline grammar yields the right `code_span`, but `outline::literal` walked up with `Node::parent`, which loses an injected layer's offset and skipped the span at `]]`. It now walks down from the layer root. The parser tables are unchanged, so no regeneration was needed.

The generator gains 20 targeted cases (1,274 vectors) from the pinned Lezer reference: empty items interrupting under bullets, quotes in lists, ordered-in-ordered, lazy ordered/bullet markers outside their kind, empty siblings, markers and breaks at EOF, and the code span case; some, such as empty siblings, already matched and guard against regressions. `outline_commands_match_rt_parity_vectors` no longer excludes any vector, `an_embed_of_an_item_above_an_empty_child_resolves` checks the embed end to end, `carets_in_an_injected_code_span_are_literal` covers the offset walk, and three grammar tests cover interrupts, EOF markers, and list kinds across an incremental reparse. The analyzer version is bumped to 6.

Remaining divergence outside this corpus: Lezer counts the next line's cells from a UTF-16 base position and expands tabs inside container prefixes; the scanner counts characters and approximates the base from consumed indentation, so prefixes mixing tabs and astral characters may still differ.
Status: done

### NR11: Complete one native daily-page workflow

What to do: Wire the editor to the native service: create/open a daily page, edit in either presentation, record recovery, save, close, restart, and merge a peer's update into the open page.
Constraints: Shared buffers keyed by notebook/page identity; expected-version checks on asynchronous operations. Opening a notebook loads metadata and the needed page, not every body. Refresh mounted-page heads after a rescan error as well as success, because already committed batches can precede a later failure.
Write set: notebook session/buffer registry, service command types, native vertical-slice tests.
Dependencies: NR04, NR05, NR08, NR09, NR10.
Verify: An end-to-end native test covers editing through an embed, undo, crash recovery, local save, publication, remote update, a peer update merged into unsaved typing, and a stale save merged from its opened base. Measure first-editable latency and loaded body count.
Proof notes: The transactional page boundary and asynchronous native page registry are implemented below. The default editor remains an in-memory example; explicit native database arguments open stored pages. Existing healthy pages now support recovery, local saves, and guarded close/quit. Daily creation, navigation, synchronization integration, and complete parent acceptance remain open. Since `rt` 0.9.53, peer updates and stale saves merge instead of producing a conflict; NR11.4 owns that workflow.
Status: in_progress

#### NR11.1: Check out one page and refuse a stale native save

What to do: Add a single worker request that reads ordered head identities, one selected revision, and recovery in one SQLite snapshot. Add a checked native save that compares observed heads inside the save transaction. Preserve the existing conflict-producing protocol save API.
Constraints: Load at most one revision body, with a separate recovery body when present. Keep revision JSON lossless, including unrepresentable UTF-16 strings and unknown fields. A changed-head response must not create a revision, alter recovery, or acknowledge a save. Conflict resolution remains explicit.
Write set: notebook checkout and checked-save APIs, storage worker commands, focused storage tests.
Verify: Missing/new/deleted/conflicting pages; explicit head selection; recovery survives reopen; unrelated damaged bodies are not read; changed-head and invalid-parent saves preserve heads/outbox/drafts; worker command ordering and startup errors. Run storage tests and all-target Clippy.
Proof notes: `notebook/checkout.rs` reads ordered head IDs, at most one lossless revision body, and optional recovery under one deferred SQLite transaction. The selected revision must still be a head of the requested page. The shared transaction wrapper retains rollback/poison behavior; write transactions still use BEGIN IMMEDIATE. Both commands use the existing bounded worker and asynchronous Reply, with request byte accounting for identities and expected heads.

`save_if_heads` compares heads inside the write transaction and delegates to the existing atomic save only when they match. Its parents must equal the one opened version (or both lists must be empty for creation). It cannot implicitly resolve multiple heads. Changed heads return a typed HeadsChanged result without writing a revision or consuming recovery. Existing protocol save/merge behavior remains available. An acknowledgment lost after commit may produce HeadsChanged on retry; callers must reconcile the opened version before retrying rather than assume the old request was unsaved.

Eight focused tests cover empty pages, conflicts, explicit selection, tombstones, Unicode recovery across restart, unrepresentable UTF-16 and unknown revision fields, stale-head and stale-editor-version rejection, unchanged outbox/recovery on refusal, explicit merge preservation, ordered worker requests, and closed-worker rejection. Corrupting unselected/conflicting and unrelated compressed bodies proves checkout does not load them. A second connection holds an uncommitted writer transaction while checkout succeeds with the committed state, proving the read path does not acquire the writer lock. Failed body validation rolls back and leaves later checkouts usable.

The initial focused test build failed for the missing API, then the six initial cases passed. `CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo cargo test -p quire_store --locked` passes all 111 tests after the concurrency/version cases were added (`/private/tmp/quire-checkout-storage-tests.log`). All-target storage Clippy with -D warnings passes (`/private/tmp/quire-checkout-clippy.log`). Formatting, prose lint, and staged whitespace pass. Sequential review checked transaction isolation, read-lock behavior, rollback reuse, lossless representation, parent matching, one-body loading, queue closure/admission, and unchanged conflict-producing protocol saves. The simplification pass reuses the existing transaction and save implementations, adding no dependency, schema, or storage representation.

This completes only the storage boundary. Canonical native buffer registration, recovery/save acknowledgments tied to buffer versions, daily-page routing, rescan refresh after both success and error, and end-to-end UI/performance acceptance remain NR11 work.
Rebaseline note: `rt` `71b62cba83e3` no longer refuses a stale save in Quire notebooks; the engine saves it from its opened base and merges. This checked primitive stays as the internal guard. NR11.4 owns the merge path, and refusal remains for an unknown base revision or a path now held by another page.
Status: done

#### NR11.2: Open stored pages through one native buffer registry

What to do: Add a notebook-owned page registry that deduplicates concurrent opens, waits for storage startup asynchronously, prepares selected content off the foreground thread, and shares one PageAnalysis/Buffer across views. Expose stored-page previews through explicit database/notebook/writer/page arguments while persistent editing is being connected.
Constraints: Keep previews read-only until recovery, save, and close guards are wired. Do not silently replace unrepresentable UTF-16 strings. Preserve storage failures and allow retry on the same page model. Retain visited models for shared history; bounded eviction remains an explicit later policy. Load one selected revision plus optional recovery, not the notebook.
Write set: notebook session model, native loading/preview host, application dependencies, focused native tests, README.
Verify: Duplicate pending/loaded opens share identity; notebooks isolate the same page ID; recovery takes precedence without losing original storage; tombstones/conflicts are visible; startup and representation failures remain actionable; retries do not replace existing loaded buffers. Draw the stored page with two read-only editor views. Run app/storage tests, Clippy, build, formatting, and prose lint.
Proof notes: `notebook_session.rs` owns one bounded storage client and a registry of retained page entities per opened notebook. Repeated pending or completed opens return the same entity, buffer, and analysis model. Storage readiness uses a shared asynchronous future. Checkout, lossless-field validation, content extraction, and status preparation run in a background task. The foreground installs the owned text into the copied editor and one PageAnalysis; no full-notebook body list is requested. Buffer construction still performs the copied editor's rope/line-ending work on the foreground thread, which remains a performance and persistence integration concern rather than a measured acceptance result.

`editor_host/stored.rs` adds an asynchronous loading/preview host and explicit database/notebook/writer/page arguments. It constructs the existing two-pane editor only after a successful checkout. Read-only source capability prevents typing, Vim deletion, and projected controls from changing persisted-preview content. It shows recovery, conflicts, selected tombstones, and retryable read errors. Recoverable read failures retry on the same registered page; startup or stopped-worker failures require reopening. Loaded buffers cannot be replaced by Retry. Unrepresentable path/text strings fail without a replacement buffer; original lossless records remain in storage. This is an explicit interim display limitation, not complete UTF-16 parity.

Ten new tests cover concurrent-open identity, same page IDs in different notebooks, recovery precedence and retained originals, missing-page zero-body loading, startup/stopped errors, repaired-body retry, unrepresentable strings/unknown fields without storage changes, option validation, and a real headless draw of a recovered two-pane page with both conflicts and a selected tombstone. The draw test activates Write in the second pane, attempts Vim edits, and verifies unchanged buffer text, heads, outbox, and recovery. Argument parsing now precedes input-harness selection; mixed stored/input arguments cannot silently open the memory harness. The hardcoded Welcome header was removed from the shared editor host.

The initial focused test build failed for the missing model; the first four registry cases then passed. Final `CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo cargo test -p quire -p quire_store --locked --features quire/test-support` passes 229 tests (118 app and 111 storage, `/private/tmp/quire-session-final-tests.log`). All-target app Clippy with test support and -D warnings passes (`/private/tmp/quire-session-final-clippy.log`); the locked native build passes (`/private/tmp/quire-session-build.log`). The input-only configuration passes `cargo check -p quire --locked --no-default-features`. Two built-binary CLI smoke cases exit 2 with actionable errors before desktop initialization. Formatting, prose lint, and staged whitespace pass. Cargo.lock only gains four direct application edges to existing packages, with no version changes. The existing linker unwind-size warning remains.

Sequential review checked pending-open deduplication, notebook scoping, source/analysis identity, task ownership, failed startup versus missing pages, retry races, lossless storage preservation, capability propagation through projections, argument routing before window/storage startup, and accurate status/timing claims. The simplification pass reuses the existing worker, shared futures, Markdown setup, and editor window. Native desktop rendering, first-editable/release timings, persistent edits, live rescan refresh, and global notebook-open ownership remain parent work. No new vendored code or schema was introduced.
Status: done

#### NR11.3: Enable durable native editing

What to do: Retain opening revision/draft ancestry and recovery stamps in the native page model, then connect versioned recovery and checked saves to the shared editors. Protect close/quit before allowing stored pages to become editable. Keep distinct edited, recovery-recorded, saved-locally, and published states.
Constraints: Capture cheap immutable buffer snapshots on the foreground; copy/normalize/serialize text off it. Preserve original line-ending content when persisting, and keep unrepresentable originals lossless. Serialize per-page persistence without blocking new typing; delayed acknowledgments must not mark later edits as saved. Refused stale writes and queue/storage errors retain the buffer and durable recovery. Reuse one notebook owner across additional views. Inspect actual native quit/close hooks before choosing interception; a fixed shutdown grace period alone cannot establish durability.
Write set: notebook session model and persistence commands, save actions/status, close/quit coordination, native storage/editor integration tests.
Verify: Edit either view and a semantic excerpt, undo across views, record recovery, save/reopen, keep later typing dirty after an earlier acknowledgment, refuse stale heads, and retain text after busy/full/failed storage. Close/restart restores acknowledged work; close/quit failures keep work accessible. Measure foreground work and first-editable/body counts without presenting debug or preview timings as release acceptance.
Proof notes: Inspection found that App::quit directly asks the platform to terminate, while on_app_quit runs during shutdown with a 200 ms grace period. macOS invokes its existing quit callback in applicationWillTerminate and ignores the returned boolean. Durable editing therefore first needs the cancellable request boundary below. Daily-page routing, publication/rescan UI integration, refresh after rescan errors, and the complete parent end-to-end test still remain afterward.
The copied LineEnding::normalize converts both CRLF and bare CR to LF, and LineEnding::apply explicitly does not preserve mixed endings. Persistence must account for this before accepting edits rather than treating a uniform line-ending conversion as preservation. Buffer::local_normalized accepts a prepared rope; text snapshots expose old/new byte-range edits through edits_since. These are candidates for moving preparation off the foreground and retaining untouched original wire ranges, subject to focused round-trip tests.
Status: in_progress

##### NR11.3.1: Allow persistence to defer quit before native termination

What to do: Add an opt-in GPUI quit-request subscription that can veto programmatic quits without consuming shutdown handlers. Route macOS applicationShouldTerminate through the same checks before applicationWillTerminate. Expose the request path and platform-quit counts in the existing headless test platform so native host guards can be tested without terminating the test runner.
Constraints: Preserve default behavior when no guard exists and retain the existing shutdown callback semantics. Invoke all guards; preserve subscription cancellation. Do not block the foreground or treat the shutdown timeout as durability. Reject reentrant quit requests. Native OS behavior on other platforms remains an explicit acceptance gap; programmatic quits must use guards everywhere.
Write set: copied GPUI app/platform/test interfaces, copied macOS delegate hook, authored API-consumer tests, upstream fork manifest.
Verify: Programmatic and simulated native veto; default allow; multiple guards and unsubscribe; deferred completion before platform quit; no consumed shutdown handlers or closed windows after veto; reentrant requests. Build the real macOS delegate and run app/GPUI tests, Clippy, source audits, formatting, and prose lint. Native desktop and Windows/Linux event acceptance remain open.
Proof notes: The copied App now exposes on_request_quit and checks every live subscription before asking the platform to quit. A veto retains subscriptions, windows, and final shutdown handlers. Reentrant requests fail while the outer check runs; a scoped reset restores the evaluation flag afterward. The existing on_app_quit and its shutdown grace period remain unchanged. The platform interface has an optional native request callback, so existing implementations retain their source compatibility. macOS now registers applicationShouldTerminate and returns the pinned AppKit TerminateCancel/TerminateNow values before its original applicationWillTerminate handler. Native callback invocation drops the platform state lock first and uses the existing tracked AppCell borrow API.

The headless platform retains that callback and counts accepted programmatic quit requests. Its test context can simulate a native request without actually shutting down. Forced test teardown remains available and bypasses guards. Five authored API-consumer tests first failed for the absent interfaces, then passed. They cover default allow, programmatic/native veto, retained windows/shutdown handlers, all-guard evaluation, unsubscribe, reentrancy, and asynchronous preparation that stays pending for ten simulated seconds before retrying quit successfully. No storage durability is inferred from that simulated delay; it establishes that the API does not impose the shutdown timeout on preparation.

`CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo cargo test -p gpui -p quire --locked --features gpui/test-support,quire/test-support` passes 408 GPUI unit tests, one action-macro integration test, 123 app tests, and 88 doctests (`/private/tmp/quire-quit-guard-all-tests.log`). The two existing manual timing tests and ten existing doctest ignores are unchanged. All-target Clippy for gpui, gpui_macos, and quire with test support and -D warnings passes (`/private/tmp/quire-quit-guard-clippy.log`). The locked native app build, including the real macOS delegate, passes (`/private/tmp/quire-quit-guard-build.log`); the existing linker unwind-size warning remains. Formatting, prose lint, and staged whitespace pass.

The upstream audit verifies 3,008 unchanged files and 28 documented fork modifications at the pinned revision, accounting for these five newly modified source files. The tracked vendor/symlink audit passes. No dependency, version, license, ignored test, or assertion was changed. Sequential review checked callback lifetimes and cancellation, eager all-guard evaluation, recursive calls, App borrow refusal, default behavior, Objective-C selector/return encoding against the pinned generated AppKit API, dropped-lock invocation, and shutdown separation. The simplification pass reuses the existing subscription set, platform callback pattern, and deferred cleanup helper.

This provides the pre-termination boundary only. Notebook recovery/save coordination and the per-window close guard are still required before persistent editing can be enabled. Actual macOS desktop termination and native Windows/Linux event acceptance remain NR33/NR35 gates; the test callback simulation is not OS-level evidence.
Status: done

##### NR11.3.2: Preserve stored source through native line-ending normalization

What to do: Prepare normalized editor text and its rope on a background worker, retaining original wire text and a byte-offset map. Apply native snapshot edit ranges back to that original source so untouched LF, CRLF, and bare-CR endings survive; preserve no-op replacements and undo exactly. Retain the original source with the loaded page for persistence. Add Copy Markdown as the first snapshot consumer of that exact-source path, capturing the clicked version and preparing clipboard text off the foreground.
Constraints: No foreground source copy/normalization or extra parser. Use the copied local_normalized constructor and snapshot edit iterator. Keep the core mapping independent of GPUI and reject invalid, overlapping, or unnormalized edits before returning any rewritten source. Preserve the existing immutable original on errors.
Write set: pure core source-text mapping and tests, Markdown prepared-buffer constructor, native checkout preparation and snapshot integration tests.
Verify: LF/CRLF/CR/mixed endings, Unicode and empty/terminal lines, insert/delete/replace around newline boundaries, unchanged/semantic no-op source identity, whole-page undo/redo, stale/different-buffer integration boundaries, and stored-recovery round trips. Run core/app/storage tests, Clippy, build, formatting, and prose lint. Keep full persistence and close coordination pending.
Proof notes: `quire_core::source_text` retains immutable original text plus normalized-byte offsets for CRLF boundaries. Its ordered SourceEdit iterator replaces only the requested source ranges, preserving untouched bytes and semantically unchanged replacements. No effective edit returns the original Arc. Replacement newlines use the first opened LF/CRLF/CR style, with LF for a page without line breaks. Invalid UTF-8 boundaries, out-of-range/reversed/overlapping edits, and carriage returns in normalized replacements fail without returning a partial rewrite. The module uses only std and remains independent of GPUI.

`stored_source.rs` prepares normalized text and the copied rope on the background checkout path. The foreground moves that rope into Buffer::local_normalized through the shared Markdown setup and retains its opening snapshot with the original source. Rendering a captured snapshot uses the copied edits_since iterator and rejects another buffer or a snapshot predating its baseline. The opening baseline remains fixed so full undo can recover original mixed endings after intermediate checkpoints. Explicit native line-ending metadata changes use a separate uniform conversion path; ordinary text edits preserve untouched original endings. Loaded pages share this immutable adapter through an Arc, keeping page state and background requests small without copying its opening snapshot for each request.

Stored-page previews now expose Copy Markdown. It captures the clicked snapshot, prepares source text off the foreground, and writes the prepared string through the copied clipboard API. Pending copies show Copying Markdown; replacing/dropping the owned task prevents an older completion from overwriting a newer request. Source byte counts join the existing loaded-body/readiness metrics. This copies full Markdown source, including recovery text and hidden source syntax, rather than a flattened standalone export.

Five core tests cover normalization, byte translation, mixed styles, boundary inserts/deletes, exact no-op Arc identity, and unchanged originals on invalid edits. Five native tests cover actual snapshot edit ranges, all 27 combinations of three line-break styles, Unicode replacements, captured versions while later typing continues, foreign/predating rejection, explicit line-ending conversion, and 120 deterministic edits followed by full undo/redo. The existing drawn stored-recovery test now uses mixed CRLF/CR content, checks normalized read-only views, copies exact original recovery text through the headless clipboard, and verifies unchanged stored heads, outbox, and recovery.

`CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store --locked --features quire/test-support` passes 259 tests (128 app, 20 core, 111 storage; `/private/tmp/quire-source-roundtrip-all-tests.log`). After sharing the adapter and adding copy feedback, all 128 app tests pass again (`/private/tmp/quire-source-roundtrip-final-app-tests.log`). All-target core/store/app Clippy with -D warnings passes (`/private/tmp/quire-source-roundtrip-final-clippy.log`), and final app Clippy passes after the UI feedback change (`/private/tmp/quire-source-roundtrip-final-ui-clippy.log`). The locked native build passes with existing dependency artifacts (`/private/tmp/quire-source-roundtrip-final-build.log`), retaining its existing linker warning. Formatting, prose lint, and staged whitespace pass. Cargo.lock gains only the direct application edge to the already copied rope crate; no dependency versions or vendored sources change.

An additional plain --no-default-features check fails when an uncached gpui_apple variant attempts shader compilation: the selected Xcode installation lacks the Metal toolchain (`/private/tmp/quire-source-roundtrip-input-check.log`). The compiler invocation independently reports that missing component. The input-only Rust configuration checks with the existing gpui_platform/runtime_shaders feature (`/private/tmp/quire-source-roundtrip-input-runtime-check.log`); this diagnostic does not satisfy the precompiled-shader or clean-build gate. No source feature/default, build requirement, or verification assertion was changed to hide the failure. NR35 records the clean-build limitation.

Sequential review checked byte/Unicode boundaries, complete CRLF mapping, ordered sparse edits, semantic no-ops, immutable errors, snapshot identity/ancestry, fixed-baseline undo, explicit metadata changes, source ownership, foreground/background call sites, copy task lifetime, and preserved read-only/storage behavior. The simplification pass reuses the native snapshot iterator, rope constructor, Markdown initialization, and clipboard API rather than introducing a diff engine or parser. This completes source preparation and its copy consumer. The recovery/save coordinator must still retain selected/draft ancestry and stamps, track acknowledgments against captured text and line-ending state, and secure all dirty pages through close/quit guards before enabling persistent edits. Source copying is not a save acknowledgment or release timing proof.
Status: done

##### NR11.3.3: Connect native recovery, local saves, and guarded close

What to do: Retain opening ancestry and recovery stamps, add one versioned persistence stream per shared page, and connect Save plus debounced recovery and autosave to the native editors. Enable editing only after per-window and application quit guards can secure every retained dirty page. Preserve Source/Write/excerpt sharing and distinct recovery/local-save states.
Constraints: Capture immutable snapshots without copying body text on the foreground. Serialize writes and retain failed frozen requests for safe retry; later typing must remain dirty after an older acknowledgment. Include line-ending metadata in snapshot comparisons. Never replace a stale page's ancestry just to make a save pass. Preserve original text and accessible views after errors. Conflicts, tombstones, missing paths, and out-of-date recovery remain explicit read-only cases until their own resolution/create workflow exists.
Write set: page persistence coordinator, notebook loading metadata, Save actions/status, close/quit coordination, native persistence tests and docs.
Verify: Existing-page edit/save/reopen with mixed line endings; edit during an older save; coalesced recovery; prior-session draft adoption; shared undo and semantic excerpts; stale heads; queue/SQLite errors; close and quit wait for current recovery and retain the window on failure. Verify default in-memory examples cannot acknowledge a save. Vim write-command integration and daily-page routing remain parent requirements if this slice cannot complete them together.
Proof notes: `PagePersistence` retains opening heads and draft stamps beside the shared native buffer. Immutable snapshots include text version and line-ending metadata. Background work maps captured source, records recovery, and submits checked local saves through the existing bounded SQLite worker. Recovery waits 100 ms after typing; autosave waits 450 ms, matching the frozen legacy store. Explicit Save locally and Cmd-S/Ctrl-S bypass that delay. A successful local acknowledgment updates the native buffer's saved version; recovery alone does not. No publication acknowledgment is implied.

Requests serialize per page. Queue/storage errors retain the frozen request and current text for retry, including a recovery acknowledgment that preceded a failed save. Checked-head refusal blocks further saves without adopting external ancestry; newer typing can still be recorded for recovery. The original source adapter preserves mixed line endings through saves. A regression exposed that changing line endings does not advance the native text version: reverting a format change while an older save runs cannot count as secured until the current format has a new recovery acknowledgment. Snapshot comparisons and outstanding-request checks now enforce that rule.

The notebook session enables eligible existing pages only after window-close and app-quit guards are installed. Guards cover retained page controllers and resume after recovery; deferred close rechecks current versions. Failures leave the window, error, and Copy Markdown accessible. Conflicts, tombstones, missing paths/pages, and recovery based on older heads remain read-only pending their own workflows. The in-memory example's existing negative Vim-write test remains unchanged.

Eight coordinator tests cover timer boundaries, mixed-source recovery/save/reopen, prior-session draft adoption, later typing during an older save, busy-queue retry, stopped storage, stale heads, and the format-reversion race. Five native-window tests cover keyboard save, semantic-excerpt edit/source undo, successful guarded close, successful resumed native quit, and quit failure with accessible text/clipboard. Final app/core/store testing passes 272 tests (141 app, 20 core, 111 storage; `/private/tmp/quire-persistence-all-final-tests.log`). All-target app/core/store Clippy with warnings denied passes using the existing optional `gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` feature (`/private/tmp/quire-persistence-runtime-clippy.log`). Plain Clippy attempts fail before app checking because Xcode lacks the Metal toolchain (`/private/tmp/quire-persistence-app-clippy.log`); this diagnostic feature does not satisfy the normal or clean precompiled-shader build gate. The locked normal native build passes with existing dependency artifacts (`/private/tmp/quire-persistence-build.log`), retaining the existing linker unwind warning. Formatting, prose lint, and scoped whitespace checks pass. No build defaults or vendor source changed.

Sequential review checked current-versus-captured acknowledgments, queued snapshots, draft compare-and-swap, retries after partial success, original ancestry, format-only edits, timer cancellation, deferred-close rechecks, root/entity borrow boundaries, task ownership, and read-only exclusions. The simplification pass reuses the source adapter, worker, native saved-version API, existing shared analysis, and copied quit hook. The only lockfile change adds an application edge to the existing UUID dependency for editor-session identities. Native desktop event acceptance, Vim write commands, daily-page routing, live rescan refresh, global notebook ownership, and first-editable/release measurements remain parent work.
Reopened for `rt` 0.9.57. `71b62cba83e3` changed two contracts this slice froze. In Quire notebooks the legacy store still saves at 450 ms idle, but no later than 2 s after the first unsaved edit during steady typing (`saveIdle` and `saveLatest` in `src/core/store.ts`); the native autosave has only the idle timer. Pages with competing heads, stale saves, and recovery based on older heads no longer stay read-only; they merge as NR11.4 describes. Add the 2 s ceiling here; the merge workflow is NR11.4. The 2 s ceiling landed with NR11.4, whose verification includes saves during steady typing.
Status: in_progress

##### NR11.3.4: Route Vim writes through native page persistence

What to do: Reuse the copied Ex parser to identify whole-page write and write-close commands. Route eligible stored-page commands to the existing checked local save stream. Close only after the current page is locally saved and every retained page is secured.
Constraints: Keep in-memory examples and read-only pages explicit failures. Preserve parser abbreviations and existing editor-local validation. Reject filename/range writes until export semantics exist. Force forms cannot bypass checked heads. Storage errors and later typing must keep the window accessible. Leave the upstream workspace command path unchanged.
Write set: copied embedded Vim command hook and source manifest, Quire command prompt, stored window coordination, focused tests and docs.
Verify: Actual Vim :w/:write/:wq routing; preserved mixed source; no close on recovery-only or failed save; later typing during save; default example rejection; unsupported filename/range commands; app and copied parser regressions, lint/build and source audit.
Proof notes: Added a typed `PageCommand` result beside the copied editor-only API. It reuses the original Ex parser without filename completion. Whole-page writes and write-close actions become host requests; other actions keep the existing allowlist and range/pattern validation. The editor-only API still rejects writes. Quire enables write dispatch only for eligible stored pages, and maps requests to the same local-save actions as the keyboard/button. Filename/range writes and unrelated workspace actions remain explicit errors. Force forms still use checked heads.

Write-close now distinguishes local-save completion from recovery-only protection. It keeps the window until the current page is locally saved and every retained page is secured, rechecking in the deferred callback. Later typing waits for its own save; failed saves cancel the pending close and show an actionable error while keeping Copy Markdown available. The initial native :write test failed with the command still open (`/private/tmp/quire-vim-save-red.log`); the connected path passes. Four native-window regressions cover whole-page write/write-close from both panes, later typing during an older save, force-write refusal after changed heads, and rejected filename/range writes. The default example and conflicted/tombstoned recovery preview reject :wq without changing storage.

Final app testing passes all 145 tests (`/private/tmp/quire-vim-write-final-app-tests.log`). `bun run test:vim-command` passes all 26 copied command tests, including the new seven-form write matrix, editor-only rejection, ten unsupported forms, and unchanged workspace write/quit/filename/range cases (`/private/tmp/quire-vim-write-command-tests.log`). Final all-target app/Vim Clippy with warnings denied passes using the existing runtime-shader diagnostic option (`/private/tmp/quire-vim-write-final-clippy.log`); the normal precompiled-shader Clippy gate retains its recorded missing-toolchain limitation. The locked normal native build passes with existing dependency artifacts (`/private/tmp/quire-vim-write-build.log`), retaining the existing linker warning.
