Formatting, prose lint, scoped whitespace, and tracked-vendor verification pass. Upstream comparison verifies 3,008 unchanged files and 28 recorded forks; both modified Vim files were already declared forks and their manifest descriptions now include this hook. No dependencies or lockfile changes occur in this slice.

Sequential review checked parser reuse, unchanged workspace dispatch, exact typed write filtering, default/read-only rejection, command-target lifetime and stale async results, action focus routing, captured versus current saves, recovery-only versus local-save close criteria, deferred rechecks, and error visibility. The simplification pass adds typed host requests to the existing parser and close coordinator rather than adding another parser, workspace, or persistence stream. Native desktop acceptance remains open under NR11/NR33; daily-page routing, synchronization integration, global notebook ownership, and release measurements remain parent requirements.
Status: done

#### NR11.4: Merge incoming and stale edits into open pages

What to do: When an imported revision reaches a page with unsaved edits, merge the edits made since the last saved text into the incoming text and keep autosaving. When a save's base is stale, save from the opened base, let NR09.4 merge, and show the merged text while keeping typing made during the save. At open, save each recovery draft whose base is known and whose page is still at its path, which merges it; keep other drafts for review. Saving to a page deleted elsewhere revives it; unchanged text saved from a deleted version stays deleted with an actionable message.
Constraints: Apply a merge to the shared buffer as one edit that keeps selections anchored and undo usable. Never merge a draft with an unknown base, including drafts restored from a backup or migrated from `rt` 0.9.52 and older, whose `base_known` column is 0. Refuse only an unknown base revision or a path now held by another page. Folder notebooks keep their existing conflict behavior. Show the overlap warning naming the page.
Write set: page persistence coordinator, notebook session import handling, native persistence tests.
Dependencies: NR09.4, NR11.3.
Verify: Port the `rt/tests/store-merge.test.ts` scenarios: merging while typing, a stale save merged while typing continues, kept-both lines with a warning, saves during steady typing, and a restored draft keeping its own base. Add native two-window and native-plus-reference-replica tests that edit one page at the same moment and end with one head and identical text.
Proof notes: Source: `rt` `71b62cba83e3` (`absorb` in `src/core/store.ts`; `save` and `settleDrafts` in `engine.ts`), used as the behavior spec; the design follows Zed's buffer machinery rather than the TypeScript structure.

Stale saves no longer refuse. `PagePersistence` saves with the plain store `save` from the version the editor opened, so the NR09.4 merge runs inside the save transaction. When the acknowledgment carries a merge revision whose text differs from what was sent, the edits made since the sent text merge into the merged text and the editor shows the result; typing made during the save survives. An overlap count above zero sets a warning that names the page path and stays in the status. The `blocked` state and its "changed elsewhere" refusal are gone; Vim force writes now merge like any save.

`PagePersistence::absorb(revision: String, text: impl Into<Arc<str>>, cx)` brings another writer's version into an open page. Merging happens in stored form: the base is the last saved text, the first side is the incoming text, and the second side is the current buffer rendered through `StoredSource`, so original line endings survive. A background task computes the merge with `quire_core::merge::merge_text` and a `language::Diff` from Zed's `text_diff` against the captured snapshot. The foreground applies it with `Buffer::apply_diff` only if the buffer version is unchanged; otherwise it recomputes, because `apply_diff` discards hunks that intersect newer edits and a merge must never drop text. Before applying, `finalize_last_transaction` stops Zed's 300 ms undo grouping from folding the merge into the user's last transaction, and `forget_transaction` removes the merge from undo history. Mutation runs confirmed both calls are load-bearing: without either, undo removed the absorbed text. A clean page moves to the incoming text and is marked saved through `did_save`; a dirty page stays dirty and autosaves on top of the new base.

In-flight semantics. While a save is in flight, `absorb` only records a hint and a stale flag. After the acknowledgment, the page reads the store's heads: a single head equal to the saved version means the incoming version was already included, and nothing happens. Any other single head descends from the saved version, because every save and import merges to one head, so it merges in. The hint text is used when it is that head; otherwise its body is read. The same reconciliation runs whenever an acknowledgment shows that the head is not this save's result, which covers unchanged saves to a page that moved on. Three further guards keep a late or reordered call from applying text older than the saved version. A merge job remembers the base it started from and restarts through `absorb` if a save landed meanwhile. Saves requested during a merge queue until it applies. A newer merge job replaces an older one. Mutation runs showed these guards keep the stale-save test passing even with deferral disabled, so the deferral avoids wasted work while the base check and the store read carry correctness. This differs from `rt`, which drops an upsert during a save and relies on ordered IPC. Quire's storage replies arrive on independent channels, so it checks against storage instead.

`StoredSource::rebased` gives the buffer a new adapter after each merge, with the merged stored text as the original and the post-merge snapshot as the baseline. Exact original endings therefore survive even when local edits remain; the fallback to the buffer's line ending was not needed. Requests capture the adapter with their snapshot, so frozen requests and retries keep rendering through the lineage they were taken from. Copy Markdown uses `LoadedPage::current_source`.

Recovery drafts at open. A draft opens editable and settles when its base revision is in storage, live, and on the same page, and the page's single live head is still at the draft's path. Settling means saving at once when editing is enabled; the store merges, and the merged text replaces the draft in the buffer. A draft based on the current head is saved too, matching `rt`'s settle-all. Drafts with a base the store does not have, at a moved path, on a deleted page, or on a page with competing heads open read-only with "recovered unsaved changes kept for review" and stay untouched. Quire drafts always record their base and nothing imports `rt` recovery rows, so no draft with `base_known` = 0 exists here; an unresolvable base stands in for it and is never merged. Deviation: `rt` settles every draft when the notebook opens, while the native app settles a draft when its page opens, because pages load on demand through `NotebookSession`. A draft for a page that is never opened stays recorded, unmerged.

Deleted elsewhere. A changed save from a live base merges with the tombstone and revives the page through NR09.4's "an edit outlives a deletion". An unchanged save leaves the tombstone as the head. The post-save reconciliation then reports "This page was deleted elsewhere. Your text stays open here; edit and save it to restore the page." The buffer is untouched. The NR11.3 2 s steady-typing ceiling (`saveLatest`) landed here because NR11.4 verifies it. The ceiling resets when a save is requested, as `rt` resets `saveBy`, and not when a queued save starts. A first version that reset it at start produced a zero-delay save loop during busy queues, which the steady-typing test caught.

Tests. Nine new `page_persistence` tests: merging while typing, the stale save merged while typing continues (with an absorb deferred during that save), kept-both lines with the overlap warning, saves during steady typing, absorb on a clean CRLF page (exact source, base update, ignored repeat), absorb on a dirty page (anchor shift, Zed dirty state, undo skips the merge), revive after deletion, an unchanged save that stays deleted, and two editors with separate workers and buffers saving one page at once that end with one head and identical text. Two `notebook_session` tests cover a stale draft merging when editing starts and drafts that stay for review (an unknown base, a moved path). The Vim force-write test now asserts a merge and a closed window. The old stale-heads test asserted the refusal and was replaced. Mutations disabling `forget_transaction`, `finalize_last_transaction`, the 2 s ceiling, settling, the save-merge display, or the deleted notice each fail their test. Gates: app 167 tests, plus core and store suites, pass with `--features quire/test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders`, repeated five times for the persistence and session suites. App and core/store Clippy with `-D warnings` pass, as do `cargo fmt --check` and the reference vitest suite (79 tests, run with the main checkout's vitest because this worktree has no `node_modules`).

Remaining: (1) the native-plus-reference-replica test (a native editor and a frozen TypeScript replica editing one page at the same moment) needs a hook into `bun run test:sync-schedules` that drives an editor; NR09.4's mixed schedules already cover replica-level stale-save merging. (2) Bulk tag edits (NR17.2) are the first production caller of `absorb`; sync imports must also call it with the stored text after their saves. Notebook session import handling has nothing to route until synchronization runs in the app. (3) Picking up another process's writes to the same database without such a notification, for example on window activation, is not implemented. (4) Folder notebooks are unaffected because the native app opens only Quire notebooks. Found during the merge with NR16 and NR14: two processes editing one page share its single recovery slot, so the later of two concurrent recordings is refused ("Recovery changed while recording this snapshot") and that editor stops saving until retried. Its text stays in its buffer. One process never hits this because every view of a page shares one buffer. Decided in NR33: one session per notebook, enforced by an operating-system notebook lock and a single Quire per data folder, a two-way door; see NR33. The two-editor test now records sequentially and still covers the stale-save merge across two connections.
Status: in_progress


### NR12: Serve indexed queries with a live overlay

What to do: Add incremental derived tables and bounded search/page/backlink/tag/task/template/daily queries. Merge unsaved analysis without counting old and new versions twice; expose generation-aware paging.
Constraints: Disk reads, parsing, hashing, and database waits stay off the UI thread. Pin dirty buffers; bound caches and reject stale results after notebook/query changes.
Write set: `quire_store` index/query modules, live-overlay coordinator, performance fixtures.
Dependencies: NR10, NR11.
Verify: Differential results against the reference for committed and unsaved edits; deterministic invalidation/race tests; SQL plan inspection and 1x/5x work-count checks. Search/backlinks do not enumerate all page bodies.
Proof notes: NR12.1 through NR12.4 deliver metadata, the query index, search, and the live overlay. Remaining before done: NR10.8 block parity (the index inherits native block boundaries), and the 1x and 5x work-count checks with `tools/synthetic-notebook.ts`, which belong with NR34 budgets. This completes the unresolved intent of source storage task 6 once those land.
Status: in_progress

#### NR12.1: Index lossless revision metadata atomically

What to do: Extract titles from lossless stored path/text, add rebuildable revision metadata with a versioned schema upgrade, and expose bounded metadata reads without loading revision bodies. Maintain rows in the same transactions as local saves, imports, and coalescing.
Constraints: Preserve raw UTF-16 strings, unknown wire fields, conflicts, and tombstones. Keep existing schemas immutable. Upgrade existing stores atomically with bounded body-at-a-time backfill of current heads; do not read unrelated historical bodies during a schema-3 upgrade. Historical metadata remains an optional cache and history reads stay on demand. Rollback must preserve authoritative data. Metadata corruption cannot manufacture an empty title or silently change the selected revision. Recovery/live overlays and indexed date selection remain explicit parent work.
Write set: core lossless title/JSON string boundary, frozen fixtures, storage schema/migration/index/query/worker APIs, focused tests and docs.
Verify: Differential UTF-16 titles, valid native fast path, source preservation, save/import/coalescing consistency, rollback on derived-write failure, old-store upgrade and offline rollback, conflict/tombstone ordering, bounded pages, corrupted-body nonreads, SQL query plans, worker integration, and broad native/storage checks.
Proof notes: Titles are extracted at a lossless UTF-16 boundary (`crates/quire_core/tests/lossless_titles.rs` against `tests/fixtures/markdown/lossless-title-vectors.json` from `tools/lossless-title-vectors.ts`). Schema 4 adds `revision_metadata`, maintained inside save, import, and coalescing transactions (`crates/quire_store/src/notebook/metadata.rs`). `crates/quire_store/tests/metadata.rs` covers body-free head reads with zeroed bodies, editor line-break titles without source rewrites, malformed numeric head columns, coalescing/conflict/tombstone ordering, raw UTF-16 import with rollback on failure, row and byte page limits with an oversized row progressing alone, corruption requiring rebuild, and `StorageClient::page_metadata`. Migration unit tests cover schema-3 upgrade with offline rollback, current-head-only backfill, failed-backfill retry, and corrupt-head abort; query-plan tests reject full `revisions` scans. Passing on 2026-10-30: `CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo cargo clippy -p quire_core -p quire_store --locked --all-targets --no-deps -- -D warnings`, `CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store --locked --features quire/test-support`, and `bun run test:reference` (57 tests). `cargo clippy -p quire` could not run on this host because the Metal Toolchain is missing (`xcodebuild -downloadComponent MetalToolchain`).
Status: done

#### NR12.2: Analyze stored heads into a rebuildable query index

What to do: Analyze each current head with the same native block, reference, and tag code the editor uses, by building a detached Zed `BufferSnapshot` on the background executor. Store plain rows in a separate `query-index.sqlite` attached to the store connection, keyed by immutable revision ID and analyzer version. Serve block-identifier resolution, page lookup by name/title/date, backlinks, mentions, tagged blocks, and the tag catalogue with the reference's ordering and ambiguity rules. Report how many heads are not yet analyzed.
Constraints: The index is a cache. It is never part of backups, rollback, or the authoritative schema; a missing, damaged, foreign-version, or analyzer-version-mismatched index is discarded and rebuilt without touching revisions. Queries join through current heads so rows for superseded revisions never surface; bounded garbage collection removes them. Parsing and SQLite stay off the UI thread. Analysis uses editor-normalized text, exactly as the reference analyzes `editorText`. Daily-date link ownership follows the configured daily layout; a layout change re-derives date keys without reparsing pages.
Write set: `crates/quire/src/page_index*`, `crates/quire_store/src/index*`, worker APIs, frozen query fixtures from the pinned reference store, tests and README.
Verify: Block/reference/tag rows from detached snapshots match the existing frozen vectors. Query results match a frozen reference oracle over a fixture notebook covering duplicate titles, name-versus-title precedence, date links, block references with `#^`, ambiguous and missing IDs, tag case/NFC folding, conflicts, and tombstones. Index deletion, corruption, and version bumps rebuild; superseded revisions never appear; query plans avoid full scans of revision bodies.
Proof notes: `quire_store::index` keeps `query-index.sqlite` on its own worker thread (the storage queue is now generic over its state), with the notebook attached read-only through a `mode=ro` URI. A catalogue of selected live heads is built from `revision_metadata` validated by checksum, so names, titles, and dates are complete before any body is parsed; `PRAGMA nb.data_version` skips refresh work when nothing changed. `crates/quire/src/page_index.rs` analyzes text through `Buffer::build_snapshot` on the background executor with the editor's `markdown_blocks::blocks` and `analyze_inline`, and `crates/quire/src/query_index.rs` drives pending, analyze, and put in batches of 16, showing progress and errors in the stored window. Queries port the reference `findPage`, `dailyPage`, `resolve`, `backlinks`, `mentions`, `taggedBlocks`, and `tagNames`, frozen at rt 5ea5c22310ff in `tests/reference/index/page-analysis.ts`, with ICU root collation for `localeCompare` order. Rationale for a separate file: derived, parser-versioned data should not need authoritative schema migrations, checksummed rollback, or backup space, and it can be deleted at any time.

Evidence: `crates/quire_store/tests/index.rs` covers catalogue tracking of later saves, dropping superseded analysis, byte-identical notebook files after indexing, name-before-title-before-date resolution with shared titles and daily-folder preference, backlinks through names, titles, `.md` targets, embeds, owned date links, scoped and ambiguous block IDs, mentions without a page, tag folding and labels, and rebuilds after analyzer bumps and file corruption. A unit test checks every key lookup's query plan uses its index with no scan. `page_index` matches blocks, links, tags, and front tags on all 1,254 frozen vectors (NR10.8 closed the 175 block divergences). `query_index` proves end-to-end indexing and re-indexing after a save in a headless app. Gates passing on 2026-10-30: store and app Clippy with `-D warnings` (app Clippy with `--features test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` because this host lacks the Metal Toolchain), `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store --locked --features quire/test-support`, and `bun run test:reference`.

Remaining: a work-count check at 1x and 5x notebook size (catalogue refresh is one anti-join over heads per notebook change, and `tag_names` reads every tagged row); notebook daily-layout settings reach the app with NR16.
Status: in_progress

#### NR12.3: Serve block full-text search from the index

What to do: Add an FTS5 index over analyzed blocks and page titles matching the reference search: terms from `[\p{L}\p{N}_-]+`, prefix AND matching, bm25 ranking, 24-token snippets, 40 results, and a title row per page.
Constraints: Maintain the FTS table in the same index transaction as its block rows. Short and Unicode queries must behave as in the reference; add trigram support only if it preserves those results.
Write set: `crates/quire_store/src/index*`, worker API, frozen search fixtures, tests.
Verify: Differential hits, order, and snippets against the reference `SearchIndex` over the fixture notebook; Unicode, single-character, hyphenated, and empty queries; query-plan and work-count checks at 1x and 5x notebook size.
Proof notes: `QueryIndex::search` and `IndexClient::search` use an FTS5 external-content table over `search_rows` (index format 2), filled in the same transaction as the block rows and removed through the page cascade and a delete trigger. `tests/reference/index/search-index.ts` freezes the unchanged reference class from rt 5ea5c22310ff, and `tools/search-vectors.ts` runs it under Bun's `node:sqlite` (SQLite 3.54.0) over 60 seeded pages with Arabic, Urdu, CJK, emoji, diacritics, hyphens, underscores, digits, frontmatter titles, and block IDs, recording hits and bm25 scores for 33 queries (678 hits, several at the 40-hit limit). `query_index::search_tests` indexes the same pages through the production analyzer and compares every query, treating equal-score runs as multisets and checking only the size of a tie run cut by the limit. `index::tests::superseded_revisions_leave_search_through_the_cascade` re-saves a page, confirms old text is unsearchable, and runs the FTS5 integrity check. The raw-text CRLF snippet difference is deliberate and documented in README. Gates as for NR12.2 pass. Trigram search was not added: prefix-AND over `unicode61` already reproduces the reference, including single-character and two-letter prefixes. Remaining for the parent: 1x and 5x work-count checks.
Status: done

#### NR12.4: Overlay unsaved buffers on indexed queries

What to do: Let open dirty buffers replace their page's committed contribution in every NR12.2/NR12.3 query, using the buffer's current native analysis. Expose a query generation that changes when committed heads, the index, or any overlay changes, so callers can discard stale pages of results.
Constraints: Never count a page's committed and unsaved versions together. Pin overlay snapshots while a query runs; bound overlay memory to open buffers. Reject results computed against an older generation.
Write set: live-overlay coordinator in `crates/quire`, query merge logic, tests.
Verify: Differential results against the reference store with unsaved edits applied; deterministic invalidation and race tests (save during query, close dirty buffer, external import).
Proof notes: `crates/quire_store/src/index/overlay.rs` keeps overlays in TEMP tables and serves lookups through `live_pages`, `live_blocks`, `live_links`, and `live_tagged` views. A page's committed rows drop out of `live_pages`, and the joins then drop its blocks, links, and tags, so old and new versions never count together. Every open page is overlaid, not only dirty ones, which removes the save-to-analysis gap; `release_overlay` refreshes the catalogue and waits until the page's committed head is analyzed. `IndexSync::track` subscribes to each open page's buffer, analyzes the live text 150 ms after the last edit through the same detached analyzer, holds pages weakly, and releases on entity release; the stored window tracks its open pages. `QueryIndex::generation` moves on catalogue changes, stored analysis, and overlay changes, and `IndexClient::with_generation` returns a result and its generation from one worker step. Because the worker is serialized, a save cannot interleave with a query. Search remains committed-only, matching the reference `SearchIndex`, which reads saved files. Evidence: `overlays_shadow_committed_rows_and_release_after_the_final_analysis` (replacement without double counting, idempotent replacement, unsaved new pages found by title and linking like saved ones, release of never-saved pages, release waiting for the final save's analysis) and `overlay_tests::unsaved_edits_reach_queries_and_closing_falls_back_to_saved_rows` (live buffer edits reach backlinks, generation advances, and closing a dirty, unsaved page returns to committed rows); query plans through the live views still use indexes with no scans. Task, template, and daily-date catalogues reuse this mechanism once NR18, NR20, and NR16 port their analyzers.
Fix, closed pages saved elsewhere: a page that is not open has no overlay, so a save through storage (ticking a linked task, a bulk tag edit, a sync, a changed folder file) used to drop the page's analyzed revision as soon as the catalogue saw the new head, and every query read the page without rows until the head was analyzed. Linked tasks emptied and the references reappeared for a moment after every tick, and `linked_tasks_filter_above_references_tick_and_open` failed about 1 run in 10 because its "Open to empty" wait matched that gap. Now `QueryIndex::retire` keeps a page's last analyzed revision while its new head waits, `live_pages` reads it in place of the head, and analyzing the head drops it in the same transaction, so a page's rows swap atomically; intermediate unanalyzed saves and deletions still leave at once, `put` refuses the kept revision, relocation and folder-move candidates still read such a page in full, and `modified_times` reports the head. Every view reading `live_*` (linked tasks, linked references, tags and tag pages, the task board, search) gains the same guarantee. Evidence: `quire_store/tests/index.rs::a_saved_page_keeps_its_analyzed_rows_until_its_new_save_is_analyzed` (fails before the fix with an empty linked task list), the changed-file check in `folder_index.rs`, and the app test now waiting for the ticked task to be listed as done (60 of 60 runs alone).
Status: done

### NR13: Open existing Markdown folder notebooks

What to do: Add the folder adapter with ordinary files, watched external edits, revision checks, recoverable saves, history, and shared query indexing.
Constraints: Opening does not convert a folder. Preserve hidden metadata, symlink boundaries, case handling, and local/foreign drafts. Coordinate ownership with legacy processes rather than assuming app-data isolation suffices.
Write set: `quire_store` folder adapter and watcher, folder integration fixtures.
Dependencies: NR11, NR12.
Verify: Existing folder-contract fixtures plus native open/edit/save/restart; external edits/deletes, read-only files, path aliases, renamed pages, and stale saves preserve both versions when required.
Proof notes: Six slices landed. (1) `quire_store/src/folder_notebook.rs` (with `files.rs`, `history.rs`) is a store-shaped adapter on the existing `Worker`: `heads`, `checkout`, `get`, `draft`, `put_draft`, `save`, `save_many_if_heads`, plus `refresh`, `rescan`, `discard_draft`, and history. Page identity is `page_id_for_path(path)` and a page's single head is the SHA-256 of its text, as in `rt` `vault.ts`. The walk skips dot entries, `node_modules`/`dist`/`out`, and symbolic links; reads refuse invalid UTF-8 and notes over 10 MB and keep BOMs and CRLF. Saves port `saveDocument`: record a draft, compare the disk revision with the edit's base, stage a synced `.quire-*.tmp` beside the note with the note's permissions, recheck, add the replaced text to history (rt's retention and zstd content store), then rename, or `persist_noclobber` for a new note, and sync the folder. A mismatch returns a typed `FolderConflict` carrying the disk version. (2) `quire_store/src/index/folder.rs`: `QueryIndex::open_folder` runs the same index without an attached database; it walks the folder when invalidated, rereads only files whose size, mtime, or ctime changed (stamps persist in `folder_files`), and keys rows by revision and page. (3) `crates/quire/src/notebook_backend.rs`: `NotebookBackend { Store, Folder }` gives sessions, `PagePersistence`, `page_writes`, tag and task edits, and navigation the same page operations; existing tests only gained an import. (4) `PagePersistence` gains a conflict state: a refused save, or an outside change while the buffer has unsaved edits, stops saving (autosave and explicit), keeps recording recovery, and shows rt's messages; clean pages reload through the existing merge path as one non-undo edit. `use_disk` shows the file and discards the draft; a removed file becomes read-only until it reappears. `FolderRecovery` opens a draft made from the current file (or a new note's draft) and saves it at once, and opens any other draft in conflict. (5) The window: `--notebook-folder <folder> [--page <path>]`, state under `paths::data_dir()/folder-notebooks/<hash>`, Zed's `fs::RealFs::watch` feeding `NotebookSession::folder_changed` and index invalidation, and a banner with Save my version as a copy (`quire_core::markdown::copy_with_new_ids`, a port of `copyWithNewIds`), Use disk version (after a confirmation prompt), and Compare versions. (6) Fixes from an adversarial review, each with a regression test: saves refuse while another session's draft differs, so a bulk edit can no longer settle away a crash draft; `refresh` tracks what it reported separately from reads; a change queued during a refused save no longer replaces the refusal's newer disk text; a file restored to the edits' base ends the conflict.

Deviations from `rt`, chosen deliberately. State lives in application data, not `.quire/devices/<device>` inside the folder, so opening never writes in the notebook (the reference app's own records are read since the compatibility slices below). A recovered draft whose base is the current file saves at once instead of opening as a conflict, since the revision check still guards the write. A save whose text already equals the file succeeds without writing. Read-only notes are refused instead of replaced by rename. Use disk version discards the draft unconditionally after the user confirms, where rt clears it only if unchanged.

Compatibility with folders `rt` already uses, four further slices (source: `rt` 0.9.57 `5ea5c22`, `src/desktop/vault.ts`, `notebook-storage.ts`, `history-store.ts`, `src/core/daily-layout.ts`, `src/ui/FileActions.tsx`, `tests/vault.test.ts`). (7) Settings, commit `81004a3b3743`: `folder_notebook/settings.rs` reads `.quire/notebook.json` (an ordinary file in an ordinary `.quire` folder, at most 64 KB, UTF-8) and the window's `read_stored_settings`/`save_stored_settings` in `stored/daily.rs` route the daily layout through it for folder notebooks. Saving writes rt's exact `writeNotebookSettings` bytes to a temporary file in `.quire`, syncs it, rechecks that the file still has the revision Quire read, renames it into place, and syncs the folder; a file changed since reading is refused ("changed in another application"), and `.quire` is created only on that explicit save. Unknown versions and invalid layouts stay read-only through the existing `quire_core::daily::read_settings`. (8) Trash, commit `bd1d2426c5d3`: `folder_notebook/deletion.rs` ports `VaultFiles.remove` as `delete_if_heads`: each chosen note must still hold its base (`HeadsChanged` otherwise), a folder deletion refuses with `Appeared` when an unchosen note sits inside, and the folder (with its other files) or each note goes to the injected `Trash`. The app passes Zed's `fs::RealFs::trash`; without a trash the deletion is refused (`NO_TRASH`), and tests pass a stand-in that moves items to a temporary bin, so no test fills the real Trash. A note holding another session's crash draft refuses the deletion; text typed into an open page that had not reached its file is saved first, so it goes to the Trash with the note (both versions also join history). The dialog says "The files go to the system Trash, where you can restore them." and counts other files in a folder, as `FileActions.tsx` does. (9) Journaled rename, commit `36520bd4531e`: `folder_notebook/journal.rs` replaces the old unjournaled `save_many_if_heads`. Every multi-note change, and any change whose path belongs to another page (a move), checks every note, writes a synced journal (`transactions/<hash>.json` in the state folder, rt's `FileEdit` shape: `path`, `text`, `expectedRevision`, `remove`, `retirement`, `historyFrom`), creates the moved note's new file with `persist_noclobber`, rewrites the linking notes, copies the old path's history to the new path, and archives the old file under `retired/<id>.md` in the state folder, as `commitEdits`/`applyJournalEdit` do. A target name taken by any file or folder ignoring case is refused before the journal is written. Opening resumes every journal (rt's `resumeTransactions`): steps are idempotent, a finished journal is removed with a notice, and a note changed meanwhile stops recovery with a notice naming the journal, which stays with every version. `file_actions::plan_rename` now plans folder renames through the same `quire_core::relocate` path; because a folder page's identity follows its path, `Renamed` carries `previous`, and the window's `page_moved` retires the old open page and points tabs, favorites, recents, the side pane, and history at the new identity (`Workspace::rename_page`, `History::rename_page`). (10) Reference drafts and history, commit `1ae19d239665`: `folder_notebook/reference.rs` reads `.quire/devices/<device>/durability.sqlite` (`pending` and `history`/`contents`, or the older `revisions`), `.quire/devices/<device>/recovery/*.json`, and `.quire/history/<device>.sqlite`. Each database and its `-wal` are copied into a temporary folder in the state folder, integrity-checked, and read there, so nothing in the notebook is opened for writing. A source whose size and times are unchanged is skipped; each record is imported once (`reference_imports`). A draft whose text the file already holds is skipped; otherwise it becomes the page's recovery draft with base `expected_revision`, so the existing `FolderRecovery` rules apply: it saves when its base is the current file and otherwise opens in conflict. A page that already holds Quire's own unsaved work keeps it, and the reference draft becomes `Recovered drafts/Draft <device> <key>.md` with fresh block IDs and a "Recovered draft: <title>" front matter title, as rt's `importCopiedData` does for another computer's drafts; a later draft from the same computer replaces an import nobody has edited here. History rows keep their own times and follow rt's retention. The window lists recovered pages with Open buttons and other notices (finished or stuck journals, unreadable data) with Dismiss.

The `.quire` decision. Quire reads everything rt keeps in `.quire` and writes only `notebook.json`, on an explicit layout save; its drafts, history, journals, archive, index, and workspace stay in application data. Keeping state in application data is a two-way door: it can move into the notebook later without breaking either app. Writing Quire's own records into `.quire/devices/<quire-device>` would be a one-way door: rt imports every other device folder it finds, so Quire's schema would become a cross-app format the moment one notebook carried it to another computer, and today rt would fail to read Quire's `drafts` table and warn on every open. `notebook.json` is different because rt defines it as user-visible notebook settings in one small versioned format both apps already parse. When both apps open the same folder, neither writes the other's state: rt ignores Quire's application data, Quire copies rt's databases before reading them, every note write in both apps checks the disk revision first, and a settings save checks the revision Quire read. Not coordinated: a draft rt is still typing can be settled by Quire when its base is current (the file then changes under rt, which keeps rt's text in its recovery and shows its own conflict), and rt's own journals in `.quire/devices/<device>/transactions` are not resumed by Quire.

Further deviations. rt archives a moved note's original in its device folder inside the notebook; Quire archives it in application data. rt resumes a journal only when it has at least two edits; Quire resumes any. A folder deletion saves typing that had not reached the file before trashing, where rt requires the page to be flushed first. Reference drafts are imported into the page's own recovery slot when free, where rt turns every copied draft into a separate conflict page; rt's own device folder on this computer is imported like any other.

Tests added: `folder_notebook::rt_tests` (14: rt-format settings read, stale and invalid writes refused, `.quire` created only on save, linked `.quire` refused; no trash refuses; the two `vault.test.ts` deletion cases, a page changed since chosen and a folder with `photo.png` refusing `Late.md`; crash drafts refusing deletion; unsaved typing going to the Trash (regression for the refusal found in the GPUI test); a rename with link rewrite, archive, and history; taken names in either case; interruption before each of the three steps finishing on reopen and not replaying; a note changed before recovery keeping the journal; rt drafts in pending and legacy JSON, a page with Quire work getting a separate note, zstd and raw history with original times, nothing in the folder changed byte for byte with the rt database held open in WAL mode, single import, newer same-device drafts replacing; unreadable rt data reported and left alone); `quire_core/tests/workspace.rs::a_page_whose_identity_changed_keeps_its_tabs_favorites_and_history`; `stored::navigation::folder_tests` (rt layout read and saved, a layout changed elsewhere refused, newer settings read-only; page deletion to the Trash with typing kept and tabs, favorites, recents cleaned; folder deletion refusing an appeared page and a stale page, then moving the whole folder; rename parity with the Quire-notebook fixture text, archive, empty journal folder, tabs and favorites following, later saves at the new path; taken names and a file the index has not seen refused; a crashed journal in rt's shape finishing on open with a notice; rt drafts listed, the current-base draft saved, the other opening in conflict without overwriting). Commands: both Clippy gates with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, the `tests/reference` Vitest run (92 tests), and the full `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store --locked --features quire/test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` run twice (522 tests each).

Tests: `folder_notebook::tests` (15: listing and exact reads, invalid UTF-8 and link escapes, opening writes nothing inside, atomic replace with permissions and history, stale-base refusal keeping both versions, deleted notes never recreated, exclusive creation including a race, a change between staging and rename, a crash between temporary write and rename, draft ordering, refresh and rescan, case variants, checked multi-page saves, read-only notes, crash drafts surviving bulk edits, reads not hiding changes); `quire_store/tests/folder_index.rs` (the same four pages through the store and the folder answer `pages`, `find_page`, `backlinks`, and `search` identically; edits and deletes follow invalidation; reopening reanalyzes nothing); `page_persistence::folder_tests` (4 GPUI tests: saves, refusal, no autosave or explicit save during a conflict, recording continues, use disk; clean reload, dirty conflict, equal-text resolution, removal; recovered drafts settling or opening in conflict; deferred changes, revert to base, reappearing files); `stored::navigation::folder_tests` (the window indexes a folder, reloads an open page, reindexes a closed page, conflicts without autosave overwriting, saves a copy and shows the disk version; a real `RealFs` watcher reloads an open page). Commands: both Clippy gates with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, the full `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store` gate twice, and the `tests/reference` Vitest run (90 tests) pass.

Journal throughput. Changes across notes (rename, Move, folder rename, tag and task bulk edits, Markdown import) wrote each note through `save`, which on macOS cost four `F_FULLFSYNC` drive-cache flushes per new note and five per replaced note: the recovery draft's SQLite commit (`fullfsync` with WAL `FULL`), the staged file's `sync_all` (Rust maps it to `F_FULLFSYNC` on Apple targets), the folder's `sync_all`, the draft-settling commit, and a history commit for a replaced note. Checking heads also relisted the whole notebook for every page without a file, so a 1,000-note import walked the folder 1,000 times. `crates/quire_store/examples/journal_bench.rs` (`cargo run --release -p quire_store --example journal_bench -- 1000`) measured on this Mac, before: 1,000-note import 37.4 s (37 ms per note) and one change editing those 1,000 notes 70.8 s (71 ms per note); 200 notes took 5.5 s and 17.7 s. Single primitives measured 5 to 20 ms per file `F_FULLFSYNC`, 5 to 9 ms per folder `F_FULLFSYNC`, 5 to 16 ms per SQLite commit, and 0.6 to 7.7 ms per plain `fsync`. The journal now applies a change in phases: check the steps against the disk in order (no listing per page; a step that fails its check stops the change after the steps before it, as before), write every new text to a temporary file beside its note with plain `fsync`, one `F_FULLFSYNC` per drive, record every replaced, archived, or moved version in one SQLite transaction, rename and archive in step order with each note's revision checked again, plain `fsync` each touched folder once, one `F_FULLFSYNC` per drive, then settle drafts in one transaction and remove the journal. Durability is unchanged: the journal is still fully synced before any note is touched; no note is replaced before its new text is on stable storage; the replaced text is in committed history before its file is renamed; the journal is removed only after every rename is durable. The man page for `fcntl` says `F_FULLFSYNC` asks the drive to flush all buffered data, so one flush per drive covers every file already `fsync`ed to it; other platforms keep `fsync` per file, which already reaches stable storage. A change no longer writes per-note recovery drafts, since the synced journal holds every planned text. After, the same benchmark ran six times under a load average of 28 to 38 with antivirus real-time scanning: import 0.43 to 4.4 s and the 1,000-note edit 1.45 to 7.0 s. A phase trace showed the remainder is per-file system calls (create, write, `fsync`, reread, rename) at 0.4 to 7 ms per note depending on machine load, with each barrier under 30 ms; that per-file work is the floor without weakening the per-note revision check. Tests: `rt_tests::a_rename_interrupted_at_each_batch_boundary_finishes_once_with_history_kept_once` interrupts once every text is staged, after history is committed (step 0), and after every rename (the step count, before folders are synced), checks no staged file is left beside the notes, that reopening finishes the rename once, and that the replaced text is in history once (fails with plain `history::add`); `rt_tests::a_change_across_notes_makes_the_same_number_of_full_syncs_for_any_note_count` (macOS) counts full syncs for a 4-note and a 60-note import and edit and requires equal counts, at most 6 and 4. `rt_tests::recovery_applies_the_steps_before_one_that_changed_and_stops_there` keeps that order on recovery. The existing journal interruption, move, rename, stuck-change (store and window), and import tests pass unchanged.

Landed under NR25: the history browser and restore (NR25.4, imported rt history included), Move and folder rename with the link-change preview (NR25.3), and Retry or Set aside for a stuck journal (NR25.5).

Batch E on 2026-10-31 (rt 0.9.57 `5ea5c22310ff`, `src/desktop/vault.ts` `importCopiedData`, `commitFiles`, `resumeFiles`, and `src/desktop/main.ts`'s 30 s `rescanTimer`). (1) `6d1d48218e54`: `folder_notebook::reference` reads each source's `foreign_drafts` table. A pending row that still equals a recorded `recovered_path` and `recovered_text` is imported as the original computer's draft under that draft's own import key, so the third computer's database and rt's recovered copy produce one draft; provenance rows whose recovered copy rt has since saved or discarded settle their key in one transaction before any source's drafts are read, so the order of device folders cannot let the original through. `rt_tests::a_draft_the_reference_app_recovered_from_another_computer_is_imported_once` puts the third computer first in name order and fails without the change (two drafts, plus a `Recovered drafts/` copy). (2) `a7d4c3857e6f`: on open, every `*.json` in `.quire/devices/*/transactions` is copied once (key `journal:<sha256>` in `reference_imports`) into Quire's journals, where `resume`, `stuck_changes`, Retry, and Set aside already apply; rt's UUID `retirement` names are accepted beside Quire's 32-hex names. Deviation: rt copies only other devices' journals into its own data folder; Quire has no rt device of its own, so it takes every device's, and rt's files stay untouched. Tests: `a_change_the_reference_app_left_unfinished_finishes_here_once` (rt's exact `commitFiles` JSON with `before` and a UUID archive, finished with history kept, rt's journal unchanged, not taken in twice) and `a_change_the_reference_app_left_stuck_is_listed_and_set_aside_once`. (3) `807693d775e8`: `FolderNotebook::open_deferring_history`, used by the worker, imports drafts at open and queues each changed source's copied database; `import_reference_history(limit)` takes 250 versions per request (22 ms per step of short notes in a debug build), thinning each path once per step instead of once per row, and marks the source's fingerprint only when it is complete, so an interrupted import resumes on the next open without repeating versions. The window asks for steps after the first page's checkout, so the first page never waits. `open` still finishes synchronously for tests and migration. Tests: `a_long_reference_history_is_taken_in_after_opening_in_bounded_steps` (600 versions, three steps, resume after drop) and `folder_tests::reference_history_is_taken_in_after_the_first_page_loads`. (4) Same commit: `StoredWindow::rescan_folder_backstop` rereads the folder through the existing `folder_changed(None)` every 30 s, as rt does, and when the window becomes active (`a_change_the_watcher_missed_is_reread_periodically_and_on_activating_the_window`, which checks activation without advancing the clock). (5) `e50613192708`: a close while a conflicted page's typing is unrecorded asks with `lifecycle::unrecorded_conflict(path)`, naming the note and the banner, instead of "storage reported an error"; real storage errors keep that text (`lifecycle_tests::closing_during_a_conflict_with_unrecorded_typing_names_the_conflict`). Attachments in folders landed under NR21 and NR23.

A progress line while reference history is taken in landed on 2026-10-31 (batch H, `ab809f5f7dd6`): each queued reference source counts its saved versions when queued (`history_count`), `FolderClient::import_reference_history` reports how many remain after each step of 250, and the folder banner reads "Taking in the reference app's saved history: N of M versions…" until all is in; `folder_tests::reference_history_is_taken_in_after_the_first_page_loads` checks the three lines for 600 versions (0, 250, and 500 of 600) and that the line clears. The banner shares the notices area, so it is hidden while a conflict banner shows.

Batch L (2026-10-31, rt `src/desktop/legacy-names.ts` `adoptLegacyMetadata`). Opening a folder notebook renames its `.threadnote` folder to `.quire` when no `.quire` exists, before the reference data is read, and otherwise keeps both and adds a notice naming the Threadnote computer to update; a failed rename is a notice too and changes nothing (`folder_notebook::tests::a_threadnote_metadata_folder_becomes_quire_on_first_open`, `a_threadnote_folder_beside_quire_is_kept_and_named_in_a_notice`). Choosing a folder that holds `quire-notebook.json` opens it as a Quire notebook (NR33).

Batch M (2026-10-31, `e31a55aa7276`). The change journal gains a folder step (`folderTo` on a step whose `path` is the folder; Quire-only, so the reference app never reads it): applying a journal runs its leading folder steps first, each idempotent (done when the folder is already at its target; a folder at both places or at neither stops the change for review), then plans the note steps. `move_folder_if_heads` and `move_noteless_folder` live in `folder_notebook/folder_move.rs` and share one check of the folder move (valid paths, not inside itself, the target free in any letter case). A moved note's history is copied from its old path even though no file remains there. Details and tests in NR16 batch M.

Remaining: a native folder picker (S02); ownership coordination beyond revision checks with a concurrently running reference app.
Status: in_progress

Gate B: Both backends must run the daily-page workflow. Format compatibility, recovery, unsaved-query visibility, and bounded startup must pass before replacing more screens.

## Notebook features

### NR14: Port complete outline editing

What to do: Implement Enter, indent/outdent, block movement/join, multi-line paste, numbered lists, folding, zoom, and corresponding Vim overrides. Backspace or Delete on an empty bullet with blocks after it, including a page's first bullet, removes it and lifts its children; a lone empty bullet stays (`957da33d4766`). On a task line, Home, `0`, and `^` reach the text, then the checkbox, and arrowing left from the checkbox reaches the line above (`35e673677291`). List conversion accepts an explicit bullet or numbered style.
Constraints: Preserve descendants, IDs, caret placement, selection, and shared undo; retain ordinary paragraph behavior.
Write set: notebook editor commands and structural-edit tests.
Dependencies: NR11, NR10.
Verify: Port `block-commands`, `outline-caret`, `list-numbers`, `block-wrapping`, and relevant editor scenarios to native input tests in Write/Source/embeds, with Vim on/off.
Proof notes: Write view outline editing landed in `crates/quire/src/outline.rs` (actions, key bindings, editor glue), `outline/model.rs` (pure plans over the native block model), `outline/lists.rs` (numbering), and their tests. The Write projection adds a `QuireWrite` key context, so bindings apply only there and every command that does not apply calls `propagate`, falling through to Zed's next binding (ordinary Newline, Tab, Backspace, Paste, Vim `o`/`J`/`>`). Commands read the page through `current_blocks`, apply one `Editor::transact` bracketed by `finalize_last_transaction` (one undo step that restores selections), and renumber the numbered runs their edits touched. Source edits use a new vendored `ViewEditIntent::Structural`, which the Write edit filter leaves unchanged. Vim overrides use two vendored hooks, `vim::begin_host_insert` and `vim::record_host_command`, so `o`/`O` enter recorded Insert mode and `J`, `>>`, `<<` are dot-repeatable. Folding uses typed Zed folds (`OutlineFold`) over `content_to..subtree_to`; fold commands never remove Write's hidden-syntax folds. The task-line Home rule lives in Write's caret clamp, which now remembers each caret's previous head: a jump from the text start or checkbox lands on the checkbox, from further along on the text, and single steps through the visible checkbox gap are kept; Zed's Home, Vim `0`/`^`, and arrows all follow it. Deviations from `rt`: literal detection uses tree-sitter node kinds (`fenced_code_block`, `code_span`, `pipe_table`, `html_block`, headings, rules, link definitions); Vim undoes Enter with the rest of its Insert session, as Vim does, while non-Vim Enter is its own undo step; tree-sitter does not let `2.` interrupt a paragraph, so rt's nested `   2.` renumber fixture uses a list that starts at `1.`. Tests: 10 model tests port `block-commands`, `outline-caret` (merge, move, join, paste, empty-bullet removal, ID retention), and `list-numbers` vectors; 14 GPUI tests drive real keystrokes in Write with Vim on and off (Enter, numbered continuation with undo, Tab/Shift-Tab and bare bullets, Vim `>>`/`<<`/`.`, Alt/Cmd-Shift move with undo, Backspace/Delete rules including the first and lone empty bullet and the referenced-block refusal notice, Home/`0`/`^`/Left on task lines, `zc`/`zo` and Cmd-Alt-[/] folding with Enter after a folded parent and merges blocked by collapsed children, `o`/`O`/`J`, multi-line paste with undo, Cmd-Shift-7 toggling and explicit `NumberList`/`BulletList`, literal code, and Source keeping Zed's Markdown editing). Commands: `CARGO_HOME=/private/tmp/quire-cargo cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store --locked --features quire/test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` (181 quire tests and all core/store suites pass); both scoped Clippy gates with `-D warnings` pass; `cargo fmt --check` passes; the worktree's `tests/reference` Vitest run passes 79 tests in 3 files. Batch A (2026-10-31), commits `2aaefd7c7d57` to `b925fef7b471`: zoom (`outline/zoom.rs`) opens a `SemanticExcerpt` of the caret's item bound to its ID (an ID-less item gets one as its own undo step), so it shares the page's buffer and history and follows the item; Cmd-. zooms in, Cmd-Shift-. or the "All blocks" bar zooms out with the caret kept, and outline commands in a zoom or a block embed map view offsets to source offsets, treat the shown item as the zoom root, and refuse edits outside it (`OUTSIDE_VIEW`). `follow_edits` in `outline.rs` follows every local edit in the focused view: it renumbers the numbered runs the edit touched and gives copied block IDs fresh ones (`outline/fresh_ids.rs`: copies of IDs already on the page at once, others after `IndexClient::resolve_block` names another page), and `amend` merges those follow-up edits into the edit's own transaction, so one undo reverts both. Vendored Vim changes: linewise `yy`/`p`/`P` use complete source lines in filtered views (`normal/yank.rs`, `normal/paste.rs` under `ViewEditIntent::Structural`), dot-repeat replays insertions into the focused Vim editor without a workspace (`normal/repeat.rs`), and `vim::take_host_count` and `vim::end_host_visual` let `3>>` and visual `>`/`<` move sibling subtrees through `model::indent_lines`, which refuses mixed levels with `NOT_SIBLINGS`. `DeleteToTextStart` (macOS Cmd-Backspace; Zed binds no delete-to-line-start elsewhere), `model::delete_empty_link`, and `model::step_out_of_link` (skipped while a completion menu shows) add the edge rules. The literal check now reads the character before the caret, as rt's left-biased lookup does, except at a line start. Tests: `outline_commands_match_rt_parity_vectors` runs all 1734 vectors of rt's `tests/fixtures/outline-parity.json` (copied unchanged from `5ea5c22310ff`) through the command layer's checks; the 2 parse gaps batch A excluded (a bare `-` after an item, a code span holding `[[x]]`) are fixed in NR10.8's follow-up and no vector is excluded. GPUI tests: `numbered_lists_renumber_after_plain_edits`, `copied_block_ids_become_fresh_ids` (Vim `yyp` and Cmd-V, with undo), `pasted_ids_another_page_has_become_fresh` (stored notebook with the index), `vim_linewise_yank_and_put_keep_hidden_syntax`, `link_pairs_delete_together_and_enter_steps_out`, `cmd_backspace_stops_at_the_text_start`, `vim_dot_repeats_opening_a_block_with_its_text`, `vim_counts_and_visual_shifts_move_sibling_subtrees`, five zoom tests, and `a_focused_zoom_counts_as_its_page_holding_focus`. Deviations from rt: zoom uses Cmd-. and Cmd-Shift-. instead of a bullet click (bullets have no controls yet) and is an excerpt of the page, not a separate page; copied IDs get fresh IDs instead of being removed, so the copy stays addressable; multi-line Vim shifts move sibling subtrees instead of shifting plain lines; the move palette refuses creating a page. Parity S18, Move block to another page (commit `073f0a1b3186`): `block_move::plan` reads source and destination from live buffers or storage, cuts the item with its children (giving an ID-less item an ID first), appends it to the destination, retargets every `Page#^id` reference to a moved block in pages the index names (`relocation`), and `page_writes::apply` writes all pages or none, refusing pages changed since the plan (`STALE_MOVE`), so a failure never removes the source; `((id))` references need no change. The page menu's "Move block to page…" opens a page palette that refuses Create and the same page (`SAME_PAGE`). Evidence: `moving_a_block_takes_its_children_and_retargets_page_references`, `a_block_without_an_id_gets_one_and_the_same_page_is_refused`, and the `block_move_tests` model tests, run with the full quire test gate on this branch. Remaining for S18: a folder-notebook move test and a fault-injection test for a write failing after the plan. Batch G (2026-10-31), folding and the remaining outline cases. Bullets: `editor_projection` replaces each list marker with an empty-text fold placeholder whose element draws rt's bullet (dot or number, rt's ink and ring colors, a ring while collapsed; click zooms through `zoom::zoom_from_bullet`, which runs `ZoomIn`) and, for an item with children while collapsed or on the caret's row (Zed's gutter rule), an arrow that folds. Display text and columns are unchanged, so no other view's test changed meaning. Three vendored editor edits make that possible and are recorded in `vendor/zed/UPSTREAM.json`: `tab_map.rs` and `display_map.rs` pass an empty-text chunk with a renderer through instead of dropping it, `fold_map.rs` includes such a fold when chunks start exactly at it (the first visible line), and `fold.rs`/`editor.rs` emit `EditorEvent::FoldsChanged`. Folds: `outline/folds.rs` inserts a Zed crease (`OutlineFold` placeholder) over every list item's children on each parse, so Zed's gutter disclosure, `editor::Fold`, `UnfoldLines`, `FoldAll`, and the existing `za`/`zc`/`zo`/Cmd-Alt-[/] all fold blocks; `FoldAllBlocks`/`UnfoldAllBlocks` (`zM`, `zR`, Cmd-K Cmd-0/J) work in zooms too. Expanding keeps nested folds (Zed's unfold removes intersecting folds, so inner ones are folded again). After an outline command, `folds::reconcile` drops folds left over non-children and folds again each collapsed block the command moved, found by its subtree text nearest its old place. On `FoldsChanged` the projection refolds when outline folds changed or a foreign unfold revealed hidden syntax. The caret clamp keeps carets out of collapsed children and a collapsed block's hidden ID (forward to the next line, otherwise the text end), as rt does. Saved folds: `outline/fold_store.rs` keeps `quire-folds.json` beside the notebook database, keyed by notebook then page ID; each folded block is a `Mark` (ID, else sibling path plus first line), resolved by ID, then path and text, then a unique first line, so a changed page never folds the wrong block. It is its own store, not `quire_core::workspace`, because folds are per-page editor state that changes with every fold and edit, while the workspace file holds navigation saved on a debounce by the navigation owner; folding there would rewrite tabs and favorites on each fold and put editor detail in `quire_core`. Writes run in the background, newest generation wins, and a failure shows a notice. Only whole-page views that are not embeds save and restore folds. Leftovers: a Vim visual count (`3>`) shifts that many levels in one undo step (`apply_then` now repeats follow-up plans); a fresh ID the index answers after more typing joins the paste's transaction through `amend_later` (finalize, edit, `merge_transactions` into the paste while history holds it; undo reverts edits by their own operations, so the typing stays); `references::new_block_id` is the only `b-` ID generator (completion, templates, Roam import, file actions, fresh IDs). Tests: `fold_store` unit tests (mark resolution, saves keeping other notebooks); outline GPUI tests `bullets_draw_without_changing_text_and_their_arrow_folds` (also bounds a bullet at 24 px), `zed_fold_actions_fold_blocks_and_keep_write_syntax_hidden`, `folds_follow_moved_blocks_and_keep_nested_folds` (move, nested folds, `zM`/`zR`), the extended `vim_counts_and_visual_shifts_move_sibling_subtrees`, `a_late_follow_up_edit_joins_its_edit_after_later_typing`; stored `fold_tests::folded_blocks_come_back_when_the_page_opens_again` (fold, file written, reopen, folds back, `zR` clears the file); `outline/editor_parity_tests.rs` runs 12 rt `editor.test.tsx` scenarios by name as keystrokes (Roam splits, Enter and Tab nesting with Vim on and off, repeated Enter, stepping across collapsed children, moving a block with a shared ID, Vim `o`, `D`, `C`, `cc`, `>>`, `J`) plus the disclosure-bullet scenario. The `wrapped_link_overlay_preserves_selection_in_fragment_gaps` fixture starts its label with a short word, since the bullet now takes room in that narrow pane. Batch I (2026-10-31), commits `48cc58115a6c` to `b996f7208c1f`, editor follow-ups. Dot-repeat after `o`: the root cause was test setup, not Vim. `editor_host::init` ran once per test view, and each run registered Vim's global keystroke observer again, so every action was recorded once per registration and `.` replayed `OpenBelow` that many times (the probe's "several empty bullets"); the app calls `init` once, and `init` now returns early on a second call. The outline test view sets `vim_mode` explicitly instead of relying on the settings reset. Regression test `vim_dot_repeats_opened_text_that_contains_o` covers typed text containing `o`, one key batch, and `.` in a separate batch, in a second view of the same app; rt's "opens a block with Vim o on an empty block, and repeats it with ." case is back in `editor_parity_tests.rs`. Vim `D` before a hidden ID: the caret clamp puts a Normal-mode caret (Zed's `clip_at_line_ends`) on the last visible character, so `- al| ^abc` becomes `- a|l ^abc`; two Enter tests that placed a Normal-mode caret past the text by hand now use `A`. Hanging wraps: a vendored GPUI change (`text_system/line_wrapper.rs`, recorded in `UPSTREAM.json`, unit test `test_hanging_prefix_indents_continuation_rows`) treats an element that replaces no text before a line's first character as the start of a hanging prefix; its width plus the checkbox and spaces after it, rounded to space widths, indents continuation rows, so a list item wraps under its text as rt's `listLayouts` lays it out. rt's two `block-wrapping` cases are ported as rendered-wrap tests (`wrapped_list_lines_hang_under_their_text`, `wrapped_rows_count_tab_stops_and_leave_code_alone`): a bullet hangs 4 columns, a nested task 6 more, a number 5, paragraphs and fenced code 0. The rounding leaves up to half a space of misalignment, since wrap indents are whole columns. rt cases ported: zoom select-all then typing keeps the page and the block's ID (`typing_over_a_zoom_keeps_the_page_around_it`; `filter_edits` now keeps a block's hidden ID when typed text replaces its text through a line end), caret clamping and Alt-Up inside a zoom (`a_zoom_keeps_the_caret_before_hidden_ids_and_moves_children`; moving a zoom's last child was refused as outside the view because the plan rewrote the newline after the zoom with itself, so `apply_then` retries with `model::trim_unchanged` edits), "does not merge lines inside an embedded block editor" (`backspace_does_not_merge_lines_in_a_block_embed`; merges that join lines do nothing in a nested embed), "removes a new block's ID with the block on undo" (`undo_removes_a_new_blocks_id_with_the_block`), and "never turns the line above a typed - into a heading" (`a_typed_dash_never_turns_the_line_above_into_a_heading`; Quire's grammar has no setext headings). Saved folds: zooms and embeds now save and restore the page's folds; each view records the folds it had at its last save and writes only what it collapsed or expanded since, so a page view that keeps editing never overwrites a zoom's folds, and a zoom does not restore a fold of its own root (`folds_made_in_a_zoom_are_saved_with_the_page`). Ctrl-Backspace runs `DeleteToTextStart` off macOS: rt binds Mod-Backspace on every platform, and Quire binds no word deletion to Ctrl-Backspace. Deviations from rt: undo groups the ID with the typing it was added during, as Zed groups edits, so one undo removes both and the next removes the new block; a fold made in a zoom shows in the page view only after the page reopens, since each view keeps its own Zed folds.
Closed in batch M (NR04.4): child guides now draw as rt draws them, a 2 px line under each parent's dot through its subtree, in place of Zed's indent guides in Write, so Help's "vertical guides show nesting depth" holds; the fold arrow shows on hover or while collapsed, not on the caret's line; setext headings stay off in every page parser (the Quire grammar, and the Write styles read from it).
Remaining: none. Closed by the S1 batch (2026-11-01): the fold store keeps each Write view of a page and brings the others to the saved folds once one view saves a change (`stored/fold_tests.rs::folds_made_in_a_zoom_reach_the_page_view_at_once`); bullet controls stay pointer-only as in rt, whose bullet buttons Tab cannot reach because Tab indents, and their commands have keys.
Status: in_progress

### NR15: Complete references and editable embeds

What to do: Implement wiki/Roam links, block completion, inline previews, full subtrees, page embeds, backlinks navigation, and nested source editing.
Constraints: Resolve through current notebook queries; preserve IDs; bound cycles/depth/views. One buffer/history across every occurrence.
Write set: reference resolver, excerpt views and completion integration.
Dependencies: NR12, NR14.
Verify: Existing reference/embed fixtures and native tests cover parent/child edits, rename, missing targets, cycles, two panes, multiple occurrences, and source deletion/conflict without stale overwrites.
Proof notes: Four slices landed, each built on Zed mechanisms rather than a parallel UI. (1) `quire_store/src/index/blocks.rs` adds `block_choices` (live, non-code, non-empty blocks containing every typed word, by page path then order) and `block_preview` (text and child count of a uniquely resolved ID) to the query index, so both see unsaved text through the overlay views. (2) `crates/quire/src/references.rs` and `references/completion.rs`: a `ReferenceHost` entity (the window as a `Notebook` trait object plus the current `IndexClient`) is installed on every page editor, Write, Source, and excerpt alike. Following reuses Zed's own actions through `register_action`: `GoToDefinition` (F12, Vim `gd`, Ctrl-]), `GoToDefinitionSplit` (Alt-F12, Vim Ctrl-W d) opening beside, and `FindAllReferences` (Alt-Shift-F12, Vim `gA`) opening the target with Linked references focused. With no reference at the caret the handler declines and Zed's binding runs. `StoredWindow::follow` resolves through `find_page` or `resolve_block` and keeps one task, so a newer request cancels an unfinished one. Completion is a Zed `CompletionProvider`: `[[` lists page titles from `pages()`, `((` lists `block_choices` with `is_incomplete` so every keystroke requeries, skipping the block being typed. Accepting an unnamed block inserts a fresh `b-<uuid>` ID, then the `confirm` callback opens the source page through the notebook, waits for it to load, and appends ` ^id` to the block found by key and text, through that page's shared buffer; a changed block gets a notice instead. (3) Write rendering in `markdown_links.rs` and `editor_projection.rs`: page links and labeled references render as their label through the existing link regions; unlabeled `((id))` inside text folds into a one-character placeholder whose element shows `↗ text` (or `id · unresolved`) from `block_preview`, re-requested when the index changes, with a sequence per target so an older answer never replaces a newer one. Click follows; Shift-click opens beside. (4) `embeds.rs`: a reference alone on its line (the reference `fullEmbed` rule, `markdown_links::is_full_embed`) folds into a header placeholder and gets a Zed custom block below its line holding a nested Write editor. A block embed views a `SemanticExcerpt` of the referenced page's analysis buffer and a page embed a singleton over that buffer, both obtained through `NotebookSession::open_page`, so all occurrences share one buffer, history, and `PagePersistence`. Nested editors carry the chain of embed keys; an embed already in the chain or past depth 8 shows a recursion notice. Block heights follow the nested editor's display rows, including its own embeds, and resize on edits. Resolution is asynchronous with per-target sequence guards; a page still loading is awaited through an observer.

Deviations from `rt`, chosen deliberately. Following uses Zed's go-to-definition actions and keys instead of rt's click-only links. The ID written on block completion is its own undo step in the source page, whereas rt keeps it out of history. Word completions are disabled in page editors through a vendored change making `Editor::disable_word_completions` public, so `[[` with no match offers nothing rather than buffer words. Previews use one-character fold placeholders because Zed's wrap and tab maps panic on longer placeholder text. Following a missing page creates it, titled by the text before any `#`. Hover popovers are not used: previews are inline. The embed header replaces the reference text while the caret is outside the embedding block, and the reference shows as source while the caret is inside it.

Bugs found and fixed, each with a regression test. Multi-character fold placeholders panicked in `FoldPoint::to_offset` (`write_renders_page_links_and_live_block_previews_that_open_on_click`). Binding Write inside a window update read the window re-entrantly to find the index; the host now carries the index client (caught by the existing stored recovery test). Zed hid custom blocks on any row whose start lies in a fold, which is every Write list item because the marker is folded; the vendored `block_map.rs` now hides a non-replacing block only when the fold covering its row start reaches across rows (recorded in `vendor/zed/UPSTREAM.json`; covered by the embed tests, since the editor crate's own tests need settings assets that are not vendored). Replacement blocks were tried first and rejected: Zed drops a replacement whose ends coincide, removing one beside another tripped a block-map row-count assertion, and a removed-then-reinserted replacement vanished.

Tests: `quire_store/tests/index.rs::block_choices_and_previews_read_live_blocks_and_refuse_ambiguous_ids`; `references/tests.rs` (target kinds, inclusive caret end) and completion unit tests (unclosed openings, plain block labels); `markdown_links::references_render_as_labels_previews_or_full_embeds`; GPUI tests with real keystrokes: `links_tests.rs` (Vim `gd`, Ctrl-W d, `gA` on page and block links; F12 family with Vim off; plain text keeping Zed's behavior; a newer follow replacing an unfinished one), `completion_tests.rs` (Vim Insert page and block completion with exact text and carets, the typed block skipped, the ID written to the source page's store head; Write with Vim off reusing an existing ID and offering no buffer words), `rendered_links_tests.rs` (labels, live preview text after an edit in the target page, unresolved after an edit, click and Shift-click), and `embeds_tests.rs` (block-embed edits with Vim on and off reaching the sibling page embed, leaving the host unchanged, persisting to the source page's store head, and undoing back to it; the caret revealing the reference; the header opening the source; Source view dropping embeds; a self-embed stopping at a recursion notice). Commands: the full `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store --locked --features quire/test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` passed three consecutive times (243 quire tests); both Clippy gates with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, the `tests/reference` Vitest run (90 tests), and `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts` pass.

Follow-up slices (batch B). Completion: `references/completion.rs` recognizes `![[` (a page, or a block written as `Page#^id`) and `{{embed:` or `{{[[embed]]:` (a page as `[[Title]]` or a block as `((id))`, then the closing `}}`, with a space added after the colon when none was typed). `block_choices` ranks matches before taking the limit: the whole typed phrase opening the text, then the phrase at a word start, the phrase anywhere, every word at a word start, every word anywhere, with shorter texts first inside a tier (`index/blocks.rs::rank`, up to 5,000 candidates). Embeds: each nested editor sits in a fixed-height wrapper sized by `content_rows`, which leaves out the empty row after a page's final newline. Heading links: `QueryIndex::find_heading` resolves `Page#Heading` to the page and its first heading whose text matches ignoring case and `#` marks; `StoredWindow::follow` uses it for page targets that name a heading and shows "<Title> has no heading “<text>”, so it opened at the top. Check the heading in the link." when the page lacks it. Caret crossing: the `Embeds` addon tracks, on every selection change, edit, and placement, whether Down or Up from the caret's row would enter the embed below or above its line, or leave a nested editor from its first or last shown row, and adds `QuireEmbedDown` or `QuireEmbedUp` to the editor's key context. `CaretDownAcrossEmbed` and `CaretUpAcrossEmbed` are bound to Down and Up (outside menus and pending Vim operators) and to Vim `j` and `k` in Normal mode without a count; the handler focuses the other editor and keeps the caret's column where the line allows. Nested embeds compose, since each nested editor carries its own `Embeds` addon and a weak handle to the view around it. Hover preview: page links in Write get a Zed hoverable tooltip (`references/preview.rs`) that asks the new `QueryIndex::page_preview` for the page title and its first six non-empty, non-code blocks with heading marks removed, starting at the heading for `Page#Heading`; a missing page says that following the link creates it, and an index failure says to wait for indexing.

Bug found and fixed with a regression test: outline commands typed in an embed (Vim `o`, `O`, `J`, `z` folds) are declined by the nested editor, and GPUI then offered them to the host editor, which is an ancestor in the dispatch tree, so `o` inside an embed inserted a list item in the host page. `outline::register` now acts only in the focused editor and propagates otherwise, so Zed's own binding runs in the nested editor (`outline_commands_an_embed_declines_never_edit_the_host_page`).

Tests added: `completion_tests.rs::embed_forms_complete_pages_and_blocks_with_their_closing_syntax`; `index/blocks.rs::ranks_phrase_openings_then_word_starts_then_anywhere`; `quire_store/tests/index.rs::heading_links_find_the_page_and_its_first_matching_heading` and `page_previews_start_at_the_page_or_its_heading_and_skip_code`; `embeds_tests.rs::embeds_follow_a_renamed_source_page`, `deleting_the_embedded_block_then_its_page_leaves_unresolved_headers`, `two_panes_show_one_embed_and_share_its_edits`, `an_embed_edit_merges_with_a_concurrent_save_of_its_source`, `outline_commands_an_embed_declines_never_edit_the_host_page`, `vim_j_and_k_walk_into_and_out_of_embeds`, and `arrow_keys_walk_into_and_out_of_embeds_with_vim_off` (seven Down steps and seven Up steps through a block embed and a page embed, asserting the focused editor and row after each key, and the host page unchanged); `links_tests.rs::a_heading_link_opens_its_page_and_says_when_the_heading_is_missing`; `rendered_links_tests.rs::hovering_a_page_link_previews_the_page_it_names` (the tooltip appears on hover; preview text for an existing and a missing page).

Deviations from `rt`, added by these slices. rt has no hover preview for page links; quire adds one because Zed's hoverable tooltip makes it cheap and it answers "what is behind this link" without a jump. Crossing between the host and an embed moves focus between two Zed editors and keeps the caret's column, not a pixel goal.

Followed `[[Page#Heading]]` links now put the caret on the heading through the shared reveal (`Jump::at(None, start)`, test `a_heading_link_opens_its_page_and_says_when_the_heading_is_missing`, which fails without it); followed block links already reveal their block (batch C).

Remaining: none for embeds here. The Q batch (2026-11-01) found `o` and Enter on an embed's last line already stay in the embed and pinned that with `stored/zoom_tests.rs::opening_a_line_on_an_embeds_last_line_stays_in_the_embed`; the host now scrolls to keep the caret in sight inside a tall embed (`embeds.rs::reveal`, `the_host_scrolls_to_keep_the_caret_in_a_tall_embed_visible`). (Fixed earlier: an empty child item such as `- holder ^spot\n  - \n` now interrupts the holder's paragraph, so `((spot))` embeds resolve; see NR10.8.)
Status: in_progress

### NR16: Port navigation and daily layout

What to do: Add physical files, logical pages/namespaces, daily browser, completion/search palette, split tabs, history, favorites, recents, and daily-layout settings.
Constraints: Namespace navigation does not reorganize files. Namespaces follow the `rt` 0.9.52 `namespaceOf` rule in parity row S20; refresh the frozen `workspace.ts` first. With Vim on, Insert-starting keys pressed while focus is outside a text field or dialog go to the last-used page editor, or the open page (`23287a0e2516`). Workspace state is local and notebook-scoped; date/title matching follows current rules.
Write set: workspace/navigation views, daily/layout core, preference persistence.
Dependencies: NR12, NR15.
Verify: Native keyboard/mouse/tab/history tests plus daily-layout fixtures, duplicate dates, profile switches, restored reading position, and empty folders. Lists remain virtualized.
Proof notes: Shared daily rules are done under NR16.1. Source for the navigation slices: `rt` 0.9.57 (`5ea5c22`), `src/ui/App.tsx` (`SearchDialog`, `visit`, tabs, history), `src/ui/NotebookBrowser.tsx`, `src/ui/PageList.tsx`, `src/core/workspace.ts`, and `src/desktop/engine/engine.ts` (settings page).

`quire_core::workspace` holds the window-independent rules: `Workspace` (tabs capped at 30, recents at 12 with 6 shown, favorites in insertion order, expanded folders, side pane, close fallback to the preferred tab, then the last tab, then the Pages browser), `History` (push skips the current entry and cuts forward entries; not persisted, as the reference resets it per load), `namespace_of`/`namespaces`/`in_namespace`, and `folder_rows`. `crates/quire_core/tests/workspace.rs` ports the `workspace.test.ts` folder, namespace (all 17 titles), and invalid-preference cases, and adds visit, close, favorite, missing-page, and history cases. `QueryIndex::pages` lists live pages (overlays included, the settings page excluded) ordered by title; `path_taken` checks a name case-insensitively.

The stored window (`editor_host/stored/navigation.rs`, `chrome.rs`, `daily.rs`) adds the find palette on Zed's `picker` crate, page creation (`navigation::create_page`: `safe_page_path`, refusal of a taken name, `page_id_for_path` identity unless taken, starting text `- `), history, recents, tabs, a side pane, favorites, a virtualized Files tree, and virtualized Pages and Daily notes browsers. Keys: Cmd/Ctrl-K find, Cmd/Ctrl-N new page, Cmd/Ctrl-J today, Cmd-[/Cmd-] (Alt-Left/Right off macOS) and mouse back/forward for history, Ctrl-Tab/Ctrl-Shift-Tab cycle tabs, Cmd/Ctrl-W close tab, Cmd/Ctrl-\ toggle the side pane, Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-E focus Files; list arrows, Home/End, Right/Left, Enter, and Shift-Enter (open beside). The palette tags each request with a sequence number and the index generation from `with_generation`; `apply` rejects superseded requests and older generations. The window reads the `s-notebook` settings page before starting the index, so `IndexConfig` carries the notebook's layout; saving a layout writes that page (base: first head, as the reference does) and restarts the index. Unreadable or newer settings stay read-only and unchanged. Workspace state is saved per notebook in `quire-workspace.json` beside the database (atomic temp file and rename, invalid entries dropped, any read error gives an empty workspace); destinations for deleted pages drop when the catalogue loads.

Deviations from `rt`, chosen deliberately. Destinations key on page identity rather than path, so renames need no rewrite.
