The pick prompt shows rt's key hint `↑↓ choose · Enter insert · Esc cancel` as the picker footer (`templates/prompt.rs::render_footer`, picks only). `template_insert_tests.rs::a_pick_for_a_tag_never_used_asks_to_name_it_and_shows_the_key_hint` checks the `Name this fresh` placeholder, the footer, and creating `[[Ann]]`. `jj_inside_an_embedded_block_inserts_into_the_source_page` types `j A Enter j j m y` in a block embed's nested editor and accepts the menu: the template lands in the source page below the embedded child and the host page is unchanged. Writing that test found the outline-command bug recorded under NR15 (an embed's declined `o` edited the host page).

Batch F. Keyboard focus: the sidebar Templates entry is a tab stop (NR16 batch F). Cmd/Ctrl-Shift-J (`FocusTemplatesGuide`) focuses the guide's first Add button for an example the page lacks; the guide is a tab group whose Tab and Shift-Tab walk every Add button, and an Added button stays focusable and adds nothing, so focus never drops when an example is added. Test: `template_tests::the_keyboard_reaches_the_templates_entry_and_the_guides_add_buttons`.
Remaining: fixing the two unrelated flakes above (the embed header click can take the same quiet-window wait); consolidating `copy_with_new_ids`, which now exists twice: NR13's line-based `quire_core::markdown::copy_with_new_ids` for store copies without a parser, and this task's block-based `quire_core::templates::copy_with_new_ids`, which the vectors check against rt's parser.
Status: done

### NR21: Add attachments and inline media

What to do: Implement attach/open, local/remote/data image rendering, PDFs using the selected renderer, missing-file placeholders, and bounded decode/texture work.
Constraints: Preserve source syntax and notebook boundaries; a missing remote asset must not block editing. New attachments have a durable local copy.
Write set: attachment service, image/PDF views, media fixtures.
Dependencies: NR06, NR09, NR15.
Verify: Attach/restart/backup resolution, both backends, path traversal/symlink rejection, late delivery, offline failures, PDF scrolling, and bounded memory on large documents.
Proof notes: New `quire_media` crate ports `rt` `attachments.ts` target rules (notebook-relative, HTTP(S), `data:`; other schemes, `//` hosts, hidden parts, `..` escapes, and symlinks refused), bounded image decoding (50 MB encoded, 16,384 px a side, 64 Mpx, downscaled to 1600 px; SVG without external loads), and PDF rendering on one thread per document: PDFium through `pdfium-render` 0.9.4 (`pdfium_7881`) for PDFs stored in the notebook, hayro 0.8.0 for remote and `data:` PDFs. PDFium comes from `bblanchon/pdfium-binaries` `chromium/7881` via `bun tools/fetch-pdfium.ts`, which checks a pinned SHA-256 per platform and unpacks into the user cache; tests never download. Write shows images and PDFs as Zed custom blocks below their line with folded `Image · name` and `PDF · name` headers; work runs on the background executor; a 192 MB decoded cache evicts with `drop_image`; PDFs render lazily through a GPUI `list`, keep 6 pages, honor `#page=N`, zoom 50 to 400%, find with Enter and Shift-Enter, select and copy text, and open in the system viewer. Attach file (Cmd-Alt-A), image paste, and file drop stream into the store attachment cache under content names (`NotebookStore::attach_file`) and insert `rt` link forms in one undo step. Folder notebooks read media by page-relative path, and since commit `0b5517a245a4` attach into `attachments/<8 hex>-<name>` as `rt`'s vault does (see NR23). PDFium calls are serialized per whole operation (open, page load, render, text, close) because the per-call lock in `pdfium-render` let concurrent documents report 0 pages. Tests: 18 `quire_media` unit tests, including six threads opening stored PDFs at once, on the checked-in 888-byte generated fixture `crates/quire_media/tests/fixtures/two-pages.pdf`, a store streaming test, and GPUI tests for local/remote/data/missing images, headers, lazy bounded PDF rendering with `#page`, zoom, and texture release, find (including Enter in the field) and drag selection with Copy, hayro and missing PDFs, attach command with undo, paste, drop, and folder notebooks with escape refusal. Gates: fmt, both Clippy gates, the full test command (531 passed in two of three final runs; the third hit a 20-second timeout in the NR15 embed test `the_caret_reveals_an_embed_reference...`, which passed 5 of 5 alone), and vitest reference (92 passed). Batch E on 2026-10-31. `140f374ba7ec`: image and PDF views that failed on a notebook file observe a `media::load::Arrivals` global and load again when it is set: by `folder_changed(None)` (the watcher reports a non-note file as a whole-folder change, and the 30 s backstop uses it too) and, for Quire notebooks, by a new `LiveSync` `AttachmentsArrived` event emitted when an import's existing `RescanReport::attachments_completed` is positive; `rescan_all` now sums that field across continuations, which it dropped before. Remote and `data:` sources are not retried. Tests: `media_folder_tests::a_picture_that_appears_in_the_folder_later_shows_without_retry` and `media_tests::a_picture_another_computer_delivers_after_its_page_shows_without_retry` (a writer's batch imported while its attachment is held back, then delivered). `d8b09ce17b49`: each media block's render measures its column (`max_width - anchor_x`) and resizes the block when the view's width changes by a pixel or more (`pictures_refit_their_blocks_when_the_window_narrows_and_widens`); a drop maps its window position through the editor's `character_index_for_point` and inserts there, falling back to the caret (`dropping_files_on_a_page_attaches_them_where_they_drop`). `e60396092f80`: GIFs decode every composited frame with its delay through `image`'s `GifDecoder` (under 20 ms shows for 100 ms, as browsers do), within `Limits::animation_bytes` (64 MB of downscaled frames, otherwise the first frame), and GPUI animates the multi-frame `RenderImage`; the cache counts every frame's bytes. SVG text renders with the system fonts, loaded once into a shared `fontdb`. Tests: `animated_gifs_keep_every_frame_and_its_delay`, `svg_text_renders_in_a_system_font`, and `an_animated_gif_shows_every_frame`. `246e23bfaa29`: hayro PDFs get text from a text-only `hayro_interpret::Device` that reads each glyph's Unicode mapping and boxes it from its advance and a 1000-unit em band, inserting a space or line break at gaps; invisible text counts. `Info::has_text` is gone since both engines have text. Tests: `hayro_renders_untrusted_bytes_and_reads_text_with_boxes_from_its_glyphs`, `hayro_text_separates_words_and_lines_the_pdf_draws_apart`, and `find_reads_the_text_of_a_pdf_hayro_renders`. `f9b5c4e376a1`: a selection is an anchor and head of page and character, highlighted per page, extended by a drag onto another page, and copied with a line break between pages (`a_selection_runs_across_pdf_pages_and_copies_with_a_break_between_them`, which drives the selection model). `7011c9a6ea84`: the new scroll-wheel test found the note scrolling with the PDF; the pages viewport now stops the scroll unless it is at its first page scrolling up or its last page scrolling down (`the_scroll_wheel_over_a_pdf_scrolls_its_pages_not_the_page_around_it`).


PDF hit test on 2026-10-31 (batch H), `1abaa6e065b3`: the cross-page miss was not zoom. Each page records its window bounds through a `canvas` that was `absolute()` with no inset, so once the page's rendered image (an in-flow child) arrived, the canvas took its static position after the image, one page height lower; mouse positions were then resolved against the wrong page's bounds, and a press on page 0's text reached page 1. The canvas now sits at `top_0().left_0()`. The earlier single-page drag test passed by coincidence: every page of its PDF starts with "Page". `media_pdf_tests::a_mouse_drag_selects_across_pdf_pages_and_copies_with_a_break_between_them` drives the mouse at 50% zoom: a press on page 0 selects "First" (it read "Seco" before the fix), a backward drag from page 2 to page 0 selects "page\nSecond page\nThird", and Cmd-C copies a one-page selection.
Remaining: attach/restart/backup resolution acceptance (NR26); AVIF, which needs an AV1 decoder (dav1d or rav1d) that is not in the lockfile and cannot be fetched offline; no command palette entry for Attach file; PDFium on Linux and Windows unverified; library signing and packaging (NR35).
Status: in_progress

### NR22: Add code highlighting and formatting

What to do: Integrate copied syntax infrastructure and the NR06 formatter choice for JavaScript, TypeScript, JSON, CSS, HTML, YAML, and Markdown.
Constraints: Formatting applies only to unchanged source as one undo transaction; unsupported languages remain editable.
Write set: code-block presentation, formatter adapters/resources, fixtures.
Dependencies: NR06, NR14.
Verify: Match reference fixtures, retain indentation/IDs, reject stale responses, cancel cleanly, copy code, and format in a package with no developer runtime installed.
Proof notes: Highlighting uses Zed's injection path rather than rt's highlight.js. `crates/quire/src/markdown.rs` registers JavaScript, TypeScript, TSX, JSON, JSONC, CSS, HTML, YAML, Python, Shell Script, Rust, C++, JSDoc, and Regex lazily with `LanguageRegistry::register_language`, from the vendored `grammars` configs and queries; the Markdown grammar's existing `injections.scm` resolves each fence tag through `language_for_name_or_extension` (names and file suffixes, case-insensitive). Extra tags follow rt's aliases: `xml` maps to HTML and `shell` to Shell Script; `c` already resolves to C++, matching rt's `c` to `cpp` alias. HTML's config and queries were copied unchanged from Zed's `extensions/html` into `vendor/zed/crates/grammars/src/html` (recorded in `vendor/zed/UPSTREAM.json`). Quire has no project LSP store, which is what reparses Zed buffers whose injected language loads later, so a registry watcher reparses open pages with unknown injections; disabling it makes three of the four highlight tests fail. Formatting lives in the new `crates/quire_format`: Prettier 3.9.10's eight standalone bundles, vendored unmodified in `vendor/prettier` (npm integrity `sha512-skGHTH1Y…` as in rt's lock, per-file SHA-256 checked by a test, `.gitattributes` keeps bytes exact), run in QuickJS-ng (`rquickjs` 0.14.0, locked checksums equal to the NR06 spike's) on one `quire-format` thread with a 32 MiB stack. Requests can be cancelled through QuickJS's interrupt handler and are bounded at 512 KiB input, 20 s, and a 512 MiB heap; the engine is rebuilt after an interrupted or failed run. `crates/quire/src/code_format.rs` adds `quire_code::FormatCode`, bound to Cmd-Shift-I (Ctrl-Shift-I elsewhere; Zed's own Format key) in `Editor && !QuireControl`, so it works in Write and Source with Vim on or off. It finds the root-layer `fenced_code_block` at the newest caret, strips the closing fence's prefix from body lines, formats, re-prefixes each output line as rt's `replaceFencedBody` does, and applies a `language::text_diff` word diff in one `Editor::transact` bracketed by `finalize_last_transaction`, through the Structural edit intent. Deviations from `rt`: a key command at the caret instead of a toolbar button on the rendered block; it also works in Source and in list items and quotes; the block is anchored, so an edit elsewhere keeps the result and an edit inside the block discards it with a notice (rt compares text at a fixed offset); a second request in the same view cancels the first; messages name the next step ("Correct its syntax, then run Format code again.") instead of rt's "edit it in Source", and untagged, unsupported, oversized, timed-out, and read-only cases get their own messages; empty blocks are left alone. The stored window now shows outline/format notices. Tests: `cargo test -p quire_format --locked` passes 8 unit tests (parser mapping, pinned hashes, rt's `const x={a:1}` case and the exact Prettier syntax error, size refusal before the engine starts, cancellation of a running request in under 1 s with the next request succeeding, queued-request drop, timeout) and the corpus gate, 21 of 21 byte-identical with `spikes/nr06/format/expected/prettier`, using no Node or Bun. In quire, 4 highlight tests assert the resolved language and theme capture for 19 fence tags, plain editable unknown tags, highlighting of a fence typed after opening, and identical keyword/property colors in Write and Source, including a list-nested fence; 10 Format code tests cover fence/prefix extraction (list, quote, untagged, unclosed, outside), rt's prefix rule, exact before/after text and caret with one undo step for Vim off (Cmd-Z) and on (`u`), list indentation and block IDs in Write and Source, JSON/CSS/YAML/XML/Markdown fences in one page, five refusal messages with the page unchanged, stale discard, an outside edit that keeps the result, cancellation by a newer request, and the timeout message. Commands: the full `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store -p quire_format --locked --features quire/test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` run (290 quire tests plus all core, store, and formatter suites) passed twice; Clippy with `-D warnings` passes for quire, quire_core, quire_store, and quire_format; `cargo fmt --check` passes. Size, measured as `__TEXT`+`__DATA` of the debug-build archives (parse tables dominate, so release is close): grammars 9.8 MB (C++ 3.6, TypeScript and TSX 2.9, Bash 1.4, Rust 1.1, Python 0.5, the rest 0.6), QuickJS 1.1 MB at `-O0`, plus 2.3 MB of embedded Prettier JavaScript; NR06 measured 3.4 MB for the formatter in a release build. A release build of the app was not measured.
Toolbar pass, source `rt` 0.9.57 (`src/editor/CodeBlock.tsx`, the `MarkdownWidget` replacement in `src/editor/Editor.tsx`, `.code-toolbar` in `src/ui/styles.css`, and the two Format code tests in `tests/editor.test.tsx`). `crates/quire/src/code_format/toolbar.rs` lists every closed root-layer fence, and the Write projection (`apply_blocks` and `refresh` in `editor_projection.rs`, the same path as media headers) folds each opening fence line into a toolbar placeholder while the caret is outside the block's lines: the language as tagged, or rt's "Plain text"; Format code when `Parser::for_tag` accepts the tag and the view is editable, reading "Formatting…" while that block's request runs; and Copy code, reading "Copied" until the block's text changes. Every control is an `inline_control` whose element ID comes from its kind and the block's ordinal, so a click survives a refold, and is a Tab stop activated by Enter or Space. Clicking the language puts the caret at the fence, which shows the fence as written. `quire_code::CopyCode` copies `Fence::code`, the body without fences or container prefixes, which equals what rt's react-markdown hands its button (body plus a final newline). The page right-click menu adds Format code (disabled when unsupported or read-only) and Copy code when the caret is in a closed fence. Format code and Copy code now locate the block through the view's buffer offset, so both work in excerpt views (semantic excerpts of embedded blocks) when the view shows the whole block contiguously; `shown_range` checks that the mapped range has the buffer's exact text, the result is checked again before the edit, and a partly shown block gets "Only part of this code block is shown here. Open its page, then run Format code." The outline notice no longer clears "Formatting code…" on a caret move or edit in a view whose request is still running. Deviations from `rt`: rt replaces the whole fence with rendered, highlight.js-colored HTML while the caret is outside it; quire keeps the body as live Zed-highlighted text and folds only the opening fence line, so the closing fence stays visible. Copy code has no clipboard failure path because GPUI's clipboard write cannot fail, and quire has no action command palette, so `CopyCode` is reached from the toolbar, the right-click menu, and Zed's action dispatch. The toolbar's placeholder text appears in the display snapshot, so two projection tests now expect `md · Format code · Copy code` in place of the fence line.

Highlighting decision for `sql` and `clojure`, measured on 2026-10-31: not vendored. SQL's only maintained grammar, `tree-sitter-sequel` 0.3.11 (DerekStride/tree-sitter-sql, MIT, 888 KB crate), ships a 17.4 MB `parser.c` that compiles to 2.42 MB of `__TEXT` at `-O2`, more than Bash (1.4 MB) and close to TypeScript and TSX together (2.9 MB debug), so it fails the "small" bar. For Clojure, the MIT crate `tree-sitter-clojure` 0.1.0 ships no `parser.c` and cannot build; the grammar Zed's extension uses (sogaiu, republished as `tree-sitter-clojure-orchard` 0.2.9) is CC0-1.0, not MIT or Apache, although its 0.8 MB `parser.c` compiles to 85 KB. Both tags stay plain, editable text. Vendoring the Clojure grammar needs the owner to accept CC0; SQL needs acceptance of about 2.4 MB per build.

Tests: `code_format_tests.rs` adds 5 GPUI tests: toolbar labels for tagged, untagged, unsupported, case-preserved, and unclosed fences plus the read-only and caret-inside cases; Copy code from the toolbar and the action with exact clipboard text for a list-nested and a quoted fence, "Copied" ending after an edit, and the refusal outside code, with Vim off and on; the Format code button pressed, refolded, and released, showing "Formatting…" and keeping the notice through a caret move until the formatted block lands; Format code and Copy code in an excerpt that shows the whole block and refusals in one that shows part of it; and keyboard activation of all three controls (Enter with Vim off, Space with Vim on). `page_menu_tests.rs` adds the right-click menu's Format code and Copy code in Write and Source. Gates: both Clippy gates with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, the `tests/reference` Vitest run (103 tests), `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts /Users/umer/code/zed` (no vendored changes), and the full test command including `quire_media`, run three times (676 tests each, no failures). One earlier full run under load timed out once in `write_renders_page_links_and_live_block_previews_that_open_on_click`, a page without code blocks; it passed alone three times and in every later full run.

Remaining: Linux and Windows builds of `rquickjs-sys`; `sql` and `clojure` highlighting if the owner accepts the costs above; an action command palette, which quire does not have, if Copy code should be listed there. NR35 measured the release build and packages the notices.
Status: in_progress

## Data exchange and durability

### NR23: Implement Markdown import and export

What to do: Import folders without altering the source, flatten a page for standalone export, and export an entire notebook with attachments and an explicit report of excluded state.
Constraints: Preserve paths, titles, IDs and attachment resolution; no silent overwrite on collisions. Export works without AI.
Write set: import/export modules, progress/cancel UI, round-trip fixtures.
Dependencies: NR13, NR15, NR21.
Verify: Round-trip both backends; interrupt and resume staged import; compare page/attachment counts and hashes; flattened embeds/citations match current behavior; unknown formats fail safely.
Proof notes: pending; whole-notebook export was a source-plan gap. NR11.3.2 adds exact-source Copy Markdown for a stored-page preview, including recovery text and original line endings. It does not implement filesystem import/export, flattened embeds, or whole-notebook exchange.

Markdown-only import and export landed in three slices. Source: `rt` 0.9.57 `5ea5c22310ff` (`src/core/page-export.ts`, `src/core/attachments.ts`, `src/desktop/page-export.ts`, `NotebookEngine.importMarkdown` in `src/desktop/engine/engine.ts`, the `data:import-markdown` handler in `src/desktop/main.ts`, `tests/page-export.test.ts`, `tests/engine.test.ts`). (1) Core, commit `57dcea6ad0dc`: `quire_core::exchange::page_export` ports `exportMarkdown` (links as text, block references and embeds written out to depth 3, IDs and created stamps dropped, list-item page embeds nested under the page title, `quire-vault:` image links with JavaScript `decodeURI`/`encodeURIComponent` semantics), `localizeAttachments` with a resolver, and `exportName`; `exchange::import` ports the walk classification, `notebookPath`, and the case- and NFC-insensitive skip rule; `exchange::notebook_export` validates export paths and words the report. `tests/reference/core/page-export.ts` and `attachments.ts` are frozen with import-path changes only; `export-files.ts` and `markdown-import.ts` are extractions; hashes are under `exchange_extractions` in `tests/reference/UPSTREAM.json`. `tools/exchange-vectors.ts` writes 47 export notebooks (the rt case, cycles, re-indented embeds, media and escapes, a malformed escape, Unicode and duplicate IDs, front matter, 40 seeded), 3 attachment rewrites, 7 names, and 4 import folders; `crates/quire_core/tests/exchange.rs` answers each case with the reference's own parses and lookups and matches every output byte for byte. `tests/reference/exchange.test.ts` reruns the rt expectations through the frozen code. (2) Store, commit `874eca8b1512`: `quire_store::markdown_import::read_source` walks depth first in name order, skips dot entries and links, reads pages lossily as rt does, reports counts, honors a cancel flag, and refuses a source inside the notebook; `copy_files` installs other files with `persist_noclobber`, skipping existing ones, and returns what it created. `NotebookStore::import_pages` plans against live path keys and creates every page in one `save_many_if_heads`, giving a page whose derived ID already has history (a deleted page at that path) a fresh ID. `FolderNotebook::import_pages` creates notes through the existing journal in groups of at most 1,000 (`MAX_EDITS`) and removes the notes it created when a later group fails; reopening resumes nothing. (3) App, commit `6f7d953f72d4`: `crate::exchange` holds the actions, the status line (running label, Cancel while nothing is written, Show file, Dismiss), and `flatten_page`, which runs the core export over a `Gathered` snapshot and loads what each round found missing (native tree-sitter references and blocks, `find_page`, `resolve_block`, open buffers or stored heads) until nothing is missing. `editor_host/stored/exchange.rs` wires Export as Markdown…, Import a Markdown folder…, and Export notebook as Markdown… into the page header, with Zed path prompts, the save panel's own replace confirmation for a page, and a `window.prompt` before a notebook export replaces files; the notebook export stages in `.quire-export-<time>` inside the chosen folder and renames into place.

Deviations from `rt`, chosen deliberately. Import is all-or-nothing where rt commits 500-page chunks after copying files, so an unusable name refuses the whole import instead of leaving a prefix. Folder notebooks accept imports (rt refuses with "Folder notebooks already read Markdown files directly"), as the plan asks. Quire notebooks copy only the files an imported page links to (see the attachments slices below), since an unlinked file in the content-addressed cache would be invisible and never published; rt copies every file. The walk sorts entries by name, where rt uses `readdir` order. Imports are capped at 48 MB of page text to fit the storage queue's 64 MB budget, with an actionable refusal. Whole-notebook export is new; it writes open pages' live text and lists recovery drafts and competing versions as excluded.

Tests: `crates/quire_core/tests/exchange.rs` (5), `crates/quire_store/tests/markdown_import.rs` (5: the rt engine case with the source byte-identical and a second import changing nothing, an unusable path writing nothing, case and NFC collisions, a deleted page's path, folder notes and files never replaced, the walk order with cancellation and the inside-notebook refusal), `folder_notebook::markdown_import::tests` (rollback of a failing group, nothing resumed on reopen, retry landing whole), and `editor_host/stored/exchange_tests.rs` (7 GPUI tests: rt's import report and page list, Cancel writing nothing and a second import finishing, an unusable name, rt's flattened page through native analysis, notebook export cancel and Replace/Cancel prompts with exact files and CRLF kept, folder import with files plus the inside-notebook refusal and an export equal to the folder, image links localized only inside the notebook). Measured: the folder journal creates notes at roughly 70 ms each on this macOS disk (about 2,000 notes in 159 s in an abandoned 1,001-note test), so large folder imports are slow but correct; NR35 should measure them. Gates: core/store/format and app Clippy with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, the `tests/reference` Vitest run (103 tests), `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts /Users/umer/code/zed` (no vendored changes), and the full `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store -p quire_format --locked --features quire/test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` run twice (586 tests each, no failures).

Attachments after NR21, source `rt` 0.9.57 (`Vault.attach`, `ReplicaStore.attach`, `localizeAttachments`, `importMarkdown`). (4) Commit `26c5d44af342`: `quire_core::exchange::file_links` finds local file targets in inline links and images, `![[...]]`, and `{{pdf: ...}}` outside fenced code, resolves them from the page's folder with the ported `relativeVaultPath`, keeps `?query` and `#fragment`, and rewrites them. `NotebookStore::import_folder` plans as before, streams each linked source file into the attachment cache with `attach_file`, points the links at `attachments/<content name>`, and saves every page in one checked write in the same worker request; when attaching or the save fails it removes the attachments it added (names neither cataloged nor requested by a synced page before), so no blob is orphaned. rt does not rewrite links (its Quire notebook root holds files at their relative paths); quire has no such folder, so this rewrite is a deliberate deviation. (5) Commit `5583d721a37d`: notebook export reads each page through `page_writes::read`, so open pages export their live text, and for a Quire notebook copies each linked stored `attachments/<name>` (resolved through `Files::Store`) to `attachments/<name>` at the export root, rewriting links from each page's folder; linked attachments missing on this computer are listed as not exported. Page export resolves `quire-vault:` links to the stored attachment file, as rt's `localizeAttachments` does under its notebook root. Exporting then importing gives back byte-identical page text. (6) Commit `0b5517a245a4`: Attach file, paste, and drop in folder notebooks copy into `attachments/<8 hex>-<name>` as rt's `Vault.attach` does (`<>:"/\|?*` become `-`, a temporary file installed without replacing, file and folder synced, a linked `attachments` folder refused with rt's message adapted). Deviation: the link is written from the page's folder (`../attachments/...` in a subfolder), because rt's notebook-relative `attachments/...` resolves from the page's folder and breaks in subfolders. Slices 2 and 3 of this pass share one commit because both rewrite the same export loop. Tests: `file_links` unit tests (3), store unit tests for rollback after an injected save failure and a source file replaced by a link, `folder_attachment` unit tests (3), the store integration test `quire_notebooks_copy_linked_files_as_attachments_and_point_links_at_them`, and GPUI tests for an import attaching linked files, a Quire notebook export with copied attachments and an uncopied link, a Quire page export pointing at the stored file, unsaved typing in a notebook export, and folder-notebook attach (exact files, link text, picture shown, one undo) and its linked-folder refusal. Gates: both Clippy gates with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, the `tests/reference` Vitest run (103 tests), `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts /Users/umer/code/zed`, and the full test command including `quire_media` run twice (631 tests each, no failures).

Batch D (2026-10-31, `9ae854d428bc`, `90dc49207268`). Folder imports now write journals of 100 notes and report each note to the status line ("wrote N of M"), where 1,000-note journals at about 70 ms a note gave no progress. `exchange_tests` prove an import the process did not survive resumes without duplicates (`markdown_import::tests::importing_again_after_an_interrupted_import_writes_only_the_rest` in `quire_store`) and that export then import keeps page and attachment counts and SHA-256 hashes on both backends (`a_quire_notebook_round_trips_through_export_and_import_with_equal_counts_and_hashes` and its folder twin). Settings > Data and the File menu's Import and Export submenus run the commands in the active profile's notebook window (`data_tests::the_data_section_runs_backups_in_the_notebook_window_and_saves_the_personal_remote`).

Remaining: native file pickers and packaged-app acceptance (headless GPUI only). A crash inside a journal reruns that journal of at most 100 notes; whole journals already written are skipped.
Status: done

### NR24: Implement Roam JSON/EDN exchange

What to do: Port imports, source archives, metadata round trips, orphan recovery, and JSON export against the new service.
Constraints: Keep original bytes and unknown metadata. Never execute graph scripts or commit private graphs.
Write set: Roam/EDN codecs, metadata storage, synthetic fixtures.
Dependencies: NR10, NR23.
Verify: Existing import/export fixtures and detached-block cases; stable IDs, timestamps, order, references, and archive hashes; large synthetic import stays responsive. Private-copy verification records only counts/timings.
Proof notes: Source: `rt` 0.9.57 `5ea5c22310ff` (`src/core/roam.ts`, `roam-edn.ts`, `edn.ts`, `src/desktop/roam-files.ts`, `roam-import-worker.ts`, the `vault:import` and `vault:export` handlers in `src/desktop/main.ts`, `App.tsx`'s `switchVault(true)` and `exportRoam`, `tests/import.test.ts`, `roam-edn.test.ts`, `roam-files.test.ts`, `roam-worker.test.ts`). Three slices. (1) Core, commit `8faef79b33c3`: `quire_core::exchange::roam` ports `importRoamJSON` (portable titles, UID remapping with JavaScript `String.replace` patterns, `renderNodes`, `listText`, macro and remote-image notices, the 10 MB note limit) and `exportRoamJSON` (metadata validation, unchanged pages and blocks restored from metadata, Markdown blocks, gaps and custom front matter as blocks, link and embed conversion), `edn` ports the data-only `readEDN`, `ednJSON`, and `importRoamEDN` (cardinality, ordering, user references, aliases, empty UIDs, detached-block recovery), and `js_json` parses JSON into insertion-ordered objects and writes `JSON.stringify` output (array-index keys first, JavaScript numbers). Markdown references and blocks come from the caller through `RoamMarkdown`, and identifiers from a caller's generator. `tests/reference/core/roam.ts`, `roam-edn.ts`, and `edn.ts` are frozen (only `roam.ts`'s import path changes, to `roam-markdown.ts`, which records parses and counts identifiers); `tests/reference/roam-import.test.ts` and `roam-edn.test.ts` rerun rt's own tests through the frozen code; hashes are under `roam_extractions` in `tests/reference/UPSTREAM.json`. `bun tools/roam-vectors.ts` writes 62 JSON imports, 51 EDN imports, 41 EDN reads, and 67 exports (rt's test inputs, malformed graphs, depth limits, fences and list markers, titles, attribute order, numbers, escapes, replacement patterns, invalid databases, recovery, 30 seeded graphs and 20 seeded databases, and round trips with and without edits); `crates/quire_core/tests/roam.rs` matches every document, report line, metadata byte, and export byte, and checks that `reference_texts` lists exactly the texts the import parses. (2) Store, commit `594ff4555c0c`: `quire_store::roam_files` reads an export (100 MB cap, strict UTF-8, `.edn` by extension), publishes an import as `<parent>/Roam import <stamp>-<8 hex>` by writing each note with `create_new`, `.quire/roam.json`, `.quire/source.<ext>` (the exact bytes), and `.quire/import-report.txt` into `.<name>.partial`, flushing, and renaming, removing the staging folder on failure or Cancel; reads metadata back (absent is fine, unreadable refuses); and writes an export exclusively with its `.report.txt`, refusing a destination inside the notebook, its state folder, or Quire's data folder. (3) App, commit `ef6535254db1`: `crate::exchange::roam` runs the conversion off the UI thread, parsing references and blocks with the editor's tree-sitter Markdown 256 texts per UI trip (CRLF offsets mapped back, list continuation lines de-indented as `blockText` does), the export in two passes so only the block texts it rewrites are parsed. Import Roam data… and Export Roam JSON… sit beside the Markdown buttons and reuse the NR23 status line with progress, Cancel until the rename (import) or the write (export), and Show file. The imported notebook opens in a new window through `FolderOptions` on the Pages browser, with `report[1..]` in its notices; export report lines after the first join the notebook's notices, as rt pushes them into `notebook.warnings`.

Tests: `quire_core` `tests/roam.rs` (4 vector suites and a 2,000-page, 50,000-block round trip), `js_json` and naming unit tests; `quire_store::roam_files` (5: publication with exact files, a failing write and a Cancel leaving the parent unchanged, an invalid folder name, source limits, exclusive export and report, metadata reads); `exchange::roam` offset mapping; and `editor_host/stored/roam_tests.rs` (6 GPUI tests): rt's roam-files JSON case end to end (exact note, metadata, archive, report, notices, the Pages browser, export byte-equal to the original, `already exists` and `outside live notebooks` refusals), rt's EDN archive case, malformed JSON, EDN, and duplicate IDs plus a Cancel during reference parsing leaving the parent empty, rt's native-hierarchy export from a Quire notebook (exact JSON with generated IDs masked and the attachment notice), unsaved recovery and unreadable metadata refusals, and rt's remapped-ID case with a CRLF block through the native parser both ways. Measured on this Mac: the 5.3 MB, 50,000-block graph imports in 73 ms and exports unchanged in 100 ms in release (564 ms and 661 ms in debug), with a 170 MB peak resident size for the whole round trip test.

Deviations, chosen deliberately. Quire has no in-place notebook switch, so the imported notebook opens in a new window and the status in the importing window reads rt's "... Details are available in notebook notices." with Show file revealing the folder. Without a chosen page, a folder notebook holding `.quire/roam.json` opens on Pages, as rt's `origin: "roam"` does; quire has no Files-panel toggle, so rt's collapsed Files panel has no counterpart. Path prompts cannot filter by extension, so any file is accepted and anything not `.edn` is read as JSON, refused with rt's message when it is not. The vectors' `references` adapter returns a copy: rt's `roam.ts` reverses the parser's cached array in place, so a second rewrite of an identical text in rt walks references forward and can splice at shifted offsets; the port always walks them in reverse. Lone UTF-16 surrogates from EDN `\u` escapes or JSON become U+FFFD (JSON with one is refused by the JSON reader), and JSON nesting deeper than 320 levels is refused up front with the nesting message, since rt's 128 block levels need about 260. The export orders a folder notebook's pages by `localeCompare` (ICU collation) and a Quire notebook's by UTF-16 code units, as rt's two snapshots do. rt has no Roam EDN export and no single-page Roam export, and imports Roam only into a new folder notebook; quire matches that rather than inventing an EDN writer or an import into the open notebook.

Gates: core/store/format/media and app Clippy with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, the `tests/reference` Vitest run (130 tests, 27 of them rt's Roam tests on the frozen code), `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts /Users/umer/code/zed` (3,006 files verified, no vendored change), `bun tools/roam-vectors.ts` reproducing the recorded hash, and the full `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store -p quire_format -p quire_media --locked --features quire/test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` run twice (677 tests each, no failures). No new crates: `quire_core` enables `serde_json`'s `unbounded_depth` (depth is capped before parsing) and `float_roundtrip` (JavaScript's exact number parsing) features.

Batch E on 2026-10-31, `d1cba1c87d80`: a folder that holds `.quire/roam.json` and no notes opens on Pages with `Untitled.md` as its page, created only when saved, as rt shows an empty imported vault (`roam_tests::an_import_with_no_pages_opens_on_pages_with_a_note_to_write`).


Streaming conversion on 2026-10-31 (batch H), `9749ba9911fe`. `js_json::each_element` reads a top-level JSON array one element at a time through serde's `SeqAccess` (same insertion-ordered values, depth check, lone-surrogate refusal, and error precedence as `parse`: after a callback error the rest is still checked, so a later syntax error wins). `roam::RoamImport` splits `importRoamJSON` into `scan` (titles, duplicate checks, UID registration, one queued local ID per block) and `convert` (normalize, render, metadata page), and `import_roam_json` now runs on it, so the 62 JSON and 51 EDN vectors exercise the streamed engine; `streamed_roam_json_imports_match_the_whole_graph_import` also runs every JSON vector through two `each_element` passes with metadata written entry by entry and matches documents, report, and metadata bytes. `roam_files::ImportStaging` writes each page and its `.quire/roam.json` entry into the staging folder as it is converted, then the archive and report, and publishes in one rename; dropping it unpublished removes the staging folder. The app scans before reference parsing (page errors now appear before that step) and converts and writes in one pass with "wrote N of M pages" progress. EDN pages are converted and written one at a time after the database rebuilds. Measured with `cargo run -p quire_store --example roam_memory` (debug build, macOS, generated graphs only; `generate` writes them, `import` runs the streamed path, `import-whole` the earlier one): a 99.6 MB JSON export (3,539 pages, 566,240 blocks) peaked at 1,150 MB resident before the change (10.3 s) and 289 MB after (14.4 s, two parses); a 99.6 MB EDN database (2,670 pages, 427,200 blocks) peaks at 1,288 MB the earlier way and 1,203 MB streamed, because `read_edn`'s parse tree, the entities, and the rebuilt pages are all needed before the first page exists. Moving datom values out of the parse tree instead of cloning them measured no gain (1,214 MB) and was not kept.
Remaining: private-copy verification against a real Roam graph (counts and timings only), which needs the owner's export; EDN memory below the rebuilt database: a datom-streaming EDN reader that feeds entities without keeping the parse tree would cut the largest part, but the entities and pages are needed whole before any page exists, so an EDN import stays bounded only by the cap (about 1.2 GB resident at 100 MB).
Status: in_progress

### NR25: Port history, relocation, and recovery workflows

What to do: Add history browsing/restore-as-new-page, stale-safe conflict resolution, foreign drafts, and journaled page/subtree moves with link updates.
Constraints: Preserve history and identities, including case-only renames and collision handling. Do not promise cross-file undo that the source lacks.
Write set: history/conflict/move commands and views, interruption fixtures.
Dependencies: NR09, NR13, NR15, NR16.
Verify: Local multi-page atomicity, kill/restart at move steps, external changes, competing heads, save-copy IDs, and restore history without overwriting the current page.
Proof notes: Delete (NR25.1), rename (NR25.2), move and folder rename (NR25.3), page history and restore (NR25.4), and stuck-journal resolution (NR25.5) landed in both backends. Gates after NR25.3 to NR25.5: core and store Clippy and app Clippy with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, the `tests/reference` Vitest run (100 tests), `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts` (no vendored change), and the full `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store -p quire_format` command run twice (586 tests each). Remaining for NR25 as a whole: stale-safe conflict resolution beyond the folder banner and the Quire-notebook merge path (NR11.4); pointer menus for these commands landed under NR16.2. Foreign drafts landed on 2026-10-31 (`6d1d48218e54`, see NR13).
Status: in_progress

#### NR25.1: Delete pages and folders

What to do: Offer Delete for a page or folder with a dialog naming what goes, pages and other files, and how many links will stop resolving. Folder notebooks move the files to the system Trash. Quire notebooks record tombstones so every computer removes the page while its history stays, and forget the deleted folder and the empty folders inside it. Close tabs, favorites, and recent entries for deleted pages.
Constraints: All or nothing: refuse with an actionable message when a page changed after the choice or a page appeared in the folder. No in-app restore, and no promise of history by path. Automated tests never fill the real Trash.
Verify: Port the `vault` deletion cases, the `engine` case that deletes pages and folders on every computer including the peer's sync, and the `file-actions` dialog and link-count cases.
Proof notes: Source: `rt` `957da33d4766`; this replaces the earlier "no general delete UI" limitation. Quire notebooks are done; folder notebooks wait for NR13. Commits `ca914adcaba2` (store) and `fd412e660b93` (app). `NotebookStore::delete_if_heads` writes every tombstone or none: it refuses with `HeadsChanged` when a base moved and with `Appeared { path }` when a live page sits inside the folder but was not chosen, and accepts only deletions inside the folder. `crates/quire_store/tests/deletion.rs` ports the `engine` case "deletes pages and folders on every computer, refusing a stale deletion": office and home replicas, a stale base, a page that appeared in a nested folder, tombstones keeping text and history, and publish plus rescan on the peer. `QueryIndex::deletion` counts links from pages that stay, matching the `file-actions` link count. The app routes deletions through `page_writes::delete`, so an open page's unsaved typing is saved into its tombstone; deleted open pages are retired (read-only, never saved again) and their index overlays released. Entry points are the `DeletePage` action ("Delete page…" in the page header) and `DeleteFile` on the Files tree (`delete`, Command-Backspace or Ctrl-Backspace), opening a Zed `ui::Modal` with Enter and Escape. `crates/quire/src/editor_host/stored/file_tests.rs` covers deleting the shown page with 3 breaking links (tabs, favorites, recents, history and overlays cleaned, nothing revived by later typing), a folder refused for `Late.md` appearing after the choice and then deleted with its expanded folders forgotten, and a stale page refusing the whole deletion. Deviations: quire keeps no empty-folder inventory, so "forget the folder" removes it and its descendants from the workspace's expanded folders, and the tree, derived from page paths, drops it. Gates: core and store clippy, app clippy, `cargo fmt --check`, the vitest reference suite (92 tests), and the full test command run twice (450 tests each). Folder notebooks, commit `bd1d2426c5d3` (NR13 slice 8): the same dialog and `page_writes::delete` call `FolderClient::delete_if_heads`, which moves the note, or the whole folder with its other files, to the system Trash through Zed's `fs::RealFs::trash`; the detail reads "The files go to the system Trash, where you can restore them." and a folder heading counts other files. The two `vault.test.ts` deletion cases are ported in `folder_notebook::rt_tests`, and `folder_tests.rs` covers a page and a folder end to end with a stand-in Trash. The up-front `FOLDER_DELETION` refusal is gone. Remaining: pointer menus (NR16.2).
Status: done

#### NR25.2: Rename a page with its title

What to do: Add Rename beside Move. Rename changes the file name and the page title together, rewrites every link to follow, and updates a front matter `title`; Move keeps preserving the title.
Constraints: Reuse the journaled relocation path and its preview; keep IDs and history.
Verify: Port the rename cases added to `rt/tests/relocate.test.ts` and the in-place rename e2e scenario.
Proof notes: Source: `rt` `35e673677291` (`planRelocation` with `rename`). Commits `b66c64c4785e` (core port) and `9ab95e340e16` (app). `tests/reference/core/relocate.ts` and `file-paths.ts` freeze the reference with hashes in `tests/reference/UPSTREAM.json`; `tests/reference/relocate.test.ts` reruns the rename, move-keeps-title, collision, and traversal cases from `rt/tests/relocate.test.ts`, and `bun tools/relocate-vectors.ts` writes 175 deterministic vectors that `crates/quire_core/tests/relocate.rs` matches exactly through `quire_core::relocate`. `page_index::tests::native_links_match_the_relocation_vectors` checks that the editor's references and Markdown URL ranges match the reference parse on every vector page. The app's `RenamePage` action ("Rename…" in the page header) and F2 on a Files page open a dialog; `file_actions::plan_rename` reads the page and the index's link candidates from open buffers or stored heads, and `page_writes::apply` writes all of them or none, after which open pages take their new path and text while keeping unsaved typing. `file_tests.rs` covers a rename with links in open and closed pages (CRLF and code spans kept, a percent-encoded Markdown link followed, front matter `title` updated, typing kept, later saves staying at the new path) and refusals: a taken name ignoring case, a slash, an invalid file name, a case-only change, and a page saved elsewhere after planning (`STALE_RENAME`). A full-suite run under load showed the page list keeping the old title: an open page's overlay can carry the new path before the merged text with the new title, and the list stopped following at the path. It now follows until both the path and the planned title show (`a_rename_is_followed_until_the_page_list_shows_its_new_title`, which fails when the title check is removed). The same runs exposed two older races, in the tag rename and task move tests, which asserted on an open buffer before its asynchronous merge; both now wait for it. Deviations: F2 opens a dialog instead of renaming in place, and the rename applies without a preview, as rt's in-place rename does. Candidates come from the index (backlink sources plus pages whose indexed blocks contain `.md`) rather than a scan of every page, which covers everything the reference rewrites: rt skips front matter when extracting both references (`references` excludes `0..frontmatterLength`) and Markdown URLs (parser nodes ending inside front matter are skipped), and every other place a link can sit is an indexed block. Replacements are literal where JavaScript would expand `$&`, and fragment-only URLs stay unchanged. Folder notebooks, commit `36520bd4531e` (NR13 slice 9): the same plan writes through the folder journal (rt's `commitEdits` and `resumeTransactions`), creating the new file without replacing anything, archiving the old one with its history, and finishing an interrupted rename on the next open; the window moves tabs, favorites, recents, and history to the renamed page's new path-derived identity. `folder_tests.rs` checks the written text equals the Quire-notebook fixture's, and `folder_notebook::rt_tests` interrupts before each step. The up-front `FOLDER_RENAME` refusal is gone. Remaining: Move and its link-changes preview (NR25), folder rename, and the in-place rename e2e scenario and pointer menus (NR16.2). Move and folder rename landed under NR25.3. The in-place rename e2e scenario (`rt/tests/e2e/editor.spec.ts` "renames a page in place from the Files menu and its links follow") is ported as `folder_tests::renaming_a_folder_page_in_place_from_files_follows_links_and_escape_keeps_it`: F2 on the Files row opens the name field holding "Old name", the file and the linking page are rewritten byte for byte, Files lists the new name, and Escape on a second F2 leaves it. The right-click Files menu it starts from waits for pointer menus (NR16.2).
Status: done

#### NR25.3: Move a page or a folder with a link-change preview

What to do: Offer Move for a page and rename or move for a folder, in both backends, with the reference link-change preview before anything is written.
Constraints: Reuse `quire_core::relocate` so every following link is rewritten; all or nothing; keep IDs and history; refuse a page changed after the preview.
Verify: Folder-move vectors against the frozen `planRelocation`, store and journal interruption at every step, and GPUI tests with exact text, previews, and stale refusals.
Proof notes: Source: `rt` 0.9.57 `5ea5c22` `src/ui/FileActions.tsx` (`planRelocation` preview: "N other pages need link updates", the page list, and "Review N link changes" twenty at a time). Commits `0d65605b83ab` (core) and `d8fb0dbfb6a2` (app). The reference has no folder rename, so Quire defines a folder move as the reference moving each page of the folder in turn, in path order, on the notebook the previous move left, except that pages keep their file names and so get no recorded title. `quire_core::relocate::relocate_pages` does every move in one pass (`relocate_page` is now its one-move case) and `folder_relocations` plans the moves, refusing the same folder in any case (`FOLDER_UNCHANGED`), a folder inside itself, an empty folder, and a target page taken outside the folder. `bun tools/relocate-vectors.ts` also writes `tests/fixtures/folder-relocate-vectors.json`: 68 vectors (fixed cases with relative links, embeds of other files, duplicate titles, a `Projects2` prefix trap, nested folders, CRLF, a taken target, traversal, and reserved names, plus 60 seeded mixtures of every link form from inside and outside the folder) composed from the frozen code with the recorded title taken out, and `crates/quire_core/tests/relocate.rs` matches each exactly; the 175 single-page vectors still pass through the generalized code. In the app, `file_actions::plan_relocation` plans `Relocate::{Rename, Move, Folder}` alike: candidates are the union of each moved page's index candidates, read from open buffers or stored heads; `RelocationPlan` carries the preview (heading, detail, updated pages, link updates sorted by page) and writes through `page_writes::apply` with `STALE_MOVE`. The dialog (`navigation/file_dialog.rs`, `Mode::Move`) previews on the first Enter, applies on the second, drops the preview when the path is edited or when a write is refused, and shows link changes with Show more link changes. Entry points: Move… in the page header (`MovePage`), Shift-F2 on a Files item (`MoveFile`), and F2 on a Files folder. A regression found while testing: a page saved by another writer and not yet analyzed by the index had no links or blocks to match, so planning skipped it; `QueryIndex::relocation` now also returns unanalyzed pages (`index::relocation_candidates_include_pages_not_analyzed_yet`). Tests: `move_tests.rs` (a move with links in open and closed pages, exact preview text and link list, nothing written by a preview, editing dropping it, typing kept, later saves at the new path; TAKEN, INVALID_PATH, and CASE_ONLY refusals and a stale preview refused then previewed again; a folder renamed from Files with exact preview, identities kept, titles kept without front matter, Files following, and a folder inside itself refused), `folder_tests.rs` (a folder moved in a folder notebook with the journal, two archived originals, an image left in place and linked from the new place, and tabs following the new identity), and `rt_tests::an_interrupted_folder_move_finishes_on_the_next_open_from_every_step` (all five journal steps). Deviations: the reference lists link updates by page in notebook order; Quire sorts them by page path. Folder moves keep other files in place rather than moving attachments with the folder. Expanded-folder state is not carried to the new folder name.
Status: done

#### NR25.4: Browse page history and restore a version

What to do: List a page's saved versions with times, including history imported from the reference app, show a version, and restore it without losing anything.
Constraints: Restore never overwrites unseen text: unsaved typing is saved first and stays in history, and a page changed after the comparison is refused. Keep the reference restore-as-new-page.
Verify: Store listing in both backends and GPUI tests for listing, diff, restore over unsaved typing, stale refusal, and the copy.
Proof notes: Source: `rt` 0.9.57 `src/ui/PageHistory.tsx`, `tests/page-history.test.tsx`, `engine.ts` (`revisions`, `revisionText`), and `store.ts` (`restoreRevisionCopy`, `createSavedCopy`). Commit `baa975e418e2`. `NotebookStore::history` lists every stored revision of a page except its current heads, newest first by creation time, as the reference engine's `revisions` does, so revisions imported from other computers or the reference app appear with their own times; `version_text` refuses a revision of another page with `VERSION_UNAVAILABLE`. `FolderNotebook::history` maps the existing history rows (including imported rt history) to the same `SavedVersion` shape, and `NotebookBackend` dispatches both. `navigation/page_history.rs` is a modal: the versions as buttons labelled with date and 24-hour time in the notebook's time zone (Up and Down move), and a read-only Zed `Editor` over a `MultiBuffer` holding the version with a `buffer_diff::BufferDiff` whose base is the current page text (the live buffer when open), hunks expanded, so restoring's effect shows as Zed diff hunks. Restore (Enter) calls `file_actions::restore_version`, which refuses with `STALE_RESTORE` when the page no longer reads the compared text and otherwise writes through `page_writes::apply` (open pages save their typing first); a refusal compares again. Restore as a new page (Command-Enter, Ctrl-Enter elsewhere) uses `file_actions::page_copy`, now shared with Save my version as a copy, and opens the copy. Tests: `quire_store/tests/history.rs` (both backends: order, heads excluded, texts, unavailable versions), `history_tests.rs` (exact labels, diff display and one hunk, read-only editor, Up and Down, restore over unsaved typing kept in history, a later save on top; a refusal after typing reached the page, the comparison redone, and a copy with fresh block IDs leaving the page; an empty history), and `folder_tests::folder_page_history_restores_an_earlier_file_and_keeps_the_replaced_one`. Deviations: rt offers only Restore as a new page; Quire adds restoring in place because the replaced version stays in history and the restore is checked. Restoring is not an undo step, as merges are not. The list is not virtualized; history retention bounds it in folder notebooks but not in Quire notebooks.
Status: done

#### NR25.5: Resolve a stuck folder journal in the app

What to do: Replace the notice that only names a stuck journal file with a way to finish or abandon the change.
Constraints: Never overwrite a note changed after the change was planned; keep every planned version.
Verify: Store tests for state reporting, retry, and set aside; GPUI tests for both actions.
Proof notes: Commit `533c59b895eb`. `FolderNotebook::stuck_changes` lists each journal still waiting after open with every step's state (`Done`, `Waiting`, `ChangedElsewhere`, `Unreadable`) read without side effects, and `StuckChange::summary` names the notes by state. `retry_change` applies the journal again with the same idempotent, revision-checked steps as resuming, and `set_aside_change` moves it to `transactions/set-aside`, which resuming skips. Journal names with paths are refused. The folder notices list each stuck change with Retry and Set aside; either rereads the folder and adds a notice, and a failed retry says to put the note back or set the change aside. Tests: `rt_tests` (states and summary for a rename stuck on a changed linking note, retry refused without overwriting, invalid names, retry after the note is put back reaching the full rename; set aside keeping the journal bytes and every note, and not resumed on reopen) and `folder_tests` (both actions through the window). rt's own interrupted journals in `.quire/devices/<device>/transactions` are taken in once on open since `a7d4c3857e6f` (2026-10-31, see NR13), so they resume, or are listed with Retry and Set aside, like Quire's own.
Status: done

### NR26: Implement verified backup and restore

What to do: Add consistent staged snapshots, manifests/hashes, scheduling/listing, and restore into a new notebook identity. Include recovery, hidden metadata, archives, attachments, and folder journals.
Constraints: No raw copy of a live SQLite file. Preserve unpublished work or fail explicitly; never claim a partial snapshot completed. Restored recovery drafts carry an unknown base and are offered for review, never merged automatically (`rt` `71b62cba83e3`).
Write set: backup/restore service, settings UI, snapshot fixtures.
Dependencies: NR23, NR24, NR25.
Verify: Restore both backends into fresh roots; compare content, heads/history, drafts, archives, attachments, and query results; inject damage/full-disk/interruption and retain older completed backups.
Proof notes: Three slices (`7d728486ce85` store backups and verification, `e64d06c2b871` restore, `ca8dd35ba0b2` window commands). Source: `rt` 0.9.57 `5ea5c22310ff` `src/desktop/backups.ts`, `engine/backup.ts`, `main.ts` (hourly timer, 15 minute retry), `ui/DataSettings.tsx`, `tests/backups.test.ts`.

`quire_store::backup` writes `<destination>/Quire Native/<sha256 of kind and canonical path>/<UTC time>-<8 hex>/`. A Quire notebook backup holds `local.sqlite` and `attachments/<name>` for every row of the snapshot's attachment catalog, each checked against the catalog hash; a folder notebook backup holds `notebook/` (every file but `.git`; `.quire/**/*.sqlite` snapshotted, their `-wal`/`-shm`/`-journal` left out) and `state/` (`folder.sqlite` snapshotted, journals and archive copied; dot entries, `query-index.sqlite`, and `quire-workspace.json` left out as derived). Every database is copied by attaching it read-only (`file:...?mode=ro`) to an in-memory connection and running `VACUUM backup INTO`, then switching the copy to rollback journaling, syncing it, and running `integrity_check`; no live database file is copied raw. Other files are copied with streaming SHA-256 and `sync_all`. A folder notebook is listed and hashed again after copying, and any difference fails the backup ("The notebook changed while it was being backed up..."). `manifest.json` (format `quire-native-backup`, version 1) records kind, time, source name, notebook identity, every file's hash and size, and every database's per-table row counts. The backup lives in `.partial-<id>` until a full `verify` from the drive passes, then is renamed and its parent synced; any failure removes the partial folder. `list` returns only folders with a valid manifest of the right kind, newest first. `verify` is read-only and fails on a missing, extra, or changed file, a failed integrity check, different row counts, a wrong application ID, schema, or identity, a head, parent, or outbox entry naming a missing revision, an undecodable revision (through `read_revision` on the attached schema) or draft, or an attachment catalog that differs from the files.

`restore` verifies first, refuses a parent overlapping the backup or the open notebook, backs up the open notebook (the restore stops if that fails), then copies into `.restoring-<id>` beside the target and reports `SafetyBackup`, `Copied`, `Adopted`, `Verified`, `Swapped`. A Quire notebook copy is opened once with its old identity (migrating an older schema), then `notebook::adopt_restored` gives it a new UUID notebook identity, keeps the writer, clears cursors, batch hashes, a pending batch, and observed revisions, refills the outbox with every revision in generation order, and clears each draft's base. It must then open with the new identity, keep the revision, parent, head, draft, and attachment counts of the manifest, and re-hash its attachments before the rename to `Restored <time>-<id>`. A folder notebook's state goes to a hidden folder beside `state_for(target)`, its `meta.root` is pointed at the target, drafts lose their base, and draft, history, and content counts are checked; both folders are renamed into place and `FolderNotebook::open` must succeed. Any error removes only what this restore created, with "The restore did not finish and the current notebook is unchanged."

The window (`crates/quire/src/backups.rs`, `editor_host/stored/backups.rs`) adds Backups… and the `quire_backup` actions. The schedule checks every minute on the executor clock: back up when the last completed backup is an hour old (counted from the newest listed backup at open) and the last attempt is 15 minutes old. Restore asks "Restore the backup from <time> as a new notebook?" with the detail that Quire backs up this notebook first and leaves it unchanged, then a parent folder, then opens the restored notebook in a new window at the shown page and closes the old window only if its pages are secured. Folder notebooks restore with `state_folder(root)`; `FolderOptions::open` now calls the extracted `open_folder_window`.

Data-safety guarantees and the tests that prove them, all in `quire_store::backup::tests` unless named: consistent snapshots while another thread writes, with every head present and the outbox between the counts before and after (`backups_of_an_open_store_stay_consistent_while_another_thread_writes`, three backups during at least five concurrent saves); a live WAL `.quire` database snapshotted with its uncheckpointed row and no `-wal` copied (`folder_backups_copy_notes_state_and_live_reference_databases_consistently`); a folder edited during backup by atomic renames, as Quire and rt save, either backs up a whole version or fails with nothing partial left (`folder_backups_fail_rather_than_mix_versions_when_a_note_changes_meanwhile`; a file another application rewrites in place can be caught half written if it holds still across both reads, which no copying backup can detect, and an early version of this test that wrote in place failed this way); verification catches a flipped attachment byte, a removed attachment, a flipped database byte, an extra file, a missing manifest, a changed count, and a damaged revision whose manifest hash was rewritten to match, and changes no byte (`verification_reports_...`, `a_backup_holds_every_cataloged_attachment_and_verifying_changes_nothing`); a failed backup keeps both earlier backups and lists neither `.partial-crashed` nor a folder without a manifest (`failed_backups_keep_every_completed_backup_and_never_list_partial_copies`); destinations or restore parents inside the notebook are refused (`backup_folders_inside_the_notebook_are_refused`, `a_damaged_backup_is_refused_before_the_safety_backup_or_any_copy`); a restored Quire notebook has a new identity the old one cannot open, equal heads, history, and head records, all 3 revisions queued, both drafts with empty bases, byte-identical attachments including one no page links to, and the original stays writable (`a_restored_store_is_a_new_notebook_with_the_same_pages_history_drafts_and_attachments`); a restore stopped after each of the five steps leaves the parent empty, the original's files byte-identical, its draft intact, every backup verifiable, and the original writable (`a_restore_interrupted_at_any_step_leaves_the_original_and_creates_nothing`; folder counterpart `a_folder_restore_interrupted_at_any_step_leaves_no_folder_or_state_behind`); a restored folder notebook keeps notes, attachments, history, and the draft for review, without `.git` (`a_restored_folder_notebook_keeps_notes_history_and_drafts_for_review_in_a_new_folder`). GPUI tests in `stored/backups_tests.rs`: Back up now without a folder, an overlapping folder refused, a verified backup listed and verified, a damaged one failing Verify; the hourly schedule and the 15 minute retry after an unplugged folder; restore Cancel doing nothing, then restore backing up first, opening the restored text in a new window and closing the old one while the original keeps its later edit; a damaged backup refused with no safety backup, no files, and the window kept.

The rt compatibility decision, a one-way door declined. Quire Native backups are not readable by `rt`, and Quire does not write `rt`'s format. `rt`'s Quire notebook backup is its synced change-log folder plus its own `local.sqlite` schema, and its folder backup carries `recovery.sqlite` in `rt`'s durability schema; matching either would freeze Quire's store and state schemas as a cross-app format. The separate `Quire Native` folder keeps the two apps from listing each other's backups even when they share a destination (`rt` lists only `Quire/<profile>/<64 hex>/`). Reading `rt` backups in Quire is a two-way door left for NR36 migration.

Commands: `cargo clippy` for the four library crates and for `quire` with `--features test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders`, both with `-D warnings`, pass; `cargo fmt --check` passes; the full `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store -p quire_format -p quire_media --locked --features quire/test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` passed twice (674 tests each); the `tests/reference` Vitest run passed 103; `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts /Users/umer/code/zed` verified 3,006 files (no vendored change). One new dependency edge: `quire_store` uses the workspace `uuid` with `v4`, already locked for `quire`.

Deviations from `rt`. Quire restores Quire notebooks by rewriting the database rather than re-encoding each device's log; all history is republished from this writer. A safety backup precedes every restore (`rt` has none). Since NR33 the backup folder and the last outcome belong to the profile (rt keeps them in `profiles.sqlite`), and closing or quitting waits for a running backup, offering Close anyway after three seconds. `backup-folders.json` from earlier builds is no longer read; no released build wrote it.

Skipping unchanged hourly backups (decision: a two-way door; no backup is ever deleted or pruned). Without pruning, an open but idle notebook gained 24 full copies a day. The rule: `backup::fingerprint` is the SHA-256 of one sorted line per file a backup would read, holding its path, size, modification time, and on Unix its change time and inode, without reading contents. A Quire notebook contributes `local.sqlite*` beside the database (the `-wal` and `-journal` included, since commits land there before a checkpoint) and every file under `attachments/`; a folder notebook contributes every notebook file but `.git`, and every state file but the derived ones (`skip_state` minus journals, now `derived_state`). SQLite `-shm` files are left out because readers write to them too. Change time is the safety margin: a save, an in-place rewrite at the same size, a rename into place, or a sync client restoring an older modification time all set a new change time that tools cannot set back. `create` stores the fingerprint taken before staging in the manifest as an optional `fingerprint` field (absent in older backups, which therefore never match); `create_if_changed` returns None only when the fingerprint was taken, equals the newest listed backup's, and every file that backup's manifest lists is still an ordinary file of its recorded size. Any doubt (no fingerprint, a stat error, an older backup, a missing or resized file) backs up. The window's hourly tick calls `create_if_changed` and, when skipped, counts the hour from the check and says "Nothing changed since the last backup, so the hourly backup was skipped. Back up now makes one anyway."; Back up now and the safety backup before a restore call `create` and always back up. Alternatives rejected: row counts or the maximum revision rowid alone miss in-place draft updates, which are the unpublished work backups exist for; hashing every file or database page would cost about as much as the backup; an in-memory fingerprint would redo a backup after every launch. Older Quire builds reject a manifest with the new field (`deny_unknown_fields`); no released build exists, so the format version stays 1. Tests: `scheduled_store_backups_skip_an_unchanged_notebook_and_follow_saves_and_attachments` (unchanged skips, a save, an attachment, and a draft each back up, `create` always backs up, and a newest backup missing a file is not trusted), `scheduled_folder_backups_follow_external_edits_new_files_and_state_changes` (an in-place same-size external rewrite, a new note, and a recovery draft back up; a `.git` change does not), GPUI `the_hourly_backup_skips_an_unchanged_notebook_but_back_up_now_does_not` (hourly ticks with no change, after one edit, after an attachment, then Back up now), `folder_tests::the_hourly_backup_of_a_folder_notebook_waits_for_a_change_such_as_an_external_edit`, and the hourly schedule test, which now edits between expected backups.

Batch D (2026-10-31, `f8edd2f68d26`, `95ba0d61b268`, `90dc49207268`). Backup writes go through `write_within_space`, which names a full drive with what to do; a test seam (`with_free_space`) fills the drive at every write of a backup and a restore, and `backup/tests/full_disk.rs` proves a full drive fails a store backup with nothing partial listed, a store restore creates nothing and keeps every backup, and a folder backup and restore leave nothing behind. `backup/tests/queries.rs` proves a restored notebook's query index answers as the original's. A restore now writes the window's tabs and favorites for the restored notebook first (`carry_workspace`), and `backups_tests::a_folder_notebook_backs_up_and_restores_through_the_window_with_its_tabs` runs both through the window. A restored notebook becomes the notebook of the profile that held the original, keeping no synced folder (`data_tests::a_restored_notebook_becomes_the_notebook_of_the_profile_that_held_the_original`); Settings > Data and File > Backups… and Back Up Now reach the commands. rt backups restore from the Move from rt window (NR36).

Remaining: native picker and packaged-app acceptance; real external and network drives (the full drive is simulated).
Status: done

### NR27: Verify provider transport and Personal Git

What to do: Port the finished Git algorithm and provider handling, retaining per-profile restrictions and credentials through Git. Measure a synthetic year of repository growth.
Constraints: No force-push, unexpected remote changes, or claim that folder publication confirms delivery. Preserve logs after failed pushes and do not assume immutable filenames alone prevent every Git conflict.
Write set: provider/Git adapters, transport acceptance scripts, status view.
Dependencies: NR09, NR26.
Verify: Two-device OneDrive/Dropbox/Git handover, placeholders, conflict copies, divergent commits, failed upload/push, copied identity, and excluded transcripts/live databases. Record actual provider results separately from simulations.
Proof notes: Four code slices (`4bf786f1d479` provider detection, `8071d9ab5ffc` Git sync, `56b96eeaa355` growth measurement, `52fba14e744e` and `5832edf46a52` the Sync view). Source: `rt` 0.9.57 `5ea5c22310ff` `src/desktop/git-sync.ts`, `profiles.ts`, `main.ts` (`data:git-remote`, `data:git-sync`, `protectNotebookGit` at open), `sync/format.ts` (strict names), `ui/SyncStatus.tsx`, `ui/DataSettings.tsx`, `ui/help-content.ts`, and `tests/git-sync.test.ts`.

Simulated and real results are separate. Every result below is a simulation on one Mac (macOS, APFS, Git 2.55.0): two computers are two clones of a bare repository in a temporary folder, and providers are folder names plus placeholder files. No OneDrive, Dropbox, iCloud Drive, or Google Drive client ran, no second device was used, and no network remote was contacted. Real provider handover, real cloud-only placeholders (`SF_DATALESS` on macOS, Cloud Files attributes on Windows), real provider conflict copies, HTTPS or SSH remotes with credential helpers, and Windows or Linux remain **unverified**.

`quire_store::git_sync` ports `git-sync.ts`. `sync_folder` (rt `syncPersonal`) fetches, fast-forwards only when the remote moved and the folder is clean, refuses divergent histories ("Both versions are retained... Quire will not merge automatically") and a behind folder with local edits, refuses a remote on another branch, then commits everything and pushes `HEAD:refs/heads/<branch>` with `--porcelain` and never `--force`. `sync_quire` (rt `syncQuireGit`) commits first, merges the remote, aborts a conflicting merge with "...Someone edited the notebook folder outside Quire, or two computers share one device identity...", and pushes. `open` keeps rt's checks: Personal only, a configured remote, no remote that starts with `-`, holds control characters, `::`, user information in an HTTP(S) URL, or a scheme other than `https`, `ssh`, `file`; an unusable `.git` is never reinitialized; a folder inside a larger repository, tracked `.quire/devices` or `.quire/ai`, a different `origin`, and unresolved conflicts are refused; the local `info/exclude` gains `/.quire/devices/` and `/.quire/ai/` once. `protect_notebook` (rt `protectNotebookGit`) runs when a folder notebook opens and returns rt's untrack warning. `check_remote` ports `setGitRemote`. Git runs as a separate program, as rt and Zed's `GitBinary` do, with Zed's background rules: `credential.interactive=false`, `core.askPass=`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GIT_ASKPASS` and `SSH_ASKPASS` removed, `core.fsmonitor=false`, and SSH composed in batch mode from `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` or `core.sshCommand` (Zed's `compose_batch_mode_ssh_command`, plus rt's `ConnectTimeout=15`). Credential helpers stay enabled, so keychain and manager helpers answer without prompting; a missing credential fails. Inherited `GIT_DIR`-style overrides are removed and every command has rt's 60 second limit. Zed's `git` crate was not called directly: its `GitRepository` trait has no `merge --ff-only`, `merge-base --is-ancestor`, `check-ref-format`, or `ls-tree`, and `GitBinary` is crate-private and needs a GPUI executor, so the store would have needed a vendored fork for synchronous, testable calls. No vendored file changed.

`quire_store::provider` detects OneDrive, Dropbox, iCloud Drive, and Google Drive from the folder's real path (so `~/OneDrive` resolves to `Library/CloudStorage/OneDrive-Personal`), finds placeholders (iCloud's `.<name>.icloud` stubs; `SF_DATALESS` on macOS; offline, recall-on-open, and recall-on-data-access attributes on Windows) from metadata only, and finds conflict copies whose names only a provider makes: Dropbox's "conflicted copy", "(Case Conflict", and "(Selective Sync Conflict" and any non-strict copy of a format 1 change file or `quire-notebook.json`. OneDrive's `Note-COMPUTER.md`, iCloud's `Note 2.md`, and Google Drive's `Note (1).md` are names people also choose, so they are not reported for notes. rt has no placeholder or conflict-copy detection beyond strict change-file names and the Help advice to keep the notebook offline.

The Sync… view (`crates/quire/src/sync_status.rs`, wired in `editor_host/stored/sync.rs`; actions `quire_sync::ShowSync`, `SyncWithGit`, `CheckSyncFolder`) shows the provider with rt's "Saved locally confirms this computer's write" reminder, cloud-only notes and conflict copies with Check again, the Git exclusion warning (also shown without opening the view, as rt's notice), and Personal Git: the remote field, Save remote, Sync with Git, the last successful push ("A push puts the commits on the remote; another computer has them only after it syncs"), and the last Git error. Records live per notebook folder in `personal-git.json` in application data, as rt's `profiles.sqlite` holds `gitRemote`, `lastPushAt`, and `gitError`. A successful sync rereads changed notes into open pages through the folder watcher path. Help gained one step describing Sync.

Tests. `quire_store::provider::tests` (5): provider names and a link into a provider folder, placeholders and conflict copies (a stand-in predicate replaces `SF_DATALESS`, which only the provider's system extension can set), no placeholders in an ordinary folder, an unavailable folder's message. `quire_store::git_sync::tests` (4): remotes with credentials or unsupported transports refused and Work or Current refused; remote commands carry `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, batch SSH, no `credential.helper` override, and no force flag; ISO commit times; a missing Git says to install it. `quire_store/tests/git_sync.rs` (14, local bare remote, private Git configuration, no network): folder handover by fast-forward; local edits kept when the remote changed; divergent commits stop with both sides unchanged; a push rejected by a `pre-receive` hook keeps the commit, the next sync delivers it, and the old remote head stays an ancestor; an unreachable remote fails the fetch and changes nothing; Work and nested folders refused; a different origin or branch never changed; live databases and AI transcripts never committed or pushed, a second computer's own live data never touched by a fast-forward, and a remote that tracks live data refused; the exclude file written once with the tracked warning; an iCloud stub stopping the sync before any commit; repository creation and an unfinished merge refused; Quire notebooks merging two diverged histories and a hand-edited `quire-notebook.json` stopping with no `MERGE_HEAD`; a copied device identity whose two `000000002-*` files merge in Git without conflict, keep both files, and are refused by the rescan; a failed push keeping the published change file, the other computer still showing the old text, and the next sync delivering it. GPUI `stored::navigation::sync_tests` (5): a Quire notebook window says Git needs a folder; cloud-only notes and conflict copies listed until resolved; a credentialed remote refused, Cancel on the Personal question saving nothing, then sync pushing and a note another clone pushed reaching the open page; a failed push shown with its reason until a later sync; a tracked rt database reported and the exclude file written.

Growth, method. `cargo run --release -p quire_store --example git_growth -- 365` simulates 365 days from 2026-01-01 against a local bare remote with a private Git configuration. Home writes a 12-line daily page and adds 2 lines to each of two of 12 project pages every day; office, on weekdays, writes an 8-line meeting page and adds 3 lines to a project page. Each computer syncs at the start of its session (bringing in the other's work) and at the end; only the end sync is timed. The folder notebook syncs through `sync_folder`. The Quire notebook saves each added line as its own revision (autosave granularity), publishes once per session, and syncs through `sync_quire` after rescanning. Sizes are the bare remote's bytes on disk as pushed and after `git gc`. Times include machine load: other agents' builds held the load average between 8 and 19 during the run.

Growth, results (simulation, this Mac, 2026-10-31):

| Notebook | Commits | Tracked files | Notes on disk | Remote as pushed | After `git gc` | Sync median / p95 / max | Median by quarter |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
