| Markdown folder | 626 | 638 | 583 KiB | 2.55 MiB | 729 KiB | 391 / 1,362 / 6,823 ms | 377 / 408 / 415 / 363 ms |
| Quire (format 1) | 626 | 627 | 1,696 KiB | 2.84 MiB | 2.15 MiB | 360 / 606 / 4,175 ms | 349 / 353 / 368 / 381 ms |

The folder notebook's sync time did not grow over the year; its remote grows by loose objects until Git packs them, which `gc.auto` does in normal use (Quire does not disable it). The Quire notebook keeps every revision: each autosaved line is a full page text inside a zstd-compressed, immutable change file, so its folder is 2.9 times the Markdown notes, and `git gc` recovers 24% of its remote against 72% for Markdown because compressed files leave Git no deltas. Its median sync rose 9% from the first quarter to the fourth; publication took a median 42.4 ms (max 379 ms). For this workload a year adds about 2.2 MiB to a packed Quire remote and 0.7 MiB to a Markdown one. That heavier writers grow roughly with revisions saved is an assumption to check against a real notebook. The whole run took 1,206 s.

Commands: both Clippy gates with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, the full `cargo test -p quire -p quire_core -p quire_store -p quire_format -p quire_media --locked --features quire/test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders` run twice (769 tests each, none failed), the `tests/reference` Vitest run (130 tests), and `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts /Users/umer/code/zed` (3,004 files, no vendored change) pass. One new dependency edge: `quire` uses the workspace `serde` with `derive`, already locked.

Deviations from `rt`. Since NR33 Git sync belongs to the Personal profile, as in rt; the per-notebook confirmation and `personal-git.json` that stood in for profiles are gone (no released build wrote the file). The root is canonicalized instead of refused when it is reached through a link. Ignored files under `.quire/devices` and `.quire/ai` do not count as local edits when the remote does not track them: rt's `--ignored` status made every behind folder that rt itself had opened refuse to fast-forward; Quire refuses instead when the remote tracks those folders, which is what could overwrite live data. Cloud-only notes stop a sync before the commit, where rt would record them as deleted. Each sync backs up first when Personal has a backup folder and stops if that backup fails; rt refuses to sync at all without a backup folder, which Quire does not.

Remaining: real OneDrive, Dropbox, iCloud Drive, and Google Drive handover on two devices, real placeholders and provider conflict copies, HTTPS and SSH remotes with credential helpers, and Windows and Linux runs (all unverified); Git sync for Quire notebooks in the window landed with NR09.5 (`4906752b63ef`: back up the database, publish, `sync_quire`, import; temporary dot files never committed; tested with a bare remote and a second clone); OneDrive and iCloud conflict-copy names for notes, which need the provider's own record rather than a name guess; a schedule or sync-on-close (rt has neither; both sync on demand).
Status: in_progress

## AI and application completion

### NR28: Port CLI transforms and proposals

What to do: Implement backend detection/test, process streaming/cancellation, range transforms, proposal diff/review, prompt history, and native Vim/shortcut entry points.
Constraints: Proposals stay outside notebook storage until accepted. Preserve target versions, IDs, single-step undo, backend settings, and documented CLI isolation limits.
Write set: AI process host, proposal/prompt views, protocol fixtures.
Dependencies: NR14, NR15.
Verify: Fixture CLI processes cover streaming, malformed output, failure/cancel, backend switching, accepted/rejected proposals, stale ranges, and notebook switch. Test installed supported CLIs separately.
Proof notes: Commits `c3eed4395693` (protocol and commands in `quire_core::ai`), `22041081031f` (process host), `bf174bf3d08b` (settings and the Settings AI section), `2b739f45f079` (kill-on-drop), `b0ca14b42622` (proposals, prompt, entry points). `quire_core::ai::{commands,backend}` port rt's `ai-commands`/`ai-cli` pure code: verbs, `:ai` parsing, prompt building, marker restore, CLI arguments with rt's isolation flags (Claude `--restricted --strict-mcp-config --tools "" --max-turns 1 --no-session-persistence`; Codex `exec --json --sandbox read-only --ephemeral` with the quire MCP server disabled), and both stream parsers; `crates/quire_core/tests/ai.rs` replays rt's recorded fixtures (25 tests). `crates/quire/src/ai/process.rs` runs the CLI through Zed's `util::process::Child` (own process group; kill is `killpg` SIGKILL) with smol IO and timers: prompt on stdin, stderr drained concurrently, a fresh empty working folder per run, 120 s transform and 60 s Test limits, PATH from the login shell plus `~/.local/bin`, `/opt/homebrew/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`, and 0600 transcripts. Cancel, a dropped cancel handle, and a dropped run future all end the whole tree and remove the folder. Settings are Zed settings under `quire.ai` (vendored `QuireAiSettingsContent`, recorded in `UPSTREAM.json`); the tested backend+binary pair and 30-entry prompt history live in an `AiState` file in the data folder. `proposal.rs` shows a run in an editor block (Zed inline-assist style) that streams tokens, then a read-only `MultiBuffer` + `BufferDiff` editor with hunks expanded, as NR25's history view does; Accept applies one `transact` (`ViewEditIntent::Structural`) after `finalize_last_transaction`, so one undo restores the page; it restores dropped `^id` markers on unchanged lines, drops IDs taken elsewhere in the page or index, and refuses a target edited meanwhile. `prompt.rs` is an embedded Zed `Picker` with `select_history` recall. Entry points: Cmd-I/Ctrl-I, `\a` in Vim Normal and Visual (Visual rows recorded before leaving Visual), `:ai` through vendored `vim::host_command` (Vim's own range grammar, recorded in `UPSTREAM.json`), and Ask AI… in the page menu; Enter/Escape accept/reject outside Insert mode. Fixtures are `crates/quire/examples/fake_ai_cli.rs` (a Rust fake CLI driven by `FAKE_AI_*` variables) plus rt's jsonl streams; no test reaches a real service.

Tests: `ai::process` (12: Claude token order, Codex rules on stdin, malformed lines and drift message, failed exit and expired login, missing binary and search path, cancel kills child and grandchild, dropped cancel handle, dropped run removes folder, timeout with retry, early cancel, transcript, Test), `ai::settings` (3), `ai::proposal` (10: streaming leaves the page alone then Enter accepts and `u` undoes in one step; marker restore with Cmd-Z; Escape rejects and ends the tree; stale range refused; Continue inserts below dropping copied IDs; blank-line drafting; a newer command ends the earlier run; closing the window (notebook switch) ends a writing run; failures and Off; Codex protocol after switching), `ai::prompt` (7: target label and verbs, typed instruction runs and is remembered, verb row, Up/Down recall and draft, Escape returns focus without a request, Vim Visual lines, blank line Continue only and Off notice, page menu entry). Installed CLIs: `cargo test -p quire --features test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders -- --ignored installed_clis --nocapture` (ignored by default; version only, no prompt) found Claude Code 2.1.316 at `~/.local/bin/claude` and Codex 0.165.0 at `/opt/homebrew/bin/codex` on 2026-10-31. Gates: both clippy commands, `cargo fmt --check`, the full `cargo test` command twice (436 passed, 1 ignored in `quire`), `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts /Users/umer/code/zed`, and the vitest reference suite (130) pass.

Deviations from rt: transforms run in an empty temporary folder, not the notebook root; transcripts go to the notebook's Quire state folder, not into the notebook; returning to a previously tested backend+binary counts as tested, though commands still need turning on; the prompt does not open where no block is at the caret (rt opened with presets only); `:ai` without a range and the pane commands (ask, presets) give a notice, since the answer pane is NR30.
Remaining: the answer pane, presets, Ask, and Tidy (NR30); notebook tools, AI writes, and terminal registration (NR29); daily-page detection for `:ai` context is passed as false until NR30 needs it.
Status: done

### NR29: Port live notebook tools and terminal MCP

What to do: Implement compatible tool schemas, read queries, staged/apply write ledger, token-checked bridge, stdio server, and reversible terminal registration.
Constraints: Tools use the live notebook and overlay, never a second filesystem view. Keep Off/Review/Apply and notebook identity/version checks.
Write set: notebook tools/bridge/MCP mode, tool transcript fixtures.
Dependencies: NR12, NR18, NR28.
Verify: Existing tool/bridge/MCP tests translated to native processes; unsaved reads, stale writes, denied writes, concurrent tool calls, server closure, and calls from a terminal produce expected results.
Proof notes: Commits `da2bab13df43` (catalogue and MCP protocol in `quire_core::ai::{tools,mcp}`), `fdfbdf2619d8` (bridge, stdio server, read tools, AI writes setting), `571511d76293` (write tools, change ledger, AI changes window), `55eaa8765dd3` (Use from a terminal). `quire_core::ai::tools` holds rt's 14 tool specs, `validateInput` (UTF-16 lengths, ASCII-class patterns), block keys (FNV-1a over UTF-16, base36), the 200-item cap and 60,000-character text window, and rt's `diffLines`; `listed_tools` hides the write tools when AI writes is Off. `mcp::answer_line` is rt's JSON-RPC 2.0 handler (protocol 2025-06-18, also 2025-03-26 and 2024-11-05; initialize, ping, tools/list, tools/call; parse, request, and method errors with rt's codes and texts). `quire --mcp-server` (`notebook_tools::stdio`, std only, before GPUI starts) reads `QUIRE_BRIDGE` and `QUIRE_RUN`, rereads `bridge.json` on each call, answers each line on its own thread, lists the catalogue when the app is closed, and tells the model to open Quire otherwise. `notebook_tools::bridge` binds a Unix socket in the data folder (temporary `quire-<sha>.sock` when the path is 100 bytes or longer), chmods it 0600, writes `{version:1, socket, token, pid}` atomically at 0600, compares tokens in constant time, caps lines at 1 MB, and checks unknown tool, AI commands on, writes off, input, then notebook open, as rt does, with a 20 s call timeout; it runs only while AI commands are on and is removed at quit. Reads (`read.rs`) run on the index thread through `IndexClient::with_generation`, so open pages' unsaved text comes from the overlays, with new `QueryIndex::{live_blocks,page_blocks,modified_times}`. Writes (`write.rs`, `changes.rs`) propose rt's `ChangeRequest`, stage it in an `AiChanges` ledger keyed by notebook identity and run (`terminal` when the call carries none), and apply in Apply mode, rejecting on failure. An open page is edited as one buffer transaction after `finalize_last_transaction`, refused when the buffer version moved; a closed page goes through `page_writes::apply` (all-or-nothing, stale refusal). Undo restores the before text only while the page is exactly as applying left it. The AI changes window (`review.rs`, action `quire_ai::ReviewAiChanges`, page menu AI changes…) ports `AiChangeList`: labels, diff, Apply, Reject, Undo, Apply all with "N of M changes did not apply", and "Applied as a new page"; a terminal write raises a notice pointing there. `terminal.rs` ports `terminalArgs`, `shellLine`, and `registerTerminal`: per CLI it locates the binary, removes an older entry (ignoring errors), adds the server, records the shell line in `AiState`, and reports rt's messages; Remove touches only recorded CLIs. Transforms already keep the registered server off (`--strict-mcp-config`; Codex `mcp_servers.quire.enabled=false`).

Tests: `quire_core/tests/ai_tools.rs` (13: catalogue byte-for-byte against rt's `listedTools`, schema validation texts, block keys, line diff, marker stripping, outside paths, caps, protocol negotiation, ping and errors, result wrapping, catalogue when closed); `notebook_tools::bridge` (12: private file and socket removed on stop, long folders, wrong and restarted tokens, write tools hidden while writes is Off, run ID and writes forwarded, write calls blocked while Off, input and unknown tools checked before the app, no notebook and AI off, timeout, malformed lines, the 1 MB cap, concurrent calls); `notebook_tools::stdio_tests` (5, the real server as a native process from `examples/quire_mcp.rs`: initialize/list/call and exit 0 on stdin close, app closed, stale token and malformed frame, bridge stopped, and a terminal session against the live notebook that reads and stages a write); `notebook_tools::read_tests` (8: every read call against rt's outputs in `fixtures/ai/tool-reads.json`, unsaved reads, long-page windowing, limits, tagged-block speed, outside paths, no notebook); `notebook_tools::changes` (9: every write call in Review and Apply against rt's outputs and exact page texts in `fixtures/ai/tool-writes.json`, open-page append and task state as one undo step, reject and undo rules, stale closed and open pages, created pages and today's page with Apply all, notebook scoping, writes off and run grouping, and the review window applying, failing, and undoing); `notebook_tools::terminal_tests` (4 plus 1 ignored: exact commands and quoting, remove-then-add per CLI through the fake CLI with argv logged, failure and missing-binary messages, Register only with AI on and saved/removed through `AiState`; `registers_with_the_installed_clis` changes the real `~/.claude` and `~/.codex` and runs only with `--ignored`, not run here); `ai::settings` (AI writes default and save). Gates: both clippy commands, `cargo fmt --check`, the full `cargo test` command twice (888 passed, 2 ignored, each run), the `tests/reference` Vitest run (130), and `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts /Users/umer/code/zed` (3,004 files) pass. New dependency edges: `quire` uses the workspace `sha2`, `hex`, and `rand` under `full-editor`; vendored `settings_content` gains `quire.ai.writes` (recorded in `UPSTREAM.json`).

Deviations from rt: the server is `quire --mcp-server`, not Electron run as Node with a script, so the registration has no `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`; the bridge is Unix only (no Windows named pipe), and AI changes… is disabled on Windows; modification times come from revision times (Quire notebooks) or file times (folders); append and capture use Quire's own page analysis and capture placement; Undo of an applied change is a forward edit restoring the prior text, not a history pop, and its refusal names Command/Ctrl-Z; changes are reviewed in an AI changes window rather than rt's AI page; an append to today when today's page is missing creates it without a template; the registration commands are kept in Quire's AI state file rather than the AI settings.
Integration with NR30 (commits `752a44c19cc1`, `b2f768dead67`, `b9786b574ee5`): ask runs attach the tool server in their arguments, the change list is one view shared by the answer pane and the AI changes window, and Tidy runs are held to their limits in the tool host; see NR30's proof notes.
Remaining: profile integration (NR33; rt has no profile gating for AI tools); a Windows transport; running `registers_with_the_installed_clis` by hand on a machine whose CLI settings may be changed.
Status: done

`references::Notebook` gains `ask`, `daily_date`, and `daily_title`, so `:ai` without a range, `:ai <preset>`, `:ai ask` over a selection, and Tidy reach the window, and daily-page detection now feeds `parse_command`. Tidy (`proposal::tidy`) sends the whole day's page with existing page titles that share a word with it (at most 200, daily pages excluded) and a due example from the layout's next day, filters the reply with `tidy::filter` (only allowed changes reach the diff; refused lines show with their reason; dropped `^id` markers are restored; added or deleted lines are refused), and reuses the NR28 proposal block, so Accept is one undo step and is refused when the page changed meanwhile.

Gates: both clippy commands, `cargo fmt --check`, the full `cargo test` command twice, `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts /Users/umer/code/zed`, and the vitest reference suite pass (counts in the final report).

Integration with NR29: commits `752a44c19cc1` (ask runs through the tool server), `b2f768dead67` (shared change list in the pane), `b9786b574ee5` (Tidy through the tools). Questions now reach the notebook only through the notebook tools, as in rt; the excerpt gathering above was removed except for a synthesis's linked references (rt's `scope`), and context.rs now reads only those. `quire_core::ai::backend::ask_args` ports rt's ask branch of `backendArgs`: Claude gets `--restricted --strict-mcp-config --mcp-config {mcpServers.quire: {type: stdio, command, args: ["--mcp-server"], env: {QUIRE_BRIDGE, QUIRE_RUN}}} --tools "" --allowedTools mcp__quire__…` and Codex `exec --json --sandbox read-only --skip-git-repo-check --ephemeral -C <folder>` with `-c mcp_servers.quire.{command,args,env,default_tools_approval_mode="approve",enabled_tools}`; `allowed_tools` leaves the write tools out while AI writes is Off, and `ask_system_prompt` is rt's tool rules, write rule, date, and conventions. `notebook_tools::ask_server` names the server while the bridge runs (this binary in MCP mode); without it a question fails with rt's `noToolServer` message. Each question is its own run (`ask-<uuid>`), carried as `QUIRE_RUN`, so its writes group under it. Cited blocks are labeled from the index (`block_preview`) after the answer finishes. `notebook_tools::review::ChangeList` is the one change list (labels, diff, Apply, Reject, Undo, Apply all with "N of M changes did not apply"): under each question as Changes, at the top of the pane as Changes from a terminal (the `terminal` run), and for every run in the AI changes window. Tidy now asks rt's `tidyQuestion` in the pane (title "Tidy <day>"); while it runs, `host::start_tidy` holds its run to the day's page and `write::answer` refuses other write tools (`TIDY_ONLY`), other pages, and anything `tidy::check_change` refuses (links resolved through the index, due dates through the daily layout), and stages even with AI writes on Apply; the hold ends with the turn or the pane. It needs AI writes as rt's `tidyBlocked` does. `changes::apply_all` folds each open page's transactions into the first (`merge_transactions`), so Apply all is one undo step per page, keeping the old proposal's single-undo acceptance. The fake CLI (`examples/fake_ai_cli.rs`) plays a Claude ask run with tools: `FAKE_AI_TOOLS` lists calls it makes through the MCP server named by `--mcp-config` (refusing tools `--allowedTools` leaves out, with Claude's permission text), streams each as `tool_use`/`tool_result`, and logs calls and results to `FAKE_AI_TOOL_LOG`.

The pane is a column beside the main page, not a tab, and has no Open as tab. Apply all makes one undo step per page (rt applies each change separately). The tool server and so questions are macOS and Linux only.

Find offers rt's AI commands (`App.tsx` 1690-1770): the six verbs with Vim off, AI: Ask the notebook, and the presets `Preset::offered` allows for the page in the main pane, Tidy this day on a daily page. A verb runs `proposal::start` on the page editor used last (`StoredWindow::active_page_editor`, shared with insert-anywhere), deferred out of the window update; Ask and presets open the pane through `open_answer`, and Tidy through `tidy_shown`. Tests: `answer_tests::the_find_palette_runs_ai_and_appearance_commands` (labels with Vim off and on and with AI off, Tighten on the caret block through the fake CLI, Ask the notebook, Appearance: Dark), `answer_tests::synthesize_on_a_tag_page_sends_every_block_carrying_the_tag` (exact prompt with nested block and ID, context summary). Open as tab: `quire_core::workspace::BrowserView::Ai` (saved as rt's `{"kind":"browser","view":"ai"}`, so rt's tab migrates too) is the "Ask the notebook" tab, with a sparkle icon (`chrome.rs` `tab_icon`, `destination_label`), shown by `StoredWindow::show_answer_tab` and drawn by `answer_tab_content` in `render_body`. ### NR31: Port AI-assisted PDF and Word export

What to do: Flatten source with NR23, run the selected CLI in an isolated temporary export directory, validate the result, and offer save/cancel with progress.
Constraints: Preserve current backend sandbox behavior and transcript privacy. Failures leave the notebook untouched and clean temporary files. Remove the temporary export folder before reporting completion (`rt` `9cbf33a8b95e`).
Write set: document-export runner/UI, artifact validation fixtures.
Dependencies: NR23, NR28.
Verify: Fake-runner failure/cancel/malformed-artifact tests plus actual PDF/DOCX export from a synthetic page; re-open outputs to confirm valid formats and retained content.
Proof notes: Source `rt` 0.9.57 at `9cbf33a8b95e` (`src/desktop/page-export.ts`, `exportRules`/`exportPrompt`/`exportFile` in `src/core/ai-commands.ts`, the export branch of `backendArgs` and the 600 s limit in `src/desktop/ai-runner.ts`, `src/ui/PageExport.tsx`, the More menu in `src/ui/App.tsx`, `tests/page-export.test.ts`).

Rendering decision (two-way door: the runner hands bytes to `save`, so another producer can replace the CLI without touching the UI). The CLI formats the document, as in rt. rt's feature is "the writer's AI and document tools produce a consulting-grade deliverable"; its prompt asks for installed PDF/Word skills first, then pandoc, Typst, LaTeX, a headless browser, python-docx or ReportLab. Alternatives considered: (a) a native Rust writer (`docx-rs` for Word, `printpdf` or Typst for PDF), deterministic, offline, and license-clean, rejected because it is a different feature: a Markdown-to-document converter whose layout Quire would own, with no AI involvement, which the plan and the owner's parity rule do not ask for (it stays the natural offline fallback if wanted later, and needs a layout engine for tables, images, and pagination); (b) render through PDFium, rejected because PDFium reads PDFs and does not author them; (c) Quire writes Markdown and the CLI only converts, rejected as weaker than rt's prompt with no gain. Determinism and offline operation are therefore not available for this feature; what Quire controls is checked instead: exact prompts and arguments, the isolated folder, cancellation, file checks, and save semantics.

Commits `952b82f7396d` (core), `400a519f8a9e` (runner, checks, fake CLI documents), `776b86685c51` (window), `a1c3d49177ce` (status-line ticker). `quire_core::ai::export` holds rt's nine rules plus "Today is …", the request text, `export.pdf`/`export.docx`, and rt's arguments: Claude Code `--tools`/`--allowedTools Bash,Read,Write,Edit,Glob,Grep,Skill`, `--permission-mode acceptEdits`, `--permission-prompts none`, `--settings {"sandbox":{"enabled":true,"autoAllowBashIfSandboxed":true}}`, `--strict-mcp-config`, no `--restricted`, so the writer's own skills and settings apply; Codex `exec --json --sandbox workspace-write --ephemeral -C <folder>` with the quire MCP server off (the `command="quire-off"` override follows quire's transform arguments). The process host gained `Mode::Export` and `Host::run_in(folder)`, so exports use the same spawn, process-group kill, stderr, timeout (`EXPORT_TIMEOUT` 600 s), and 0600 transcript path as NR28. `document_export::produce` writes the flattened, attachment-localized page as `page.md` into `quire-export-<uuid>`, runs the CLI there, opens `export.*`, and drops the folder (a guard, so a dropped or canceled run also removes it) before returning. Checks go beyond rt's first-bytes test: Word must read as a ZIP (`async_zip`), its `word/document.xml` must parse (`quick-xml`), hold text, and show only images present in the file (external links allowed); PDF must open in hayro with a page, never PDFium, because the bytes are untrusted (NR06 decision 3); PDFs over 64 MB fall back to the header check. `save` writes with `create_new` when the target did not exist at the dialog, else stages beside it and renames; a file that appeared during the run gets a `window.prompt` ("Replace Plan.pdf in …?") and Cancel leaves it untouched. UI: Export as PDF/Word with <backend>… beside Export as Markdown… (actions `ExportPagePdf`, `ExportPageWord`), disabled with rt's tooltips while AI is off; the NR23 status line shows rt's "Claude Code is formatting Plan as PDF… N s · K steps" with Cancel, which now wakes a stoppable run through a oneshot (`Exchange::start_stoppable`); closing the window drops the run and its process tree. The fake CLI gained `FAKE_AI_WRITE` (rt's) and `FAKE_AI_RENDER`, a small Word (Heading 1 to 3, List Bullet, Normal, embedded images) and PDF (Helvetica lines) writer over `page.md`, so tests reopen real files.

Tests: `crates/quire_core/tests/ai_export.rs` (5: request and rules text, both argument lists, signatures and messages); `document_export::tests` (7 plus 1 ignored: a Word export reopened with styles, text, and the image's bytes, exact stdin, sandbox flags, system prompt, folder gone, one transcript; a PDF reopened through PDFium with every line; wrong, broken-ZIP, unopenable-PDF, and missing files refused; a failed CLI; cancel ending child and grandchild with the folder gone; `save` never replacing unagreed files and leaving no staging file; Word files with no text or a missing image); `document_export_tests` (6 GPUI tests in a folder notebook: Word export with its image and step count, Cancel with the clock-driven seconds and nothing written, a file appearing mid-run with Cancel then Replace, a file chosen in the dialog replaced without a second question, a malformed file reported, AI off). Real CLI: `cargo test -p quire --features test-support,gpui_platform/runtime_shaders -- --ignored real_cli_exports --nocapture` exports a synthetic page as PDF and Word through installed Claude Code and reopens both; it sends the page to the service, so it was not run here. Gates on the final tree: both Clippy commands with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, Vitest reference (130 passed), `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts /Users/umer/code/zed` (3,004 files, 34 documented forks), and the full test command twice (quire 531 passed, 3 ignored, each run; 51 test binaries passing).

Deviations from rt: a canceled export says "The export was canceled. Nothing was written." in the status line (rt dismisses silently); errors show in the status line rather than a window banner; the buttons live in the page header beside Export as Markdown… (rt's page More menu); the AI-off check also requires a passed Test, as all quire AI commands do; files are opened, not only signature-checked; a file appearing at the target mid-run is asked about (rt overwrote it); transcripts go to Quire's state folder (NR28).
Remaining: the real-CLI export has not been run on this machine; Codex's `workspace-write` sandbox and Claude Code's sandbox are the CLIs' own and unverified on Linux and Windows; PDF image presence and content retention are not checked in-app (hayro has no text API and PDFium must not read untrusted bytes), only by tests on fake output.
Status: done
