#### NR32.1: Check spelling in page editors

What to do: rt's editor sets `spellcheck: "true"` (`src/editor/Editor.tsx` 2263), so Chromium underlines misspelled words with the system checker. `src/desktop/main.ts` sets no spellchecker languages and handles no `context-menu` event, and the editor's own `contextmenu` handler replaces Chromium's menu, so rt offers no suggestions, Add to dictionary, or preference. Quire matches the underline and adds what rt lacks: suggestions, Learn spelling, Ignore spelling, and a preference.
Constraints: Use the platform checker so languages and learned words follow the OS. Check prose only, off the UI thread, debounced, for visible rows plus a margin, caching per word. Tests never reach the OS checker.
Write set: `spellcheck.rs` and `spellcheck/`, `page_menu.rs` (spelling entries first), preferences (`quire.spellcheck`), one vendored settings field, `page_analysis` (gap ranges), and the attach call in `editor_host.rs`.
Verify: Ranges skipped, underline placement in Write and Source, the word being typed, visible-row bounds, per-word cache, suggestion replacement as one undo step, Learn and Ignore, the preference live and saved.
Proof notes: `spellcheck::Speller` is the checker seam: `misspelled(&[&str])`, `guesses`, `learn`. On macOS `spellcheck/macos.rs` wraps `NSSpellChecker` through `objc2-app-kit` (new `NSSpellChecker` feature; `objc2` added as a direct dependency for `autoreleasepool`; both were already in the lock), with language `nil` so the user's automatic language choice applies, one spell document tag for the app, and a mutex so background checks and the menu never call it at once. A scratch probe on this machine (macOS 27) confirmed per-word checks from a background thread, `recieve` misspelled with guesses `receive`, `relieve`, and Urdu words accepted. Tests install `FakeSpeller` with `set_speller`; `cfg(test)` builds never construct the system checker. `InlineAnalysis` gained `prose_gaps` (sorted, merged), computed in the existing background inline analysis from front matter, code spans and blocks, link and image destinations, bare and angle URLs, inline HTML, HTML blocks and comments, images, link reference definitions, references, and tags; link labels stay prose. `spellcheck/words.rs` splits UAX #29 words and keeps words of two or more letters made only of letters and apostrophes, not after `^` (block IDs) and not overlapping a gap. Each page editor gets a `SpellCheck` addon from `NotebookWindow::refresh_tags` (Write, Source, the second pane, and zooms); scroll, selection, edits, analysis, settings, theme, and learned or ignored words reschedule a check 250 ms (`QUIET`) after the last one. The check maps the visible display rows, a screen above and below, through `range_to_buffer_ranges` to whole page lines, reads only those lines, and asks the checker on a background task about words not in the session cache (50,000 verdicts, then cleared). A word whose caret sits in it after an edit there is held until the caret leaves. Underlines are a `NavigationOverlay` text highlight with a 1 px wavy `UnderlineStyle` in the theme's `status().error` colour, anchored in the page buffer and lifted with `anchor_range_in_buffer`, so excerpts show them too; underlines outside the checked lines are kept. A right-click or Shift-F10 on a misspelled prose word puts up to six guesses ("No guesses found" otherwise, disabled in read-only views), Learn spelling, and Ignore spelling above the page menu; a guess replaces the word in one `transact` only if the word is still there. Settings > Editing > Check spelling (`quire.spellcheck`, default on, a vendored `QuireSettingsContent` field recorded in `UPSTREAM.json`) clears every underline at once when turned off.

Tests: `spellcheck::words::tests` (3), `spellcheck::tests` (8: only prose underlined, wavy, in Source and Write; each word asked once; the word being typed; only rows near the view; a guess as one undo step; the menu only on misspelled prose; Learn and Ignore in both panes; the preference off and on live and saved), and `preferences::tests` (defaults, the saved `"spellcheck": false`, Restore defaults, and the Settings Tab walk now toggling Check spelling with Space).

Deviations from rt: suggestions, Learn spelling, Ignore spelling, and the preference are additions. Ignore lasts the session for every page, not one document. Link labels are checked; image alt text, HTML, and URLs are not, where Chromium checks every visible editable word.
Remaining: Windows (`ISpellChecker` through the `windows` crate's `Win32_Globalization` feature) and Linux (no Hunspell or Enchant in the dependency tree) have no checker; the setting says so and nothing is underlined. Embedded block editors inside a page (`embeds.rs`) are not checked. The underline and menu were checked on the headless test platform only, not on a real display.
Status: in_progress

### NR33: Complete profiles and application lifecycle

What to do: Add Current/Work/Personal settings, overlap checks, independent backup/operation status, local workspace restoration, single-instance handling, menus/window controls, and close coordination.
Constraints: Flush before switching, preserve edits after failures, and distinguish local durability from publication/push/backup. Stalled services cannot cause silent data loss or an unexplained endless close.
Write set: application lifecycle/profile modules, native window/menu tests.
Dependencies: NR16, NR26, NR27, NR32.
Verify: Switch profiles with pending edits and AI runs; close/restart with slow, failed, and unavailable services; second-instance behavior; native shortcuts and window actions on supported OSes.
Proof notes: Three code slices (`abbc638ef090` notebook lock, `8da04f4856fe` profile records, `3ef1491f4164` profiles, close coordination, single instance, and the menu in the app). Source: `rt` 0.9.57 `5ea5c22310ff` `src/desktop/profiles.ts`, `single-instance.ts`, `close-coordinator.ts`, `main.ts` (menus, `data:profile`, `data:git-sync`, `second-instance`, `before-quit`), `ui/DataSettings.tsx`, and `tests/profiles.test.ts`, `close-coordinator.test.ts`, `single-instance.test.ts`.

The recovery-slot decision (NR11.4), a two-way door. Two sessions on one notebook share each page's single recovery slot, so the later of two concurrent recordings was refused. Quire keeps one slot per page and allows one session per notebook: `quire_store::notebook_lock` takes an exclusive operating-system lock (`File::try_lock`) on `.<database>.quire-lock` beside a Quire notebook's database, or `.open.lock` in a folder notebook's state folder, for the life of the `NotebookSession`; a second session, in this process or another, fails with "This notebook is already open in another Quire window or process. Switch to that window, or quit the other Quire, then open the notebook again." The lock holds no data and a crash releases it; dot names keep it out of backups and their change fingerprint. Per-session slots were rejected: they change the recovery schema (a one-way door for a format other builds read) and still leave two editors producing competing saves of one page. The lock is reversible: removing it, or adding per-session slots later, changes no stored data. Single instance (below) makes the lock's refusal rare in practice. Tests: `notebook_lock::tests` (a second holder refused while the first keeps recording drafts, independent notebooks, release on drop, locks outside backups and fingerprints) and `notebook_session::tests::a_second_session_on_an_open_notebook_is_refused_until_the_first_closes` (both backends).

Profiles. `quire_store::profiles` ports `profiles.ts` as `profiles.json` in application data (atomic replace with `sync_all` and a synced folder; a damaged file is reported and never overwritten, and Quire then keeps profiles in memory for the run). Each of Current, Work, and Personal holds a notebook (a folder, or a Quire notebook's database with its identities), a backup folder, the last backup time, path, and error, and for Personal a Git remote, last push, and error. Overlap checks follow rt: no notebook overlaps another profile's notebook or any backup folder (case-insensitive, links resolved as far as the path exists), no backup folder overlaps a notebook, and only Current's own notebook may move into Work or Personal, which clears Current. A different notebook keeps the backup folder but resets results and the remote. The app (`crates/quire/src/profiles.rs`) keeps the records as a GPUI global; a window's notebook belongs to the profile holding it, and opening a notebook makes it the active profile's unless another profile holds it (rt's `open(root)`), after `check_open` refuses overlaps. Backups and Git now read and record through the profile (rt keeps both in `profiles.sqlite`); `backup-folders.json` and `personal-git.json` from NR26 and NR27 are no longer read (no released build wrote them). The status line beside the profile buttons shows the last backup or its failure and Personal's last push or Git failure, separately from the page's local save status.

Switching (`editor_host/stored/profiles.rs`, actions `quire_profile::UseCurrentProfile`, `UseWorkProfile`, `UsePersonalProfile`, `OpenNotebookFolder`). A switch refuses an overlapping folder and a running backup or Git sync (rt's "data operation is still running"), dismisses every AI proposal (NR28: dropping the run kills its process tree), saves and records every open page in every notebook window, then waits until all are saved locally, not only recorded for recovery. A storage failure, or work still busy after 10 s, stops the switch with the reason and "Your edits stay open in this window."; nothing changes. Then it sets and activates the profile, opens the notebook (asking for a folder when the profile has none), and closes the old windows. A Quire notebook reopens at its workspace's active page; a folder notebook at its active page when that note still exists, else its first note (local workspace restoration; tabs and favorites were already restored per notebook). Launching without arguments reopens the active profile's notebook.

Personal Git backs up first. When Personal has a backup folder, `SyncStatus::sync` runs `backup::create` on the folder notebook and its state before Git; a failure is recorded in the profile and stops the sync with "Git sync did not run because the backup before it failed: ... Fix the backup folder in Backups…, then sync again." Deviation: rt refuses to sync without a backup folder; Quire syncs without one, as the task asked.

Close coordination (`crates/quire/src/lifecycle.rs`, `CloseState` in `stored.rs`). A window's close and the app's quit guard combine the pages (busy until recorded, failed on a storage error) with the window's services (backup, Git sync, and fakes in tests). Idle closes at once. Busy work is polled every 100 ms; after 3 s (rt's `CloseCoordinator` timeout) Quire asks "Quire is still finishing work for this notebook." naming what runs, with Keep waiting (asks again 3 s later) or Close anyway. A failure asks at once, "Some work for this notebook did not finish.", with Keep editing (the window stays with the reason) or Close anyway. The detail says what Close anyway keeps (everything saved or recorded for recovery) and loses (unrecorded typing), and that an interrupted backup leaves only a hidden partial folder. Close anyway on a quit sets a forced flag every window's guard honors. `:wq` keeps its own save-error path. Deviation from rt: rt cancels a Keep waiting close; Quire keeps waiting and finishes on its own. Since NR09.5 a Quire notebook with a synced folder publishes its saved changes as a close service: busy while publishing, failed with the reason when publication fails, the close starting publication at once (tests: slow, failed, and unavailable folder in `live_tests`).

Single instance (`crates/quire/src/single_instance.rs`, `editor_host/stored/instance.rs`), after Zed's Linux listener. The first launch binds `quire.sock` in application data; a later launch sends its version, executable, and arguments as one JSON line and exits once answered. The running Quire focuses the window already showing the named notebook, opens it otherwise (at its last page), or comes forward without arguments; a newer version (rt's `isNewer`) makes it quit through the guards and start the newer executable with its arguments from `on_app_quit`, answering "busy" meanwhile so the newer launch retries (50 × 200 ms) until the socket closes. A socket left by a crash is removed and claimed. Windows has no handover yet; the notebook lock still guards there. Editor harness modes skip it.

Menus and window controls (`crates/quire/src/app_menus.rs`, `cx.set_menus` as Zed's `app_menus.rs`): Quire (Settings…, Hide, Hide Others, Show All, Quit) on macOS; File (New Page…, Today, Open Notebook Folder…, Profile ▸ Current/Work/Personal checked by the active profile, Save Locally, Back Up Now, Sync with Git, Close Window, and on other platforms Settings and Quit); Edit (Undo, Redo, Cut, Copy, Paste, Select All with their OS actions, Find ▸ Find…, Find and Replace…, Find Next, Find Previous, Find in Notebook…); View (Appearance ▸ System/Light/Dark checked by the setting, Source or Write, Page Beside, Enter Full Screen); Window (Minimize, Zoom); Help (Help and Shortcuts, README Sample). Menus are rebuilt when settings or profiles change. Close Window goes through the window's close guard; Cmd-M, Cmd-Shift-W, Cmd-H, Cmd-Alt-H, and Ctrl-Cmd-F (Ctrl-Shift-W and F11 elsewhere) are bound. Native macOS traffic lights stay; rt's hidden-inset custom controls were not copied.

Tests. Store: `profiles::tests` (4: Current moves into Work and survives reopening, overlap refusals including a Quire notebook and backup folders, Git Personal-only with failures keeping the last success and a new notebook clearing the remote, a damaged file untouched and a failed save changing nothing), `notebook_lock::tests` (3). App: `lifecycle::tests` (1), `single_instance::tests` (3: version order, arguments handed over and a newer build taking over, busy while quitting, a stale socket reclaimed), and GPUI `stored::navigation::lifecycle_tests` (12): a slow service closes on its own without a question; an unavailable service asks after 3 s, Keep waiting asks again, Close anyway closes; a failed service asks at once, Keep editing keeps the window and reason, and a forced quit quits; Close anyway after storage stops keeps the saved text for the next launch and releases the lock; a switch with pending edits saves them to disk, opens Work, checks Work in the menu, and switches back; a switch that cannot record pages changes nothing and keeps the edit; overlapping folders and a running backup refuse a switch; a reopened folder notebook shows its last page; a second launch focuses the open notebook, opens another, and a newer one quits; the menu lists Appearance, Find, profile, and window entries that dispatch (Find in Notebook opens the palette, Close Window closes through the guard); the Appearance items follow Dark; a switch ends a hanging AI CLI's process tree and leaves the page unchanged. `sync_tests` now cover Git refused outside Personal, a backup before each push verified on disk, and the profile's push records; `quit_failure_keeps_the_window_and_current_text_accessible` answers the new failure question. Switch tests step the fake clock one 100 ms poll at a time with 25 ms of real time between steps: under the full suite, `wait_until`'s 200 ms steps once used up the 10 s switch patience before the storage thread had saved, and the switch was refused. Gates: library and app clippy with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, the full Rust suite twice (860 passed, 1 ignored, each run), the reference Vitest suite (130 passed), and `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts` (3004 files; no vendored Zed changes).

Batch D (2026-10-31). File > Open Quire Notebook… and Settings > Data > Choose Quire notebook choose a `local.sqlite`, read its identity without writing (`NotebookStore::identity`, `identity_reads_a_chosen_database_without_writing_and_refuses_other_files`), and open it as the active profile's notebook (`data_tests::choosing_a_quire_notebook_database_opens_it_as_the_active_profiles_notebook`); a database chosen this way has no synced folder until `--synced-folder` names it. Settings > Data shows the active profile with its notebook, backup destination and last backup, and Personal's Git remote with Save and Sync. On macOS notebook windows draw rt's hidden-inset title bar (`5e6bb56d691c`, see NR32).

Batch L (2026-10-31, rt `5ea5c22310ff` `main.ts` `open` and `isQuireNotebook`, `DataSettings.tsx` New Quire notebook, `engine.ts` `createQuireNotebook`). Choosing a folder that holds `quire-notebook.json` opens it as a Quire notebook (`quire_store::quire_folder::open`): the identity is checked as `rt` checks it, this computer's database is `notebooks/<id>/local.sqlite` in application data (the path migration uses), made the first time under a writer no folder writer used and filled from the change files, and reused afterwards; a database there for another notebook is refused untouched. Settings > Data > New Quire notebook (experimental), `quire_profile::NewQuireNotebook`, writes `quire-notebook.json` with `create_new` into a folder holding only hidden files and opens it. A Quire notebook with no pages opens on today's daily page instead of refusing. `import_log` and the writer choice moved from migration into `quire_folder` for both. Tests: `crates/quire_store/tests/quire_folder.rs` (4) and `data_tests::choosing_a_quire_notebooks_synced_folder_opens_it_as_a_quire_notebook`, `data_tests::a_new_quire_notebook_needs_an_empty_folder_then_opens_there`.

Remaining: native desktop acceptance of the menus, close button, Cmd-Q, logout and session end, the title bar, and the handover on macOS, Windows, and Linux (unverified; only the headless GPUI platform ran); single-instance handover on Windows; a database chosen with Choose Quire notebook still has no folder until its folder is chosen (a database does not record its folder); the native folder dialog on each OS.
Status: in_progress

## Acceptance and cutover

### NR34: Enforce performance budgets

What to do: Run the documented workload on release builds, identify costs with traces, and fix measured bottlenecks in bounded slices. Record process/GPU memory and loaded-body/view counts.
Constraints: No feature disabling, durability reductions, hidden helpers, or comparison of first paint with first editable. Run timing gates on controlled hardware.
Write set: `tools/bench/`, performance reports and narrowly scoped measured fixes.
Dependencies: NR12, NR15, NR19, NR21, NR27, NR33.
Verify: Every main-plan budget, 1x/5x scale ratio, large-page typing, and concurrent sync/index/backup workload. Preserve raw measurements and rerun correctness tests affected by each optimization.
Proof notes: performance instrumentation starts in NR11, not only here. One measured fix landed early: folder-notebook changes across notes batch their syncs (NR13 "Journal throughput"; a 1,000-note import fell from 37.4 s to 0.43 to 4.4 s on a loaded Mac, measured with `crates/quire_store/examples/journal_bench.rs`, which belongs in the release-build workload).

Harness. `bun run bench` (`tools/bench/run.ts`) builds release binaries, launches the app on seed-1 fixtures at 1x (6,536 pages) and 5x (32,680 pages) with `QUIRE_BENCH`, and compares percentiles with `tools/bench/budgets.json` (plan budgets, plus first-open, warm-index, large-page, and import limits anchored to `rt` measurements, 10% margin). The app's report names its analyzer version, which the runner passes to `index_queries` (store queries without the app); a failure there is recorded instead of discarding the launches. Raw results stay in `target/bench/results/`. Limits: the bench needs a real window, so it cannot run headless; macOS builds use `gpui_platform/runtime_shaders`; a covered window or locked screen paints nothing, so every measure settles for state and is counted in `unpainted`. All runs below had a locked screen (`unpainted` 266 of 266), so frame times and typing work are not comparable with a painted run; launch, index, query, and memory numbers are.

Fixes, each with tests. Before numbers come from merged main at 13e6c6aaee74 (results `2026-10-31T20-28-45-711Z` for 5x, load 1.4 to 3.1; 1x launches at load 2.1 to 7.2), after numbers from `2026-10-31T22-14-46-917Z` (load 2.1 to 9.5; 9.5 and 7.3 during two 1x warm launches).
- Warm index wait: every open ran `PRAGMA quick_check` over the whole index file (179 MB at 1x, 844 MB at 5x). The index now seals itself when analysis goes idle, on quit, and on close: it checkpoints the write-ahead log and records the file's size, modification time, and change time in `query-index.sqlite-sealed`. An open skips the scan only when the record matches and the log is empty; a crash with unsealed changes, an outside edit, or a mismatch scans, and damage still rebuilds (tests corrupt a page with and without a matching seal). Warm index ready p95: 1x 1,450 to 1,169 ms; 5x 2,770 to 1,598 ms. The earlier 23 to 41 s warm waits did not recur on a quiet machine; they were recorded at load 47 to 101.
- Backlinks: block-reference resolution grouped rows, which kept SQLite from flattening the live views, so each call copied all 32,681 pages into a temporary index. Backlinks p95 at 5x: 2,013 to 0.87 ms; store-level worst page 2,066 to 0.96 ms. A test pins the query plan.
- Tag completion: typing at the end of a tagged line asked for every tag on each keystroke, a 125 ms tally of 198,483 tagged blocks at 5x that kept 68% of index-thread samples busy. Saved tags are tallied once per committed change; open pages' overlays fold in per call.
- Linked tasks pane: each index change read, parsed, and sorted all 48,260 tasks at 5x. Only links that could name the page are resolved and only matching tasks are read.
- Tag labels: every edit parsed the whole page into blocks before checking for tags. Large-page typing work p95 at 1x: 43.0 to 13.1 ms.
Resident memory after 20 page opens: 1x 597 to 327 MiB; 5x 1,547 to 870 MiB. Search p95 at 5x fell from 1,945 to 480 ms because backlinks no longer queue ahead of it; the query itself is unchanged.

Final run, pass or fail against budgets: pass for warm editable (1x 463 ms, 5x 686 ms), warm index (1x 1,169 ms, 5x 1,598 ms), first open editable at 5x (971 ms), typing frame (unpainted), page open (1x 13.8 ms, 5x 10.4 ms), backlinks at 5x (0.87 ms), large-page open (16.3 ms) and frame (unpainted), and folder import (0.57 ms a note). Fail for warm startup ratio (1.47 against 1.25), first open editable at 1x (2,049 ms against 1,500), first open index (1x 9.5 s against 2.8 s; 5x 49.6 s against 15 s), typing work (1x p95 16.6 ms, p99 19.0 ms; 5x p95 11.9 ms; against 4 and 8), search (1x 125 ms, 5x 480 ms against 100), tasks at 5x (259 ms against 50), tags at 5x (146 ms against 50; the first call after typing commits retallies), large-page typing work (13.1 ms against 8), and memory (1x 327 MiB, 5x 870 MiB against 250; growth 542 MiB against 50).
Second pass on the store's offenders, measured while two other agents loaded the 12-core Mac (load averages beside each number; the app bench was not used as evidence). `quire --measure-index` (a `test-support` mode, see README) indexes a seed-1 fixture headless with the app's analysis loop and types into `Topic 0.md` while rerunning the open page's queries; `index_queries` times store queries on the index it leaves. Baseline is merged main at 7ffaf03b9781 built with the same harness; runs alternated baseline and new binaries, two rounds each, and figures are medians of five or six runs per query. Raw results stayed in the session's scratch folder, not the repository.
- Search ranks before it makes snippets: the first pass asks FTS5 for the best-ranked row IDs only (`bm25`, then row ID for ties), skipping saved rows of open pages and deleted pages, reading more only when skipped rows leave fewer than 40; the shown rows are copied into a scratch FTS5 table with the same columns and tokenizer, searched there for their snippets, and rolled back, since snippets read only their own row. Equal ranks now order saved rows first, then by row; ties were unordered before. 5x "budget": 611 and 735 ms to 89 and 70 ms (load 17.0 to 19.1); the slowest of the ten queries 644 and 735 ms to 92 and 75 ms. 1x: 90 and 108 ms to 14 and 13 ms (load 15.9 to 19.1). What remains is FTS5 scoring every match: 184,233 rows match "meeting" at 5x, so the floor is about 70 ms under this load. Tests compare every result, snippet, and order with the rank-everything query on a generated notebook with shared prefixes, open pages, and a notebook where most best matches belong to open pages, and bound the ranked rows read by 40 plus the open pages' saved rows.
- Task board: tasks carry `day` (the due date, or the daily page's date) under a `(state, day)` index, so counts come from the index alone (all saved rows, less the saved rows of open or deleted pages, plus unsaved rows), the chosen list reads its rows from the tables directly instead of through the live views (whose per-row joins tripled the cost), and only its first `limit` tasks are read in full; project actions are read only for tasks on project pages, tasks whose links could name one (a lookup in the new task link index), and tasks tagged `project/...`; project pages are found by index lookups. 5x board by list (Due/Inbox/Next/Upcoming): 448/411/465/337 and 325/319/338/310 ms to 15/43/45/9 and 15/45/42/5 ms (load 16.9 to 19.1); 1x 41 to 63 ms to 1 to 17 ms. Tests compare every view, filter, project, limit, and open-page case with the read-every-task board, and bound the rows read by the list's rows plus `limit` plus candidate project actions.
- Linked tasks: task links are stored with their page key and date (`task_links`, indexed by both), so the query reads only links that could name the page. 5x: 73 and 75 ms to 0.34 and 0.32 ms per page (load 16.9 to 19.1); while typing at 5x in the harness, 55 ms to 0.33 ms a change (load 8.4 and 18.0). Tests compare every page, with open pages, with the scanning version, bound the rows read by the links naming the page, and pin both lookups to their indexes.
- Tags: triggers on `main.tagged` record which tag keys changed, and the cached tallies recount only those keys after a commit; a cold tally is unchanged (5x 188 and 231 ms to 143 and 140 ms, the first call after open). The test saves, reanalyzes, and deletes pages, checks the incremental tallies against a full tally, and asserts the rows reread equal the changed tags' rows.
- First-open build: index inserts prepare each statement once per batch instead of once per row; a backlog (a batch of 16 or more pages) checkpoints the write-ahead log per 64 MiB instead of per 4 MiB until the index seals, since checkpoints and their file syncs took 37% of the index thread in a 5x sample, and sealing restores the default and empties the log, so crash behavior is unchanged; folder scans and pending reads stat, read, and hash files on up to eight threads; and the app parses each batch of 64 pages while the index stores the previous one. Paired runs (baseline, then new): 1x 7.5 to 4.4 s (load 13.8 to 12.4) and 22.1 to 14.1 s (load 24.8 to 27.6); 5x 108 to 62 s (load 11.8 to 27.6), 131 to 60 s (load 28.5 to 25.3), and 86 to 51 s (load 6.0 to 22.0). The initial folder scan at 5x fell from 4 to 9.5 s to 1.6 to 5.7 s. Tests: the backlog setting test, and every existing folder index test.
- Memory: the harness types 300 characters at 5x into an open page and reruns the page's queries; resident memory stays flat before and after these changes (228 to 208 MiB, load 6.5 to 18), so the index and its queries do not hold what the app accumulates while typing. The large transient reads that ran per change (all 48,260 tasks for linked tasks, all 198,483 tagged blocks after each autosave) are gone, which the earlier allocator snapshot pointed at, but the app's growth has not been remeasured.
Status: in progress
Remaining: typing work p95 rose from about 10 to 16.6 ms at 1x and 7.9 to 11.9 ms at 5x between the run after the backlinks fix and the final run, with no offender visible in a 10 s main-thread sample (keystrokes took 175 of 5,406 samples); measure it again on an unlocked, painting screen before blaming any change. Search at 5x is now FTS5 scoring every match, 70 to 92 ms medians and up to 104 ms p95 at load 17 to 19 against 100; staying under on a loaded machine needs scoring fewer rows, which FTS5 offers no exact way to do. The 5x Inbox and Next lists take 43 to 45 ms against 50, most of it joining 8,442 rows to their pages for the path order. The first tag tally after an open still reads every tagged block (140 ms at 5x); only later calls are incremental. First-open builds stay over budget, 1x 4.4 s against 2.8 s and 5x 51 to 62 s against 15 s, all measured at load 12 to 28, where parsing on the shared executor dominates; time them again on an idle machine before changing analysis. The tag page's task filter (`tagged_tasks`) and `{{tasks}}` blocks (`task_list`) still read every task. The app's resident growth while typing at 5x is unmeasured since these fixes; the index side stays flat. Warm launch under load, the large page's resident cost, and the concurrent sync, index, and backup workload are unmeasured.

### NR35: Package and verify native applications

What to do: Build macOS/Windows packages and Linux artifacts with icons, fonts, grammars, PDF/formatter dependencies, CLI/MCP mode, and correct application identities.
Constraints: No Zed/rt checkout or developer runtime at build/run acceptance. Keep development identity/data distinct until cutover; signing status must be explicit. The Go terminal app installs a `quire` command and keeps data in `$XDG_DATA_HOME/quire` (default `~/.local/share/quire`); the native CLI/MCP executable and data root must not collide with either.
Write set: packaging/CI configuration, manifests/notices, native acceptance harness.
Dependencies: NR05, NR06, NR31, NR33, NR34.
Verify: Clean isolated builds and install/open/update smoke checks; native input/IME/clipboard/accessibility, dialogs, scale factors, close saves, media, formatters, and terminal tools on each OS. Report unsigned/notarization status accurately.
Proof notes: During NR11.3.2 an uncached build failed because Xcode's Metal toolchain was missing. It is now installed (`xcrun -sdk macosx metal --version` reports 32023.922), and the NR35 bundle was built with precompiled shaders; `tools/bundle-mac.ts` stops with the download command when the toolchain is absent, or builds with `--runtime-shaders` on request (that path has not been bundled).

Packaging follows Zed's `script/bundle-mac`, `bundle-linux`, and `generate-licenses`, with bun scripts in `tools/` (the README describes each). Build config: `[profile.release]` is Zed's (`debug = "limited"`, `lto = "thin"`, `codegen-units = 1`, 16 for `quire`); NR34 benchmarks built with `--release` now get thin LTO. Resources in `crates/quire/resources`: `Info.plist` (Quire Native, `app.quire.native`, macOS 12.0, no document types or URL schemes, as rt registers none), `quire.entitlements` (Zed's JIT pair, for the linked wasmtime), rt's icon (`5d3a04a9b54a`) as SVG, ICNS, and ICO, and a Linux desktop entry. `tools/pdfium.ts` now holds the PDFium pins shared by `fetch-pdfium.ts` and the packaging scripts.

Verified on this Mac (macOS 27.0.1, Apple silicon), with `bun tools/bundle-mac.ts --dmg` then `bun tools/verify-bundle-mac.ts --launch`: Info.plist keys and identity distinct from rt; arm64 executable with `minos 12.0` linking only system libraries and carrying the Frameworks rpath; PDFium in `Contents/Frameworks` with install name `@rpath/libpdfium.dylib`, system-only linkage, and no link-time FPDF imports; notices containing the 20 required components (PDFium and its bundled licenses, pdfium-render, hayro-interpret with the Foxit and CGATS notices, vello, pic-scale, Prettier with its third-party notices, QuickJS-ng, rquickjs, the tree-sitter grammars, async_zip, quick-xml, the vendored Zed crates and fonts, the @lezer/markdown port); `codesign --verify --deep --strict` on the ad hoc signature with entitlements embedded; `quire --mcp-server` listing 14 tools and reporting the closed app with no bridge; the app staying up 10 s with an empty `HOME`; recorded output hashes. Rebundling the same binary gave 7 of 7 identical files (`--compare`). A tampered copy (rt's bundle ID, notices and PDFium removed) failed 5 checks. Notices also generate without failures for x86_64 and aarch64 Linux, x86_64 Windows, and x86_64 macOS (826, 826, 723, and 738 crates). Sizes: release binary 154.4 MB, stripped executable 115.0 MB, PDFium 7.7 MB, bundle 124.4 MB, disk image 55.4 MB; cold release build 11 min 50 s.

A fresh review (no builds) found no signing-order or entitlement errors and led to `55c06669b53c`: the notarization key is written with mode 0600 and removed even when submission fails, which now requires an `Accepted` status and names `notarytool log` otherwise; the sidecar records the binary's own provenance (commit, uncommitted changes, features, shader mode, hash), and `--skip-build` keeps it only for the same binary; the verifier fails when build inputs (`crates`, `vendor`, Cargo files, toolchain) changed since the build, and checks hardened runtime, timestamp, and team ID on Developer ID signatures; notices add @lezer/markdown, fail when a `vendor/*/UPSTREAM.json` source is uncredited, count only license texts as coverage, and name each fileless crate's authors; Windows zips with System32 `tar.exe`; Linux adds a debug link and ships only the SVG icon.

Found while verifying (NR33): with a data folder path longer than `SUN_LEN`, startup fails with "Cannot reach the running Quire ... path must be shorter than SUN_LEN. Quit the other Quire" although no other Quire runs. Fixed: the instance socket now falls back to a hashed name in the temporary folder, as the notebook bridge socket already did, through one shared `single_instance::socket_path` (test `long_data_folders_put_the_socket_in_the_temporary_folder`).

Not verified: signing with a Developer ID and the hardened runtime, notarization, and stapling (no credentials here; the scripts skip with a message naming the variables); Gatekeeper acceptance (it rejects the ad hoc bundle, as expected); bit-for-bit reproducibility of the Rust build itself (only the bundling step was repeated; the binary embeds checkout paths); a `--runtime-shaders` bundle; an x86_64 or universal macOS bundle; `tools/bundle-linux.ts` and `tools/bundle-windows.ts` on their platforms, including the `rquickjs-sys` builds NR22 left open; Windows and Linux installers (Zed uses Inno Setup and a tarball install script), an icon embedded in the Windows executable, Zed's `windows_subsystem` attribute (without it the executable opens a console window), and a GPUI `app_id` matching the desktop entry; the script fonts NR32 left unbundled; install, update, and handover smoke checks between package versions; and native input, IME, clipboard, accessibility, dialogs, scale factors, media, formatters, and terminal tools on each OS.
Since the bundle above, `tools/generate-licenses.ts` also credits Noto Naskh Arabic 2.021, Noto Nastaliq Urdu 4.000 (OFL, `crates/quire/assets/fonts`), and the Lucide 1.62.0 tag icons (ISC); the bundle has not been rebuilt with them.
Status: in progress: the macOS bundle builds and passes its checks unsigned; owner signing and notarization credentials and the Linux and Windows runs remain

### NR36: Demonstrate full-state migration and rollback

What to do: Implement the documented transfer of old local-only saves/drafts, settings, and history to the native data root. Rehearse copied format 1 and folder-notebook adoption and rollback after new native edits.
Constraints: Never reuse an old writer ID, delete source state, or imply Markdown export preserves all history. Legacy drafts whose `base_known` is 0 stay review-only. Stores from `rt` 0.9.49 and older can lack revisions their own change files hold; read those back from the published log, as `c3a036587871` does, instead of treating the store as complete. Require explicit conversion before changing storage mode.
Write set: migration/recovery utilities, migration report and synthetic acceptance fixtures.
Dependencies: NR24, NR25, NR26, NR27, NR35.
Verify: Before/after manifests cover text, IDs, history, drafts, archives, attachments, and profile isolation. Interrupt migration, resume safely, then demonstrate return to the legacy app with post-cutover edits retained through a supported path.
Proof notes: Five slices (`7b319e06e63b` synthetic rt state, `ca6f791ecf0f` store migration, `5d6019566870` commands and renderer storage, `aae4ef59873e` rollback vector read by rt, `0319f72a00b0` review fixes). Source: `rt` 0.9.57 `5ea5c22310ff` `src/desktop/profiles.ts`, `notebook-storage.ts`, `vault.ts`, `history-store.ts`, `backups.ts`, `engine/{engine,engine-files,backup,disk-folder,sqlite-store}.ts`, `legacy-names.ts`, `main.ts` (data paths, `settings.json`, `appearance.json`), `ai-runner.ts` (`ai-settings.json`), `core/workspace.ts`, `editor/ai-prompt.ts`, `ui/AiPage.tsx`.

Fixtures from rt's own writers. `tests/reference/desktop/` freezes those thirteen desktop modules and `core/types.ts` (hashes and the four import-path adaptations in `UPSTREAM.json` under `rt_state_extractions`). `bun tools/rt-state-vectors.ts` runs them to write `tests/fixtures/rt-state/`: rt's application data (`profiles.sqlite` with WAL, `settings.json`, `appearance.json`, `ai-settings.json`, `device-id`, two Quire notebooks' `notebooks/<id>/notebook.sqlite`), a folder notebook with notes, an attachment, a saved version, a draft, `.quire/notebook.json`, and a history snapshot, a Quire notebook with two published saves, an attachment, a draft typed into a known version and one with `base_known` 0, a second Quire notebook whose last save rt never published, and one backup of each kind. Profile roots use the placeholder `/rt-fixture`, which tests point at their copy. `tests/reference/rt-state.test.ts` reads the fixture back through the same frozen code under Node (profiles, drafts, history, the unpublished save, and rt settling its known-base draft on open). No user data is read.

Inventory of rt state and what Quire does with it (the migration report prints the same table for the files it finds):

| rt state | Where | Format | Quire |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Profiles: notebook, backup folder, Git remote, last results | `profiles.sqlite` in rt's data folder | SQLite WAL | Imported once per item into `profiles.json`; backup and push results stay rt's |
| Last notebook before profiles | `settings.json` | JSON `{root}` | Imported as Current's notebook only when `profiles.sqlite` never recorded one (rt's `migrate`) |
| Appearance | `appearance.json`, local storage `quire.appearance` | JSON | Imported at first launch (NR32) |
| Editor settings | local storage `quire.editor.settings`, `quire.editor.vim` | Chromium LevelDB | Imported at first launch (NR32) |
| Tabs, favorites, recents, expanded folders, oldest-first pages | local storage `quire-workspace:<root>` | LevelDB JSON | Imported once per notebook into `quire-workspace.json`, paths mapped to page identities; rt's `ai`, `help`, `readme` browser tabs, positions, browser and tag view filters dropped |
| AI prompt history, synthesis instruction | local storage `quire.ai-history`, `quire.synthesis-instruction` | LevelDB JSON | Merged once into `ai-state.json` (Quire's prompts first, at most 30) |
| AI settings and terminal registrations | `ai-settings.json` | JSON | Not migrated: listed in the report; Quire enables AI only after its own Test, and terminals register again |
| Writer identity | `device-id` | UUID | Not needed; never reused (a migrated notebook's writer is a new UUID checked against it and every `devices/` folder) |
| Quire notebook local database: log cache, outbox, drafts | `notebooks/<id>/notebook.sqlite` | SQLite WAL | Read from a copy: drafts imported, pages and revision IDs compared; pages and history come from the published log; a non-empty outbox stops that notebook |
| Quire notebook change log, attachments | synced folder: `quire-notebook.json`, `devices/<d>/log/*.qlog.zst`, `attachments/` | format 1 | Imported by Quire's rescan into `notebooks/<id>/local.sqlite`; read only |
| Sync status | `devices/<d>/status.json` | JSON | Not needed |
| Folder notes and attachments | the folder | Markdown and files | Read in place |
| Daily layout | `.quire/notebook.json` | JSON | Read in place; written only on an explicit layout save, in rt's format (NR13) |
| Folder drafts and history | `.quire/devices/<d>/durability.sqlite`, `recovery/*.json`, `.quire/history/<d>.sqlite` | SQLite WAL, JSON | Imported once from copies (NR13) |
| rt's interrupted multi-note changes | `.quire/devices/<d>/transactions/` | JSON journals | Not migrated: the report counts them and asks to open the notebook in rt once |
| Originals of notes rt moved | `.quire/devices/<d>/retired/` | Markdown | Not migrated; they stay where rt's history finds them |
| Search index | `.quire/devices/<d>/search.sqlite` | SQLite FTS | Not needed |
| AI transcripts | `.quire/ai/` | Markdown | Not needed; stay in the folder |
| Personal Git repository | `.git` | Git | Read in place (NR27) |
| Pre-`.quire` recovery data | `durability/`, `transactions/`, `recovery/`, `retired/` in rt's data folder | SQLite, JSON | Not migrated; rt moves it into `.quire` when it next opens the notebook |
| Analysis cache, Electron state, sample notebook, tool server address | `IndexedDB`, caches, `Example notebook`, `bridge.json` | various | Not needed |
| Backups | `<backup folder>/Quire/<profile>/<hash>/<id>/` | rt manifest | Restorable as new notebooks |

Store migration (`crates/quire_store/src/rt_migration/`). `migrate` reads rt's profiles from a copy (`copy_database`: the file and its `-wal` copied into `.rt-migration/` in Quire's data folder, `integrity_check` on the copy), then each profile's notebook. A folder notebook is adopted by opening it once under its notebook lock with Quire's state folder (`folder_notebook::state_folder_in`, now shared with the app), which imports rt's drafts and history; the report compares notes as rt reads them (raw bytes) with Quire's reader, other files, rt drafts and versions with what Quire's state accounts for (`FolderNotebook::reference_tally`, deduplicating drafts and versions a history snapshot repeats), the layout file, and rt's pending journals. A Quire notebook gets a writer identity recorded in `rt-migration.json` before its database exists, so a resumed run reuses it; the whole published log is imported in bounded rescans, which also covers rt 0.9.49 stores that lack revisions their own change files hold; drafts are imported once each (a known base only when `base_known` and the revision exists in Quire, otherwise review-only), keyed in the record so a crash between the draft and the record cannot double it. The report compares rt's `pages` table with Quire's live pages (count and hash of path and text), checks every rt revision ID is in Quire, accounts for every draft, and checks attachment delivery. Profiles are imported item by item (`profile:<id>:notebook`, `backup`, `git`, `profile:active`); a Git remote waits until its profile's notebook is set, because setting a notebook clears the remote. `restore_backup` validates an rt manifest by rt's rules, verifies every file's size and SHA-256 without writing, checks the database on a copy, builds the restore in a hidden `.restoring-` folder that a failure removes, and renames it to `Restored <time>-<id>`. A folder backup's drafts are set to review in the restored copy's `.quire` databases and JSON records; a Quire notebook backup's change files are rewritten under a new notebook identity with their `previous` chain, as rt's `restoreQuireBackup` does (`Json::set_string`/`set_null` added beside `set_number`), then migrated with every draft for review. Both results are notebooks rt opens too. `prepare_rollback` reports, per migrated notebook under its lock, what rt will see; with `publish` it publishes Quire's outbox to the synced folder and has a fresh replica read the folder from scratch, which must show the same pages as Quire.

App (`crates/quire/src/rt_migration.rs`). `quire --migrate-from-rt [<folder>]` (default: the first of `QUIRE_DATA_DIR`, `Quire`, `Threadnote` under the platform data folder that holds `profiles.sqlite` or `settings.json`), `--rollback-to-rt [--publish]`, `--list-rt-backups <folder>`, and `--restore-rt-backup <backup> --into <folder>` run before any window opens and exit with 0 when everything checked out. The migration also imports tabs and AI history from rt's LevelDB (read-only reader from NR32) and saves the report as `rt-migration-report.md` in Quire's data folder.

What rt sees after Quire (the rollback contract). Folder notebooks: every note Quire saved, since both read the same files; rt's own drafts, history, journals, and archive unchanged; Quire's unsaved drafts and Quire's history entries are not visible to rt (the rollback report names unsaved pages); a layout saved in Quire is rt's format. Quire notebooks: rt sees Quire's saves once published under Quire's writer; rt's local database, device log, and drafts are unchanged, so rt settles its own known-base drafts on open as before. rt's profiles, settings, renderer storage, and backups are never written, so rt opens what it opened before migration. Not seen by rt: profile choices, tabs, and AI history changed in Quire; notebooks restored from rt backups unless opened in rt by folder.

One-way doors, named and guarded. Publishing Quire's saves into a Quire notebook's synced folder: immutable change files every replica keeps, guarded by `--publish` (the report without it says it cannot be undone). Everything else is two-way: migration writes only Quire's data folder (deleting `notebooks/`, `folder-notebooks/<hash>`, `profiles.json`, `rt-migration.json` undoes it), restores create new folders, and folder edits are ordinary file saves rt reads. Storage mode never changes: a folder notebook stays a folder notebook and a Quire notebook a Quire notebook.

Review fixes (fresh adversarial review, fixed in `0319f72a00b0`). `copy_database` copied a database and its WAL without checking that rt left them alone; it now compares size and modified time of both before and after and copies again, up to three times, before failing with what to do. Migration ran while rt was open; it now refuses when rt's `SingletonLock` exists. Profile import set rt's backup folder and Git remote on a Quire profile that already held a different notebook; they now join only the profile holding the notebook they served in rt, wait while that notebook is unmigrated, and profile failures reach the report's problems. A draft kept its base when the base revision existed but held different text; it now keeps a base only when that revision's page matches. A Quire notebook with no rt local database said its pages matched; the Pages check now says they were not compared. The rollback check imported only the first bounded rescan of the synced folder; it now reads every continuation and reports unreadable change files, which make the notebook not ready. The tab import released the notebook lock before writing the workspace file; it now holds the lock through the write, and it also finds rt's tabs under a profile root spelled differently from the canonical path (a symlink, for example).

Tests. Store (`rt_migration::tests`, 9): a running rt (its `SingletonLock` present) refused with nothing written to Quire and rt unchanged; a Quire profile that already holds another notebook keeps it, its own active profile, and gets no backup folder from rt; every notebook and profile migrated with rt's tree hashed byte for byte before and after, Notes/Pages/Drafts/history/attachments checks, the known-base draft keeping its head as base, the `base_known` 0 draft review-only, the unpublished notebook stopped with what to do, a new writer distinct from rt's, profiles (Current folder, Work store, Work active, Personal's remote waiting), the report's inventory, no em dashes; a second run leaves `rt-migration.json`, `profiles.json`, and the store census unchanged; an interrupted run (a notebook missing, then a crash between draft and record) finishes without doubling anything; damaged state (truncated `quire-notebook.json`, missing root, garbage `profiles.sqlite`, wrong data folder) reported with what to do and rt unchanged; both backups restored as new notebooks with drafts for review and a new identity, a tampered backup refused with nothing created, backups unchanged; rollback round trip: after one folder edit and one Quire notebook edit, the check without `--publish` names the unpublished save and changes only the edited note, `--publish` adds exactly one change file under Quire's writer and a fresh replica agrees, a second publish adds nothing; the committed vector holds only those two additions. App (`rt_migration::tests`, 3): tabs, favorites, recents, active page, and expanded folders converted for both notebooks (rt's AI browser dropped), prompt history merged after Quire's own and other AI state kept, the second run changing neither file, rt unchanged; conversion drops renamed pages and unknown destinations; command-line errors. Node: `tests/reference/rt-state.test.ts` (6) opens `tests/fixtures/rt-state-rollback/` with rt's own frozen engine and vault code: the folder note Quire edited with rt's draft still offered, and the Quire notebook page Quire published, alongside rt's settled draft. Regenerate the vector with `QUIRE_WRITE_RT_ROLLBACK_VECTOR=1 cargo test -p quire_store --lib rt_migration::tests::after_editing`.

Batch D (2026-10-31, `90dc49207268`). File > Move from rt… and Settings > Data open a Move from rt window: it finds or asks for rt's data folder, runs the migration and shows its report, checks what rt will see, publishes Quire's saves for rt behind a confirmation through `prepare_rollback`, and lists and restores rt backups. It refuses to start while any notebook window holds a notebook (`data_tests::migrating_from_rt_waits_until_no_notebook_window_holds_a_notebook`). rt's `ai-settings.json` is now imported once into an untouched AI setup, still off until Test, and keeps AI choices made in Quire (`rt_migration::tests::the_window_migrates_imports_ai_settings_checks_the_return_and_restores_backups`, `migrating_keeps_ai_settings_chosen_in_quire`, `rt_ai_choices_keep_only_values_quire_accepts`). Restored notebooks join the original's profile (NR26).

Batch M (2026-10-31, `3d6e61c88046`). The converted workspace now carries rt's `filesOpen: false` (Files folded) and Daily notes month (`browsers.daily.month`, `YYYY-MM` only) into Quire's `chrome` choices, and rt's `ai` tab as the Ask the notebook tab; the fixture's assertion that the AI browser is dropped is replaced by these (see NR16 batch M).

Remaining: `--rollback-to-rt --publish` remains for saves made while the synced folder was unavailable (profiles written by an earlier migration run lack the folder and need `--synced-folder`); rt's interrupted journals and pre-`.quire` app-data recovery need one rt open first; packaged-app acceptance on macOS, Windows, and Linux (NR35); a dry run against a real rt installation with the user's consent (only synthetic state was used).
Status: in_progress

### NR37: Review complete parity and approve cutover readiness

What to do: Review every parity row and task proof against the built package and final source contract. Resolve gaps, update README/CONTEXT/help, and record a release-readiness decision.
Constraints: A missing provider/OS/native-input result is unverified, not passed. Readiness is separate from publishing or deleting old data.
Write set: parity ledger, proof reports, product documentation, concrete gap fixes.
Dependencies: NR01 through NR36.
Verify: Complete protocol, semantic, durability, native UI, performance, package, and migration gates; fresh review of code/diff/proof; prose lint on final documentation. No required parity row has only a screenshot or source-test citation as native proof.
Proof notes: review of 2026-11-01 at `7ffaf03b9781` against `rt` `5ea5c22310ff`. **Verdict: not ready.** Gate C stays closed and the build stays experimental: 37 of 68 parity rows are not Present, the performance gate fails 9 of its budget groups, and no row has a native desktop, Linux, or Windows result, which the cutover rule in the [architecture plan](native-rewrite.md) requires. Running Quire beside `rt` on a copy of a notebook, as that plan allows before cutover, remains the right next use.

Method. Five read-only reviewers worked in parallel. Three took the ledger rows (S01 to S24, S25 to S47, E01 to E18), opened each cited test and the code behind it, and confirmed the assertions match the row rather than matching names. Two made a fresh pass over `rt`'s user-visible surface (README, `help-content.ts`, `App.tsx`, `Editor.tsx` keymaps and decorations, `vim-bindings.ts`, `main.ts` menus and IPC, `preload.ts`, the Settings panes, and every `src/ui` component) and searched quire for each label, key, and action. This session then reopened the code behind every new gap and bug before recording it. The per-row states, evidence, and open items are in the [ledger's readiness review](native-parity.md).

Counts across 68 rows: 31 Present, 25 Partial, 7 Blocked, 2 Intentionally different (S29, S45), 3 Missing (E19 spellcheck in progress, E20, E21). Present means native tests prove the semantics on the headless GPUI platform on macOS; it does not mean the platform runs passed.

Blocking list for Gate C. Each item names its owner.

Engineering:
- B1. Performance (E15, S01, S04, S24, S28). The NR34 final run fails 9 budget groups: warm startup ratio 1.47 against 1.25; first open editable at 1x 2,049 ms against 1,500; first open index 9.5 s and 49.6 s against 2.8 s and 15 s; typing work p95 16.6 ms (1x) against 4; search p95 125 ms (1x) and 480 ms (5x) against 100; tasks at 5x 259 ms against 50; tags at 5x 146 ms against 50; large-page typing 13.1 ms against 8; memory 327 and 870 MiB against 250, growth 542 against 50. The concurrent sync, index, and backup workload is unmeasured.
- B2. Spellcheck (E19), in progress in another worktree.
- B3. Pasted block references (S34). Quire leaves references inside pasted text pointing at the originals and has no cut marker, so a cut and paste can give a moved block a new ID and break references to it. This is a data integrity gap, not a cosmetic one. Fixed in the Q batch: see the proof note below.
- B4. Done after the review: a folder notebook's New folder creates the directory, and FILES has New page and New folder buttons (S17, E17).
- B5. Embeds: Vim `o` on an embed's last line, and no scroll into a tall embed (S36). Fixed in the Q batch.
- B6. Done after the review: Help names only shipped controls, and the README sample's references, embed, and tags resolve in a notebook of the sample's own (E12).
- B7. Small unverified defects to test and fix: Vim Insert keys in the Delete dialog (E03); concurrent creation of one title in a Quire notebook (S14); list styles dropped in embeds and zooms (E17). All three fixed in the Q batch.
- B8. Windows and Linux engineering before their acceptance runs: single instance (S13), MCP transport (S44), file watchers (E18), font fallback (E04), packaging (E16). Engineering done, compiled only, in the B8 batch; the acceptance runs stay with P2 and P3. Single instance and the notebook bridge use Zed's `net` sockets (AF_UNIX on Windows, as Zed's MCP and askpass sockets) with one code path and unchanged protocols, and the notebook tools now build on every OS. Folder watchers go through `folder_watch`, which registers every directory on Linux's per-directory inotify as Zed's worktree does. Windows spellcheck uses `ISpellChecker` (E19); Linux stays off because Enchant or Hunspell would be a new native dependency. Arabic fallback: verified for Linux by running GPUI's cosmic-text system in a macOS test; Windows needed a vendored DirectWrite fix so fallbacks can name fonts the app added. Packaging: `windows_subsystem`, the embedded icon (`build.rs`, `resources/quire.rc`), and the `app.quire.native` app ID with `StartupWMClass`. Proof: `cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc -p quire --bins --examples` and `-p gpui_windows -p quire_store -p quire_core --lib`, and `cargo clippy --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu -p quire -p quire_store -p quire_core --all-targets`, from macOS with zig as the C compiler and `zig rc` as the resource compiler. Linux was clean. Windows was clean except one existing lint outside B8, `clippy::permissions_set_readonly_false` at `file_actions.rs:623` (Windows clears read-only so it can replace the revealed copy); the owner of that file should decide on a fix rather than suppressing the lint. Windows test targets do not compile, because `stored/sync_tests.rs`, `live_tests.rs`, and `block_move_tests.rs` use `std::os::unix` without a cfg gate. Remaining: P2 and P3 runs of each item, Windows stale-socket recovery, and the Windows test targets.
- B9. Done after the review, macOS headless only: About, rt's Window menu, rt's window size and minimum, and startup failures in a dialog with Try Again and Open Another Folder (E21). The native dialogs and menus join P1 to P3.

Owner decisions:
- O1. Heading sizes (E01) and the Urdu 2.5 line height (E04): fund variable row heights in the vendored Zed display map, or accept bold headings as an intentional difference. Zed's display map gives every row one height today.
- O2. SQL and Clojure highlighting (E06): about 2.4 MB per build for SQL; Clojure's only buildable grammar is CC0.
- O3. AVIF (E05): add a dav1d or rav1d dependency, or leave AVIF as a link.
- O4. Real AI CLI tests (E09, E10, S42, S43, S44): consent to run the three ignored tests against the installed Claude Code and Codex, one of which edits `~/.claude` and `~/.codex`.
- O5. Signing and notarization (E16): Developer ID credentials.
- O6. Backup pruning (S38): NR26 already decided never to prune and to skip unchanged hourly backups, matching `rt`; the owner confirms the disk growth is acceptable. This is a two-way door.
- O7. First launch (E20): build `rt`'s example notebook, or keep the demo window as a recorded difference.
- O8. Real data checks: a dry run of `--migrate-from-rt` on the owner's real `rt` installation (S10) and a private-copy Roam import of the owner's export (S40).

Platform runs:
- P1. macOS desktop acceptance on a painting, unlocked screen: native input and IME, dialogs and pickers, menus, close and Cmd-Q, the title bar, Trash, Reveal, screenshots at several sizes and scales, and a fresh `bun run bench`.
- P2. Linux smoke and P3. Windows acceptance: every row's input, dialogs, PDFium, `rquickjs-sys`, watchers, and packaging.
- P4. Real providers: HTTPS and SSH Git remotes with credential helpers, and two-device OneDrive, Dropbox, iCloud Drive, and Google Drive handover (S39, S09); live sync beside the real `rt` desktop and Go apps (E14, E18).

Newly found gaps, now in the ledger: E19 spellcheck, E20 first-launch example notebook, and E21 application chrome (new rows); pasted references and cut moves (S34); New folder on disk and FILES buttons (S17); Add attachment entry (S21); duplicate daily pages notice (S32); `adoptLegacyBackups` (S10); non-media file embeds (E05); Vim cursor shapes (E11); README sample links and Help overclaims (E12); Alt-Backspace and Shift-End or Mod-Right at hidden IDs untested (E02); the inline reference copy button (S35, low).

Corrected in the ledger: S06 (Quire notebooks merge stale saves), S15 (case-only renames are refused, as in `rt`), S31 (unknown braces copy as written), S45 (transcripts live in the state folder), E06 (12 highlighted and seven formatted languages), E17 (Rename is a dialog), and two stale recorded gaps (Open as tab exists; folder moves carry attachments). Two items the brief listed as open are done: the per-card task state select (S28) and the answer pane's Open as tab.

Stale task notes found but not edited here, because their owning tasks should close them: NR18 Remaining (capture into today's page is done), NR19 and NR17 Deviations (capture draft, filters, and rich rows are now saved or rendered; references reveal their block), NR16.2 (New folder here and Ask AI exist), NR09.4 Remaining (the overlap warning is shown), NR12.4 (search reads unsaved text), NR11.4 Remaining 2 and 4, NR21 Remaining (restore checks attachments in NR26), and NR01 line 34 (`sync-vectors` is copied). NR34 and NR35 write their status as "in progress" rather than `in_progress`.

App shell batch after the review (B4, B6, B9, and ledger gaps S10, S21, S32, E05, E11). A folder notebook's New folder creates the directory through the folder worker (`FolderNotebook::create_folder`), refusing taken names in any letter case and linked parents, and FILES gained New page and New folder; the heading's buttons left the tree's `QuireList` context, where Enter had opened the highlighted file instead of pressing the focused button. Help no longer names a Page actions menu or browser Back/Forward keys; the page toolbar gained Attach… (S21) beside History…. The README sample resolves in a notebook of its own: an empty folder in the temporary folder, a folder index, and the sample's buffer as an in-memory `OpenPage` the index tracks, so `((readme-ai))` jumps within the sample, the embed edits its block, and a tag opens a sample tag page; a test checks the notebook's search never finds the sample. About Quire, rt's Window menu, rt's 1440 by 960 window with an 850 by 580 minimum, and startup failures in a native alert with Try Again and Open Another Folder close B9 on the headless platform. rt backups under `Threadnote` are listed without renaming rt's folder; dates with several daily pages get rt's notice; wiki embeds of other files get rt's open-file button; Vim cursors take rt's shapes, with color and blink recorded as intentional differences. Deviations: Add attachment is a toolbar button, not a page menu item, because quire's toolbar holds rt's other page actions; the startup failure is a native alert rather than rt's in-window screen, so it shows before the theme loads; quire lists `Threadnote` backups instead of renaming the folder, because only rt should change rt's data. Remaining for these rows: the platform runs (native dialogs, the macOS window list, the file picker, the system application for file embeds), and an asynchronous Quire-folder open chosen from the startup dialog that fails leaves the blank dialog window, which the person closes to quit. Gates for the batch: `cargo fmt --check` and clippy on all crates clean; `cargo test` passed 1,246 of 1,246 twice; the reference vitest suite passed 136 of 136; `verify-upstream` verified 2,992 copied files with 46 documented fork modifications.

Gates for this review (documentation only, no Rust changed, so `cargo test` and clippy were not rerun): `cargo fmt --check` passed; the reference vitest suite passed 136 of 136 tests in 10 files; `bun tools/verify-upstream.ts /Users/umer/code/zed` verified 2,992 copied files with 46 documented fork modifications; prose lint on the edited plans.

Open questions for the owner: the decisions O1 to O8; whether Gate C may open for macOS alone once B1 to B7, B9, and P1 pass, with Linux and Windows following as a separate gate, since the architecture plan today requires Windows acceptance and Linux smoke results before any cutover.
Q batch (2026-11-01), editor correctness blockers B3, B5, and B7, from `61b998b36c18`. B3: `outline/clipboard.rs` records what a page editor's Copy or Cut put on the clipboard (notebook, page path, text, cut or copy) and handles `editor::Paste` and the outline paste in Write. A paste whose text carries block IDs becomes a copy: `quire_core::markdown::{fresh_ids, renamed_ids}` (split out of `copy_with_new_ids`) give every ID a fresh one and point `((id))` and `Source#^id` references inside the text at the copies, renaming each of Zed's clipboard selections separately so their lengths stay right. Text this notebook cut pastes unchanged while no open page's live buffer has its IDs, so the block moves and references elsewhere still find it; a second paste of the same cut is a copy. `outline/fresh_ids.rs` now checks open pages' live text before asking the index, and keeps an ID the index places on an open page that no longer has it, so a Vim delete and put across pages keeps its ID. Tests: six `stored/fresh_ids_tests.rs` cases (copy on one page with undo, cut on one page then a second paste, cut to another page through the index, cut across panes with undo on both, cut whose source still has the block, Vim `dd`/`p` across panes), `renaming_leaves_unmapped_ids_and_their_references_alone`, `a_page_holds_only_its_block_ids`; five of the six failed with the change disabled (the cross-page case passed because the source page had left the session). `outline::tests::copied_block_ids_become_fresh_ids` now expects a pasted ID no page has to become fresh, as in `rt`. B5: Vim `o` and Enter already stayed in the embed; `embeds.rs::reveal` scrolls the outermost view to the caret on each local selection change in an embed, failing first with the host still at row 0. B7: `insert_anywhere.rs` treats `QuireDialog`, `QuireHistory`, and `QuirePreferences` as fields (`vim_insert_keys_in_a_dialog_stay_in_the_dialog`, failed first); `create_page_with` saves the path-derived page through `save_many_if_heads` with an empty base and refuses a page already live at the path (`simultaneous_creates_of_one_title_make_one_page`, failed first with both creates returning the same page); the page menu offers list styles in zooms and embeds, and embeds install the page menu (`list_styles_stay_in_the_menu_of_zooms_and_embeds`, failed first). Deviations from `rt`: Source view pastes as written, as `rt`'s source mode does; Vim `p` freshens only colliding IDs and rewrites no references; a cut pasted into another notebook window is a copy. Gates: `bash tools/gates.sh` with `ZED_CHECKOUT=/Users/umer/code/zed`.
S2 batch (2026-11-01), storage and app leftovers E20, S14, S18, and E14, from `9f6976c4ec0f`. E20 (decision O7 taken: follow `rt`): with no profile notebook, a launch writes `rt`'s three sample pages, byte-identical to `src/core/sample.ts` at `5ea5c22310ff`, into the folder notebook `Example notebook` in Quire's data folder (`QuireNative`), creating only missing files with `create_new` so an edited page is never replaced and no other file is touched, then opens it on A place to think; opening it makes it the active profile's notebook, as `rt`'s `open(root)` does (`editor_host/stored/example.rs`; tests in `example_tests.rs`). The editor harness flags keep the demo window; the README sample is unchanged. S14: `NotebookStore::create_if_free` saves a new page only while no live page holds its path (`page_at`: any letter case or Unicode normalization) and the page has no version, inside the save's `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction; `create_page_with` sends every create through it (a folder notebook keeps its exclusive file create). `simultaneous_creates_beside_a_renamed_page_make_one_page` failed first with both random-identity creates saved; `creating_a_page_refuses_a_path_any_live_page_holds_from_any_connection` covers two connections; `opening_today_twice_at_once_makes_one_daily_page` passed without a change. S18: four block-move tests (folder notebook move, stale by typing, stale by another writer, a destination note in a read-only folder); all passed without a change. Two gate runs caught the folder test reading `Refs.md` while the move was still writing: a folder notebook writes the move's notes one after another, so the test now waits for the destination to open, which follows the whole write, and also waits for the index to list `Refs.md` among the source's referrers, since a move retargets only the references the index knows, as in `rt`. E14: `go version` reports go1.26.8 and `rt`'s `tui/cmd/quire` builds offline (`GOFLAGS=-mod=readonly GOPROXY=off`), so `bun run test:sync-schedules --runtime go` runs the Go app as a harness computer (`tests/sync/go-peer.ts`, after `rt`'s `go-peer.ts`), and `tests/sync/editors.ts` runs two editors on one page at once. `--count 30 --runtime go` passed 30 schedules and 17 focused cases (16,028 native requests, 341 s); `--count 3 --runtime both` passed 6 schedules and 15 focused cases. Deviations: the editor case drives the harness editor model over the native store, not the GPUI editor; the Go hand-over leaves out `rt`'s lone surrogate because native pages hold UTF-8. Gates: `bash tools/gates.sh` with `ZED_CHECKOUT=/Users/umer/code/zed` printed ALL GATES PASSED: `cargo test` passed 1,280 of 1,280 twice, the reference vitest suite 136 of 136, and `verify-upstream` verified 2,992 copied files with 46 documented fork modifications. An earlier run under a load average near 25 failed `references_tests::linked_references_filter_by_typed_terms_and_chips` and `rendered_links_tests::write_renders_page_links_and_live_block_previews_that_open_on_click` once each; both passed on the rerun and lie outside this batch, so they are recorded as load-sensitive tests to watch.
S1 batch (2026-11-01), editor parity leftovers E01, E02, E04, E08, and E19, from `9f6976c4ec0f`. E02: Quire bound none of the platform's text keys that rt gets from CodeMirror's standard keymap, so Shift-arrows, Shift-End, Cmd-Right, Alt-Left, Alt-Backspace, and the rest did nothing with Vim off; `editor_host.rs` now loads them as a bundled keymap (`TEXT_KEYS_MACOS`, `TEXT_KEYS_OTHER`) through the same path as the Vim keymap. In Write, Alt-Backspace at a block's text start merges into the block above (`MergeBackward`, as `Editor.tsx:2048-2052`), Shift-End and Cmd-Shift-Right end before a hidden ID or collapsed children (`editor_projection::select_to_text_end`, as `toTextEnd`), and Cmd-Backspace at a list item's text start keeps the hidden marker. The fold store keeps each Write view of a page and, once one saves a change, brings the others to the saved folds after the current update (`folds::sync`), so a zoom's or embed's fold reaches the page view at once. rt's bullet buttons have no keyboard path (Tab indents), so none is owed. E01: table cells draw their inline Markdown through `rich_text::inline`, the result-row reader, and a press on a cell link opens it through the page's notebook; a test pins that front matter fences never draw as rules. E04: Markdown views outside Write set Urdu runs in Nastaliq on open, after each edit, and on leaving Write (`write_style::source_urdu`). E19: embedded block editors attach the spell checker to the embedded page's analysis. E08: the eight task vectors that diverged had two causes in the block scanner. A fence line ending at EOF was an ERROR node, and a fence closed at backticks after text on a line, since tree-sitter calls the scanner between inline tokens and the scanner never checked for a line start. The scanner now gives a fence line at EOF a zero-width line ending and records the column where each line's content starts (one serialized byte) to compare with `get_column` before closing; UPSTREAM.json and the vendor README record it, six page-index vectors and a grammar test cover it, and the analyzer version is 7 so indexes rebuild. All 224 task vectors now match the reference blocks with no exclusion list. Each fix has a test that failed before it; the front matter test pins behavior that was already right.
Remaining: every item in the blocking list above except B3, B5, and B7, which the Q batch closed, and O7, which the S2 batch settled. E14 now needs only the live runs in P4.
